####################################################################################################
FILE: config\settings.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Configuration management for the Compliance Chatbot.

Loads all application settings from environment variables or a .env file
using Pydantic BaseSettings, ensuring no secrets are hard-coded.

Environment variable mapping (env_nested_delimiter="__"):
    LOG_LEVEL               → settings.log_level
    HOST                    → settings.host
    PORT                    → settings.port
    DEBUG                   → settings.debug
    DATABASE__DB_FOLDER     → settings.database.db_folder
    DATABASE__DB_NAME       → settings.database.db_name
    MODEL__PROVIDER         → settings.model.provider
    MODEL__MODEL_NAME       → settings.model.model_name
    MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL     → settings.model.dlh_base_url
    MODEL__DLH_API_KEY      → settings.model.dlh_api_key
    MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL     → settings.model.ica_base_url
    MODEL__ICA_API_KEY      → settings.model.ica_api_key
    MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE    → settings.model.ica_namespace
"""

from pathlib import Path

from pydantic import Field
from pydantic_settings import BaseSettings, SettingsConfigDict

# Nested settings classes for database and model configuration.

class DatabaseSettings(BaseSettings):
    """Database connection configuration."""

    db_type: str = Field(default="SQLite", description="Database engine type.")
    db_folder: str = Field(default="data/comp_db", description="Relative path to the DB folder.")
    db_name: str = Field(default="COMP_REPORTS.db", description="SQLite database filename.")

    @property
    def db_path(self) -> Path:
        """Resolve absolute path to the SQLite database file."""
        return Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / self.db_folder / self.db_name

class ModelSettings(BaseSettings):
    """LLM configuration for the Compliance Chatbot."""

    provider: str = Field(default="dlh", description="LLM provider: 'dlh' or 'ica'.")
    model_name: str = Field(
        default="google/gemma-4-31b-it",
        description="Remote model name for OpenAI-compatible providers.",
    )
    model_max_new_tokens: int = Field(default=512, description="Maximum tokens to generate per inference.")
    model_temperature: float = Field(default=0.1, description="Sampling temperature for generation.")
    dlh_base_url: str = Field(
        default="http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1",
        description="DLH inference server base URL.",
    )
    dlh_api_key: str = Field(default="EMPTY", description="Bearer token for the DLH endpoint.")
    dlh_verify_ssl: bool = Field(
        default=False,
        description="Whether to verify TLS certificates for the DLH endpoint.",
    )
    ica_base_url: str = Field(
        default="https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com",
        description="ICA API host URL.",
    )
    ica_api_key: str = Field(default="", description="Bearer developer key for ICA APIs.")
    ica_namespace: str = Field(
        default="chat-models",
        description="ICA namespace: agents, assistants, chat-models, or digital-workforce.",
    )
    ica_send_temperature: bool = Field(
        default=False,
        description="Whether to forward temperature/max_tokens to ICA.",
    )

# Root AppSettings class — reads .env and exposes database + model sub-settings.

class AppSettings(BaseSettings):
    """Top-level application configuration."""

    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(
        env_file=".env",
        env_file_encoding="utf-8",
        env_nested_delimiter="__",
        extra="ignore",
        populate_by_name=True,
    )

    application_name: str = Field(
        default="COMPLIANCE-BOT (UI + LLM Hybrid)",
        description="Human-readable application name.",
    )
    page_config: str = Field(default="SBI COMPLIANCE-BOT", description="UI page title.")
    log_level: str = Field(default="INFO", description="Root logging level.")
    log_dir: str = Field(default="logs", description="Directory for rotating log files.")
    host: str = Field(default="0.0.0.0", description="FastAPI bind host.")
    port: int = Field(default=8000, description="FastAPI bind port.")
    debug: bool = Field(default=False, description="Enable debug / hot-reload mode.")

    database: DatabaseSettings = Field(default_factory=DatabaseSettings)
    model: ModelSettings = Field(default_factory=ModelSettings)

# Module-level singleton — import via ``get_settings()`` or ``from config.settings import settings``.

settings: AppSettings = AppSettings()

def get_settings() -> AppSettings:
    """Return the cached application settings singleton."""
    return settings



####################################################################################################
FILE: data\seed_synthetic_data.py

"""
Synthetic data generator for COMP_TBL.

Creates the database file, creates the table if absent, and inserts
realistic term-deposit compliance records into
``data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db``.  Run from the project root:

    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py              # create + insert 2000 rows
    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset      # drop all rows then insert
    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 500  # custom row count
    python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 0    # create DB/table only, no rows

The script uses only the Python standard library — no external packages needed.

Modular structure
-----------------
  ensure_database(db_path)          → creates the .db file and parent directories
  ensure_table(conn)                → creates COMP_TBL if it does not exist
  reset_table(conn)                 → deletes all existing rows
  generate_rows(count, start_i)     → returns a list of synthetic row tuples
  insert_rows(conn, rows)           → bulk-inserts rows
  print_summary(conn, db_path, n)   → prints statistics after insert
  seed(db_path, count, reset)       → orchestrates all of the above
"""

import argparse
import random
import sqlite3
from datetime import date, timedelta
from pathlib import Path

# DDL and INSERT SQL kept here so schema is defined exactly once.

_CREATE_TABLE_SQL = """
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS COMP_TBL (
    BRCD               TEXT,
    CUST_NBR           TEXT,
    CUST_NBR_CHKDGT    TEXT,
    ACCT_NBR           TEXT,
    ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT    TEXT,
    ACCT_BRNCH         TEXT,
    BRTH_DT            TEXT,
    AGE_YRS            INTEGER,
    SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG   INTEGER,
    KYC_FLG            TEXT,
    END_OF_DAY_BAL     REAL,
    ACCT_OPN_DT        TEXT,
    MTRTY_DT           TEXT,
    tenor_yr           INTEGER,
    tenor_day          INTEGER,
    MAT_DT             TEXT,
    NEW_EFFCTV_RT      REAL,
    PRDCT_ID           TEXT,
    PF_FAC_REPT        TEXT,
    PRODUCT_NAME       TEXT,
    BRANCHNAME         TEXT,
    CIRCLENAME         TEXT,
    CIRCLECD           TEXT,
    EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT REAL,
    ISMOD              TEXT
)
"""

_INSERT_SQL = """
INSERT INTO COMP_TBL (
    BRCD, CUST_NBR, CUST_NBR_CHKDGT, ACCT_NBR, ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT, ACCT_BRNCH,
    BRTH_DT, AGE_YRS, SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG, KYC_FLG, END_OF_DAY_BAL, ACCT_OPN_DT,

    PRODUCT_NAME, BRANCHNAME, CIRCLENAME, CIRCLECD, EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT, ISMOD
) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
"""

}

_PRODUCTS: list[tuple[str, str]] = [
    ("P100", "PUB IND UNI 181D-10YRS"),
    ("P101", "TDTDR PUB IND UNI-91-179D-INR"),
    ("P102", "SENIOR TERM DEPOSIT"),
    ("P103", "PUB IND REINVESTMENT 1-2YRS"),
    ("P104", "TDTDR REINVESTMENT 2-5YRS"),
    ("P105", "RECURRING DEPOSIT SCHEME"),
    ("P106", "FLEXI DEPOSIT PLAN"),
    ("P107", "TAX SAVER FIXED DEPOSIT"),
    ("P108", "SUPER SENIOR DEPOSIT"),
    ("P109", "SHORT TERM DEPOSIT 7-30D"),
    ("P110", "MEDIUM TERM DEPOSIT 180D-1YR"),
]

# Step 1: create the .db file and its parent directory if they do not exist.

def ensure_database(db_path: Path) -> None:
    """
    Create the parent directory and SQLite database file if they do not exist.

    This is a no-op when both already exist — SQLite creates the file on
    first ``connect()`` so the function just guarantees the directory is there.

    Args:
        db_path: Absolute or relative path to the ``.db`` file.
    """
    db_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    # SQLite creates the file on connect; touching it here just makes the
    # "created" message accurate.
    existed = db_path.exists()
    # open and close immediately to create the file
    sqlite3.connect(str(db_path)).close()
    if not existed:
        print(f"[create] Database file created: {db_path}")
    else:
        print(f"[ok]     Database file exists:  {db_path}")

# Step 2: create COMP_TBL with the correct 25-column schema if it is missing or stale.

def ensure_table(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    """
    Create ``COMP_TBL`` with the full 25-column schema if it does not exist.

    If the table already exists but is missing required columns (stale schema),
    it is dropped and recreated so the seeder can always run safely.

    Args:
        conn: Open SQLite connection to the target database.
    """
    table_exists = conn.execute(
        "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' AND name='COMP_TBL'"
    ).fetchone()

    if table_exists:
        existing_cols = {
            row[1] for row in conn.execute("PRAGMA table_info(COMP_TBL)").fetchall()
        }
        if not _REQUIRED_COLUMNS.issubset(existing_cols):
            conn.execute("DROP TABLE COMP_TBL")
            conn.commit()
            print("[migrate] COMP_TBL schema was stale — dropped and will be recreated.")
            table_exists = None  # fall through to CREATE

    if not table_exists:
        conn.execute(_CREATE_TABLE_SQL)
        conn.commit()
        print("[create] COMP_TBL created.")
    else:
        print("[ok]     COMP_TBL already exists with correct schema.")

# Step 3 (optional): wipe all existing rows when --reset is passed.

def reset_table(conn: sqlite3.Connection) -> None:
    """
    Delete every row from ``COMP_TBL``.

    Args:
        conn: Open SQLite connection to the target database.
    """
    deleted = conn.execute("DELETE FROM COMP_TBL").rowcount
    conn.commit()
    print(f"[reset]  Deleted {deleted:,} existing rows from COMP_TBL.")

# Step 4: pure row-factory helpers — no database access, only random value generation.

def _rand_date(start: date, end: date) -> date:
    """Return a random date between *start* and *end* (inclusive)."""
    return start + timedelta(days=random.randint(0, (end - start).days))

def _fmt(d: date) -> str:
    return d.strftime("%Y-%m-%d")

def _rand_rate(base: float) -> float:
    """Return a rate within ±0.75 pp of *base*, rounded to 2 dp."""
    return round(base + random.uniform(-0.75, 0.75), 2)

def _make_row(i: int) -> tuple:
    """Generate one synthetic COMP_TBL row."""
    circle_name, circle_cd = random.choice(_CIRCLES)
    branch_name            = random.choice(_BRANCHES[circle_cd])
    prdct_id, product_name = random.choice(_PRODUCTS)

    age     = random.randint(18, 85)
    sr_flag = 1 if age >= 60 else 0
    dob     = _fmt(_rand_date(date(2025 - age - 1, 1, 1), date(2025 - age, 12, 31)))

    kyc     = "Y" if random.random() < (0.80 if sr_flag else 0.90) else "N"
    balance = round(random.lognormvariate(11.5, 1.2), 2)
    balance = max(10_000.0, min(5_000_000.0, balance))

    open_dt   = _rand_date(date(2005, 1, 1), date(2024, 6, 30))
    tenor_yr  = random.choice([1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 15])
    tenor_day = random.randint(0, 364)
    mat_dt_d  = open_dt + timedelta(days=tenor_yr * 365 + tenor_day)
    mat_dt    = _fmt(mat_dt_d)

    is_mod  = "YES" if random.random() < 0.15 else "NO"
    mtrty_dt = _fmt(mat_dt_d + timedelta(days=random.randint(-30, 30))) if is_mod == "YES" else mat_dt

    base_rate = 7.0 if sr_flag else 6.5
    new_rate  = _rand_rate(base_rate)
    exp_rate  = _rand_rate(base_rate + 0.25)
    pf_fac    = "Y" if random.random() < 0.30 else "N"
    brcd      = f"{random.randint(1, 999):03d}"
    acct_brnch = f"M{random.randint(1, 99):03d}"

    return (
        brcd,                        # BRCD
        f"C{i:06d}",                 # CUST_NBR
        str(random.randint(1, 9)),   # CUST_NBR_CHKDGT
        f"A{i:06d}",                 # ACCT_NBR
        str(random.randint(1, 9)),   # ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT
        acct_brnch,                  # ACCT_BRNCH
        dob,                         # BRTH_DT
        age,                         # AGE_YRS
        sr_flag,                     # SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG
        kyc,                         # KYC_FLG
        balance,                     # END_OF_DAY_BAL
        _fmt(open_dt),               # ACCT_OPN_DT
        mtrty_dt,                    # MTRTY_DT
        tenor_yr,                    # tenor_yr
        tenor_day,                   # tenor_day
        mat_dt,                      # MAT_DT
        new_rate,                    # NEW_EFFCTV_RT
        prdct_id,                    # PRDCT_ID
        pf_fac,                      # PF_FAC_REPT
        product_name,                # PRODUCT_NAME
        branch_name,                 # BRANCHNAME
        circle_name,                 # CIRCLENAME
        circle_cd,                   # CIRCLECD
        exp_rate,                    # EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT
        is_mod,                      # ISMOD
    )

def generate_rows(count: int, start_i: int) -> list[tuple]:
    """
    Generate *count* synthetic row tuples starting from index *start_i*.

    ``start_i`` is used for ``CUST_NBR`` / ``ACCT_NBR`` sequential IDs so
    appending rows never collides with existing ones.

    Args:
        count:   Number of rows to generate.
        start_i: Index of the first new row (1-based).

    Returns:
        List of row tuples ready for ``executemany``.
    """
    return [_make_row(start_i + i) for i in range(count)]

# Step 5: bulk-insert the generated rows in a single executemany call.

def insert_rows(conn: sqlite3.Connection, rows: list[tuple]) -> None:
    """
    Bulk-insert *rows* into ``COMP_TBL`` using a single ``executemany``.

    Args:
        conn: Open SQLite connection (must already have COMP_TBL).
        rows: Row tuples as returned by :func:`generate_rows`.
    """
    conn.executemany(_INSERT_SQL, rows)
    conn.commit()
    print(f"[insert] {len(rows):,} rows inserted into COMP_TBL.")

# Step 6: query the DB and print row counts and top circles after the insert.

def print_summary(conn: sqlite3.Connection, db_path: Path, inserted: int) -> None:
    """
    Print a statistics summary after the insert.

    Args:
        conn:     Open SQLite connection.
        db_path:  Path to the database file (for display only).
        inserted: Number of rows that were just inserted.
    """
    total   = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL").fetchone()[0]
    sr      = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL WHERE SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1").fetchone()[0]
    kyc_n   = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG = 'N'").fetchone()[0]
    circles = conn.execute(
        "SELECT CIRCLENAME, COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL GROUP BY CIRCLENAME ORDER BY 2 DESC LIMIT 5"
    ).fetchall()

    print(f"\n{'='*55}")
    print(f"  Database : {db_path}")
    print(f"  Inserted : {inserted:,} rows")
    print(f"  Total    : {total:,} rows in COMP_TBL")
    print(f"{'='*55}")
    print(f"  Senior citizens  (SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG=1) : {sr:,}")
    print(f"  KYC non-compliant (KYC_FLG='N')        : {kyc_n:,}")
    print(f"\n  Top 5 circles by row count:")
    for name, cnt in circles:
        print(f"    {name:<22} {cnt:>5}")
    print(f"{'='*55}\n")

# Orchestrator that runs all six steps in sequence.

def seed(db_path: Path, count: int, reset: bool) -> None:
    """
    Full end-to-end pipeline:

    1. :func:`ensure_database` — create ``.db`` file and parent dirs if absent.
    2. :func:`ensure_table`    — create ``COMP_TBL`` if absent or stale.
    3. :func:`reset_table`     — (only when ``reset=True``) delete all rows.
    4. :func:`generate_rows`   — build synthetic row tuples in memory.
    5. :func:`insert_rows`     — bulk-insert into the database.
    6. :func:`print_summary`   — display post-insert statistics.

    Args:
        db_path: Path to the SQLite ``.db`` file.
        count:   Number of rows to insert (0 = create DB/table only).
        reset:   When ``True``, delete all existing rows before inserting.
    """
    # Step 1 — database file
    ensure_database(db_path)

    conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path))

    try:
        # Step 2 — table schema
        ensure_table(conn)

        # Step 3 — optional reset
        if reset:
            reset_table(conn)

        if count == 0:
            print("[info]   --count 0: skipping row insert.")
            print_summary(conn, db_path, inserted=0)
            return

        # Step 4 — generate rows (start_i avoids CUST_NBR / ACCT_NBR collisions)
        start_i = conn.execute("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL").fetchone()[0] + 1
        rows = generate_rows(count, start_i)

        # Step 5 — insert
        insert_rows(conn, rows)

        # Step 6 — summary
        print_summary(conn, db_path, inserted=count)

    finally:
        conn.close()

# CLI argument parser and script entry-point.

def _parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description=(
            "Create the SQLite database + COMP_TBL schema and insert synthetic data.\n"
            "Safe to re-run: creates only what is missing."
        ),
        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--count", type=int, default=2000,
        help="Number of rows to insert (default: 2000). Use 0 to create DB/table only.",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--reset", action="store_true",
        help="Delete all existing rows before inserting.",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--db", type=Path,
        default=Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "comp_db" / "COMP_REPORTS.db",
        help="Path to the SQLite database file.",
    )
    p.add_argument(
        "--seed", type=int, default=42,
        help="Random seed for reproducibility (default: 42).",
    )
    return p.parse_args()

if __name__ == "__main__":
    args = _parse_args()
    random.seed(args.seed)
    seed(db_path=args.db, count=args.count, reset=args.reset)

# Adding a New Database

The application supports multiple SQLite databases simultaneously. Any `.db` file placed under `data/` is automatically discovered and scored against the user's question on every request by the **Schema Agent** (Node 2 in the LangGraph pipeline). No restart is required for discovery.

---

## How auto-discovery works

On every chat request the Schema Agent:

1. Scans `data/**/*.db` recursively using `pathlib.Path.glob`.
2. Opens each database and reads all table names and column names via `PRAGMA table_info`.
3. Scores each database by counting keyword overlaps between the user's question and the database stem, table names, and column names.
4. Selects the highest-scoring database (falls back to the configured default when all scores are zero).
5. Builds a live Markdown-formatted schema context that is injected into the SQL Writer prompt.

No static metadata cache file is used — the schema is introspected fresh on each request, so it is always in sync with the actual database file.

---

## Step-by-step: adding a new database

### Step 1 — Place the `.db` file

Put the SQLite file in a descriptive subfolder under `data/`:

```
data/
├── comp_db/
│   └── COMP_REPORTS.db       ← existing
└── loan_db/
    └── LOAN_REPORTS.db       ← new
```

The folder name and file name are used as routing keywords, so use descriptive names (e.g., `loan_db`, `kyc_db`, `customer_db`).

### Step 2 — (Optional) Add a schema reference file

Create a `db_details.txt` alongside the new database:

```
data/loan_db/db_details.txt
```

Include:
- Database purpose
- Table names and their business meaning
- Column names, types, and descriptions
- Common filter fields and example questions

This file is for human documentation only. The Schema Agent reads the live schema directly from the `.db` file.

### Step 3 — (Optional) Add schema metadata to `compliance_schema.py`

Add an entry in [`src/models/compliance_schema.py`](../src/models/compliance_schema.py) for any tables in the new database that need LLM-assisted SQL generation:

```python
"LOAN_TBL": {
    "TABLE_DESCRIPTION": "...",
    "COLUMNS": {
        "LOAN_ID": "Unique loan identifier",
        "BORROWER_NAME": "Name of the borrower",
        ...
    },
    "FEW_SHOT_EXAMPLES": [
        {"question": "Show all active loans", "sql": "SELECT * FROM LOAN_TBL WHERE STATUS = 'ACTIVE';"},
    ],
}
```

This enriches the prompt injected into the SQL Writer Agent with business-level column descriptions and few-shot examples that the live schema introspection cannot provide.

### Step 4 — Restart the application (optional)

```bash
python main.py
```

The Schema Agent discovers the new database automatically on the next query without a restart.

### Step 5 — Test routing

Ask a question that should route to the new database:

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "Show all active loans"}'
```

The `stages` field in the response will include `schema_agent:db=LOAN_REPORTS.db`, confirming which database was selected.

---

## How the Schema Agent scores databases

The selector scores each `.db` file found under `data/` by counting keyword matches between the question and:

| Source | Match weight | Example keywords |
|---|---|---|
| Database filename stem | +1 | `loan`, `comp`, `kyc`, `customer` |
| Table name (verbatim) | +2 | `loan_tbl`, `comp_tbl`, `kyc_records` |
| Column name (verbatim) | +1 | `loan_id`, `borrower_name`, `kyc_flag` |

The highest-scoring database is selected. If no database scores above zero, the selector falls back to the configured default (`DATABASE__DB_FOLDER` / `DATABASE__DB_NAME` in `.env`).

---

## Example routing rules

| Question keywords | Database selected |
|---|---|
| "senior citizen", "term deposit", "Mumbai circle" | `COMP_REPORTS` |
| "loan repayment", "EMI", "borrower" | `LOAN_REPORTS` |
| "KYC status", "AML", "sanctions" | `KYC_REPORTS` |
| "customer demographics", "age group" | `CUSTOMER_REPORTS` |

---

## Checklist for a new database

- [ ] `.db` file placed under `data/<folder>/`
- [ ] `db_details.txt` schema reference created alongside the `.db` file (optional but recommended)
- [ ] Schema metadata entry added in `src/models/compliance_schema.py` (if richer LLM context is needed)
- [ ] End-to-end routing tested with a sample question
- [ ] `stages` field in the response confirms `schema_agent:db=<DB_NAME>.db`

> See also [multi-database-routing.md](multi-database-routing.md) for the routing design.

# Compliance Bot v2 — API Reference

All endpoints are served by the FastAPI application in `src/main.py`, started via the root `main.py` entry-point. The base URL is `http://localhost:8000` by default.

Interactive documentation is available at:
- **Swagger UI** — `http://localhost:8000/docs`
- **ReDoc** — `http://localhost:8000/redoc`

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Authentication](#authentication)
2. [Endpoints](#endpoints)
   - [GET /](#get-)
   - [GET /health](#get-health)
   - [POST /api/v1/config/llm](#post-apiv1configllm)
   - [POST /api/v1/compliance/chat](#post-apiv1compliancechat)
   - [POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate](#post-apiv1complianceorchestrate)
3. [Request Schemas](#request-schemas)
4. [Response Schemas](#response-schemas)
5. [Error Responses](#error-responses)
6. [curl Examples](#curl-examples)

---

## Authentication

The API does not require authentication by default. Provider credentials (DLH API key, ICA API key) are supplied in the request body to `POST /api/v1/config/llm` and stored in the running process.

---

## Endpoints

---

### GET /

**Description:** Serves the HTML UI (`index.html`).

**Response:** `text/html` — the full single-page application.

**curl:**
```bash
curl http://localhost:8000/
```

---

### GET /health

**Description:** Liveness probe. Returns the application status and version.

**Tags:** `Infrastructure`

**Response 200:**
```json
{
  "status": "ok",
  "version": "2.0.0"
}
```

**curl:**
```bash
curl http://localhost:8000/health
```

---

### POST /api/v1/config/llm

**Description:** Configure and test an LLM provider connection. On success, replaces the active provider **and rebuilds the LangGraph pipeline** for all subsequent chat requests in the current process.

**Tags:** `Configuration`

**Request body:** `application/json` → [`LLMConnectionRequest`](#llmconnectionrequest)

**Response 200:** [`LLMConnectionResponse`](#llmconnectionresponse)

**Response 500:** [`ErrorResponse`](#errorresponse) — provider connection or model loading failed.

**curl — DLH:**
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "provider": "dlh",
    "model_name": "google/gemma-4-31b-it",
    "dlh_base_url": "http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1",
    "dlh_api_key": "EMPTY",
    "dlh_verify_ssl": false
  }'
```

**curl — ICA:**
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "provider": "ica",
    "model_name": "your-resource-id",
    "ica_base_url": "https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com",
    "ica_api_key": "your-api-key",
    "ica_namespace": "chat-models",
    "ica_send_temperature": false
  }'
```

**Success response:**
```json
{
  "success": true,
  "provider": "dlh",
  "message": "LLM connection successful. LangGraph pipeline is ready."
}
```

---

### POST /api/v1/compliance/chat

**Description:** Simplified NL2SQL endpoint. Runs the full LangGraph pipeline but returns only the essential fields (`sql_query`, `df_output`, `error`). Use `/orchestrate` for the full stage trace.

**Tags:** `Compliance`

**Request body:** `application/json` → [`ComplianceChatRequest`](#compliancechatrequest)

**Response 200:** [`ComplianceChatResponse`](#compliancechatresponse)

**Response 503:** Service unavailable — compliance processor not yet initialised.

**curl:**
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/chat \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "Show all senior citizen accounts."}'
```

**Success response:**
```json
{
  "question_id": "3e8a1b2c-...",
  "sql_query": "SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1;",
  "df_output": [
    {"BRCD": "001", "CUST_NBR": "C001", "AGE_YRS": 64, "SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG": 1}
  ],
  "error": null
}
```

---

### POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate

**Description:** Full LangGraph orchestration endpoint. Returns the complete pipeline stage trace, interpreted question, SQL, results, result summary, and any clarification request from the Intent Agent.

**Tags:** `Compliance`

**Request body:** `application/json` → [`ComplianceChatRequest`](#compliancechatrequest)

**Response 200:** [`ComplianceOrchestrationResponse`](#complianceorchestrationresponse)

**Response 503:** Service unavailable — compliance processor not yet initialised.

**curl:**
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "How many persons from the Mumbai circle?"}'
```

**Success response:**
```json
{
  "question_id": "7f3c9d1a-...",
  "interpreted_question": "How many persons from the Mumbai circle?",
  "needs_clarification": false,
  "clarification_question": null,
  "sql_query": "SELECT COUNT(*) AS record_count FROM COMP_TBL WHERE UPPER(CIRCLENAME) LIKE '%MUMBAI%';",
  "df_output": [{"record_count": 1}],
  "result_summary": "Result: **record_count** = `1`",
  "stages": [
    "intent_agent:ok",
    "schema_agent:db=COMP_REPORTS.db",
    "sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry=0",
    "sql_review_agent:passed",
    "executor_agent:ok rows=1"
  ],
  "error": null
}
```

**Clarification response** (when question is ambiguous):
```json
{
  "question_id": "2a4b6c8d-...",
  "interpreted_question": "show me this?",
  "needs_clarification": true,
  "clarification_question": "Please provide more detail about what you are looking for. For example: KYC status, maturity date, product name, branch, balance, senior-citizen flag, or interest rate.",
  "sql_query": null,
  "df_output": null,
  "result_summary": null,
  "stages": ["intent_agent:clarification_required", "clarify_node:terminal"],
  "error": null
}
```

---

## Request Schemas

### ComplianceChatRequest

Used by both `/api/v1/compliance/chat` and `/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate`.

| Field | Type | Required | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `question` | `string \| null` | No* | Natural-language compliance question. Cannot be a blank string if provided. |
| `question_id` | `string \| null` | No | Optional caller-supplied idempotency key. Auto-assigned UUID if omitted. |

\* A request with no question returns a graceful error response (not an HTTP error).

**Example:**
```json
{
  "question": "Show customers whose KYC flag is N and balance is above 100000.",
  "question_id": "req-001"
}
```

---

### LLMConnectionRequest

Used by `/api/v1/config/llm`.

| Field | Type | Required | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `provider` | `string` | **Yes** | — | `"dlh"` or `"ica"` |
| `model_name` | `string \| null` | No | From settings | Model name or ICA resource ID |
| `dlh_base_url` | `string \| null` | No | From settings | DLH inference base URL |
| `dlh_api_key` | `string \| null` | No | From settings | DLH bearer token |
| `dlh_verify_ssl` | `boolean` | No | `false` | Verify DLH TLS certificate |
| `ica_base_url` | `string \| null` | No | From settings | ICA host URL |
| `ica_api_key` | `string \| null` | No | From settings | ICA developer API key |
| `ica_namespace` | `string \| null` | No | From settings | ICA namespace |
| `ica_send_temperature` | `boolean` | No | `false` | Forward temperature/max_tokens to ICA |

---

## Response Schemas

### HealthResponse

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `status` | `string` | Always `"ok"` when healthy |
| `version` | `string` | Application version string (`"2.0.0"`) |

---

### LLMConnectionResponse

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `success` | `boolean` | `true` if the connection test passed |
| `provider` | `string` | The configured provider (`"dlh"` or `"ica"`) |
| `message` | `string` | Human-readable status message |

---

### ComplianceChatResponse

Simplified response from `/api/v1/compliance/chat`.

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `question_id` | `string \| null` | Echo of the request `question_id` |
| `sql_query` | `string \| null` | Generated SQL statement |
| `df_output` | `array \| null` | List of result row objects (`{column: value}` dicts) |
| `result_columns` | `array[string]` | Ordered list of column names for the result set |
| `result_summary` | `string \| null` | Human-readable summary from the Executor Agent |
| `error` | `string \| null` | Error or clarification message (null on clean success) |

---

### ComplianceOrchestrationResponse

Full response from `/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate`.

| Field | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `question_id` | `string \| null` | Echo of the request `question_id` |
| `interpreted_question` | `string \| null` | Normalised question after Intent Agent processing |
| `needs_clarification` | `boolean` | `true` when the Intent Agent requests more detail |
| `clarification_question` | `string \| null` | Follow-up question to show the user |
| `sql_query` | `string \| null` | Generated and reviewed SQL statement |
| `df_output` | `array \| null` | List of result row objects (`{column: value}` dicts) |
| `result_summary` | `string \| null` | Human-readable summary from the Executor Agent |
| `stages` | `array[string]` | Ordered list of completed LangGraph node stage strings |
| `error` | `string \| null` | Pipeline-level error (null on success) |

#### Stage string format

Each element of `stages` is a raw LangGraph agent trace string:

| Stage string pattern | Agent node | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `intent_agent:ok` | IntentAgent | Question is clear; pipeline proceeds |
| `intent_agent:clarification_required` | IntentAgent | Question is vague; clarification requested |
| `intent_agent:empty` | IntentAgent | Empty question received |
| `schema_agent:db=<filename>` | SchemaAgent | Schema read from the named database file |
| `sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry=N` | SQLWriterAgent | SQL generated by LLM on attempt N (N=0 is first attempt) |
| `sql_writer_agent:extraction_failed` | SQLWriterAgent | LLM response did not contain expected SQL delimiters |
| `sql_review_agent:passed` | SQLReviewAgent | All SQL checks passed |
| `sql_review_agent:disallowed_keyword=<kw>` | SQLReviewAgent | DML/DDL keyword found; retry triggered |
| `sql_review_agent:missing_from` | SQLReviewAgent | SQL missing FROM clause; retry triggered |
| `sql_review_agent:unknown_tables=[...]` | SQLReviewAgent | Referenced tables not in DB; retry triggered |
| `executor_agent:ok rows=N` | ExecutorAgent | Query executed; N rows returned |
| `executor_agent:error` | ExecutorAgent | SQL execution error |
| `clarify_node:terminal` | ClarifyNode | Pipeline ended at clarification node |
| `validation:empty_question` | ComplianceProcessor | No question in request |

---

## Error Responses

### 422 Unprocessable Entity (Pydantic validation)

```json
{
  "detail": [
    {
      "loc": ["body", "question"],
      "msg": "'question' must not be a blank string.",
      "type": "value_error"
    }
  ]
}
```

### 500 Internal Server Error (domain error)

```json
{
  "error": "Unexpected pipeline error: ..."
}
```

### 503 Service Unavailable

```json
{
  "detail": "Compliance processor is not initialised. Please retry in a few seconds."
}
```

### 502 Bad Gateway (proxy only — `run_ui.py`)

```json
{
  "error": "<urlopen error [Errno 111] Connection refused>"
}
```

### Check health

```bash
curl http://localhost:8000/health
```

### Configure DLH and run a query

```bash
# 1. Configure the provider (rebuilds LangGraph pipeline)
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"provider":"dlh","model_name":"google/gemma-4-31b-it","dlh_base_url":"http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1","dlh_api_key":"EMPTY"}'

# 2. Ask a compliance question
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question":"How many persons from the Mumbai circle?"}'
```

### Trigger a clarification response

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question":"show me this"}'
```

### Query by KYC status

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question":"Show all accounts where KYC flag is N"}'
```

### Query senior citizens

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question":"Show all senior citizen account holders"}'
```

# Compliance Chatbot v2 — Architecture

## 1. Overview

Compliance Bot v2 is a production-grade NL2SQL banking-compliance system built on **LangGraph**.
A six-node `StateGraph` replaces the old monolithic pipeline. Each node has a single
responsibility; all nodes communicate through a shared `AgentState` TypedDict that flows
through the compiled graph.

**Only one node calls the LLM — `SQLWriterAgent` (`sql_writer_agent.py`).** The remaining
five nodes are deterministic processors (pure-Python, regex, SQLite PRAGMA, or sqlglot AST).

Key components:

| Component | File | Role |
|---|---|---|
| CLI entry-point | `launcher.py` | Starts uvicorn (backend) + `run_ui.py` (UI server) via subprocess |
| FastAPI app | `src/main.py` | Endpoints, lifespan, LangGraph construction |
| Graph factory | `src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py` | Builds and compiles the six-node `StateGraph` |
| Business logic | `src/business_logic/compliance_processor.py` | Thin adapter: `AgentState` ↔ API response models |
| LLM providers | `src/services/model_loader_service.py` | DLH (on-prem) and ICA via OpenAI Python SDK |
| UI server | `run_ui.py` | Stdlib-only HTTP server; proxies `/api/v1/*` and `/health` to backend |

---

## 2. High-Level Architecture

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                         Browser  (run_ui.py)                         │
│                                                                       │
│  Topbar    — Backend Status LED (green/red, polls GET /health 8 s)   │
│  Sidebar   — LLM Configuration · Chat navigation                     │
│  Chat page — Stage pills · Chat bubbles · ↓ Download as CSV          │
│  Composer  — Copy Chat · Export Chat · New Chat · Send               │
│  localStorage persistence (sidebar state, LLM config)                │
└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │  GET /health
                               │  GET /, GET /assets/*
                               │  POST /api/v1/config/llm
                               │  POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate
                               ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     FastAPI Application  (src/main.py)               │
│                                                                       │
│  Lifespan  — build_nl2sql_graph()  ·  ModelLoaderService.load()      │
│  GET  /health                — liveness probe                        │
│  POST /api/v1/config/llm     — reconfigure LLM, rebuild graph        │
│  POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate  — full agentic pipeline        │
│  POST /api/v1/compliance/chat         — simplified response          │
│  Static /assets/*            — Urbanist fonts                        │
└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│               Business Logic  (compliance_processor.py)              │
│                                                                       │
│  • Assigns question_id (UUID)                                        │
│  • Builds initial AgentState                                         │
│  • Calls compiled_graph.invoke(state)                                │
│  • Maps final AgentState → ComplianceOrchestrationResponse           │
└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │  compiled_graph.invoke(AgentState)
                               ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              LangGraph StateGraph  (src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py)      │
│                                                                       │
│  [START]                                                              │
│     │                                                                 │
│     ▼                                                                 │
│  intent_agent (intent.py)  ──── intent_ok=False ───► clarify_node    │
│     │                                                      │         │
│     │ intent_ok=True                                       ▼         │
│     ▼                                                   [END]        │
│  schema_agent (schema.py)                                            │
│     │                                                                 │
│     ▼                        ◄────────────────────────────────┐      │
│  sql_writer_agent ◄──────────────────────────────────────┐    │      │
│  (sql_writer_agent.py)                                   │    │      │
│  ★ ONLY NODE THAT CALLS THE LLM ★                        │    │      │
│     │                                                    │    │      │
│     ▼                                                    │    │      │
│  sql_review_agent (sql_review.py)  ── fail,retry ───────┘    │      │
│     │                                                          │      │
│     │ passed                                                   │      │
│     ▼                                                          │      │
│  sql_validator_agent (sql_validator.py)  ── invalid,retry ────┘      │
│     │                                                                 │
│     │ valid                                                           │
│     ▼                                                                 │
│  executor_agent (executor.py)                                        │
│     │                                                                 │
│     ▼                                                                 │
│  [END]                                                                │
└──────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     SQLite Databases                                 │
│  data/**/*.db                                                        │
│  • data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db  — default compliance DB             │
│  • additional *.db files are auto-discovered by schema.py            │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## 3. Node Responsibilities

Each node in the pipeline has exactly one job. Nodes 1, 2, 4, 4.5, and 5 are
**deterministic processors** — they make no network calls. Only Node 3 calls the LLM.

| Node | File | Class | LLM? | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | `src/nl2sql_agents/intent.py` | `IntentAgent` | ✗ | Keyword-based intent classification; routes to clarification or proceeds |
| **2** | `src/nl2sql_agents/schema.py` | `SchemaAgent` | ✗ | Scans `data/**/*.db`; scores by keyword overlap; builds Markdown schema context |
| **3** | `src/nl2sql_agents/sql_writer_agent.py` | `SQLWriterAgent` | **✓ only one** | Builds few-shot prompt; calls DLH/ICA LLM; extracts SQL from delimiter tags |
| **4** | `src/nl2sql_agents/sql_review.py` | `SQLReviewAgent` | ✗ | Blocks DML/DDL; requires SELECT + FROM; checks tables exist in live DB |
| **4.5** | `src/nl2sql_agents/sql_validator.py` | `SQLValidatorAgent` | ✗ | sqlglot AST parse; SELECT-type gate; SQLite dialect round-trip canonicalisation |
| **5** | `src/nl2sql_agents/executor.py` | `ExecutorAgent` | ✗ | `BEGIN DEFERRED`; `fetchmany(500)`; always `rollback()`; builds result summary |

---

## 4. Layer Responsibilities

| Layer | Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| **Launcher** | [`launcher.py`](../launcher.py) | Starts backend + UI server; watchdog loop; clean shutdown on Ctrl-C |
| **CLI entry-point** | [`main.py`](../main.py) | Parse args; launch uvicorn with `src.main:app` |
| **Config** | [`config/settings.py`](../config/settings.py) | Centralised settings via Pydantic `BaseSettings` |
| **API** | [`src/main.py`](../src/main.py) | HTTP routing, lifespan, LangGraph construction, static-file serving |
| **Business Logic** | [`src/business_logic/compliance_processor.py`](../src/business_logic/compliance_processor.py) | Invokes graph; maps `AgentState` ↔ Pydantic response models |
| **Graph** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py) | Assembles `StateGraph`; node registration; conditional edge routing |
| **State** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/state.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/state.py) | Shared `AgentState` TypedDict (`total=False`); `stages` uses `operator.add` reducer |
| **Node 1** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/intent.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/intent.py) | Rule-based intent classification; clarification detection — **no LLM** |
| **Node 2** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/schema.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/schema.py) | Live SQLite schema introspection; DB selection by keyword score — **no LLM** |
| **Node 3** ★ | [`src/nl2sql_agents/sql_writer_agent.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/sql_writer_agent.py) | **LLM SQL generation** — the only node that calls the LLM |
| **Node 4** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/sql_review.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/sql_review.py) | DML/DDL blocking; `SELECT` + `FROM` checks; table-existence validation; retry loop — **no LLM** |
| **Node 4.5** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/sql_validator.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/sql_validator.py) | sqlglot AST parse; statement-type check; SQLite dialect canonicalisation — **no LLM** |
| **Node 5** | [`src/nl2sql_agents/executor.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/executor.py) | Read-only SQLite execution; result summary — **no LLM** |
| **LLM** | [`src/services/model_loader_service.py`](../src/services/model_loader_service.py) | DLH / ICA provider lifecycle and inference |
| **Models** | [`src/models/schemas.py`](../src/models/schemas.py) | Pydantic request / response shapes |
| **Schema metadata** | [`src/models/compliance_schema.py`](../src/models/compliance_schema.py) | Column descriptions + few-shot LLM prompts |
| **UI server** | [`run_ui.py`](../run_ui.py) | Stdlib-only server; backend status LED; CSV download; proxies `/api/v1/*` + `/health` |
| **Exceptions** | [`src/exceptions/custom_exceptions.py`](../src/exceptions/custom_exceptions.py) | `ComplianceBotError` hierarchy; safe client-facing messages |
| **Utils** | [`src/utils/logger.py`](../src/utils/logger.py) | Centralised logging factory |
| **Utils** | [`src/utils/constants.py`](../src/utils/constants.py) | Shared constants (`SQL_BEGIN_TAG`, `SQL_END_TAG`, table names) |

---

## 5. Runtime Request / Response Flow

```
Browser (run_ui.py)
  │
  │  POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate
  │  {"question": "How many senior citizens in the Thane branch?"}
  │
  ▼
FastAPI  src/main.py
  │  validates payload → ComplianceChatRequest
  │
  ▼
ComplianceProcessor.process_query_with_orchestration()
  │  builds AgentState = {question, question_id, stages: []}
  │
  ▼
compiled_graph.invoke(AgentState)
  │
  ├─► intent.py :: IntentAgent.run(state)              [no LLM]
  │       • normalises question (strip, add "?")
  │       • checks _METRIC_TERMS vs _VAGUE_PHRASES / _AMBIGUOUS_TOKENS
  │       • intent_ok=True → continue  |  False → ClarifyNode → [END]
  │
  ├─► schema.py :: SchemaAgent.run(state)              [no LLM]
  │       • glob data/**/*.db
  │       • score each DB by keyword overlap with question
  │       • PRAGMA table_info → Markdown schema context
  │       • writes db_path + schema_context
  │
  ├─► sql_writer_agent.py :: SQLWriterAgent.run(state) [★ LLM call]
  │       • builds [INST]…[/INST] few-shot prompt
  │       • ModelLoaderService.generate(prompt) → raw LLM output
  │       • extract_sql() strips <begin_sql_response>…<end_sql_response>
  │       • writes sql_query
  │
  ├─► sql_review.py :: SQLReviewAgent.run(state)       [no LLM]
  │       • rejects DML/DDL keywords (regex, whole-word)
  │       • requires query to start with SELECT
  │       • requires FROM clause
  │       • checks every table referenced exists in db_path
  │       • PASS → sql_validator_agent
  │       • FAIL → sql_writer_agent (retry, max 3×, with feedback)
  │
  ├─► sql_validator.py :: SQLValidatorAgent.run(state) [no LLM]
  │       • sqlglot.parse(sql, dialect="sqlite") — full AST parse
  │       • assert statement type == Select
  │       • round-trip to SQLite dialect — canonicalise SQL
  │       • VALID  → writes canonical sql_query → executor_agent
  │       • INVALID → writes review_feedback → sql_writer_agent (retry)
  │
  └─► executor.py :: ExecutorAgent.run(state)          [no LLM]
          • sqlite3.connect(db_path)
          • conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")
          • cursor.execute(sql_query)  →  fetchmany(500)
          • conn.rollback() + conn.close()  (always, even on success)
          • _build_summary() → result_summary
          • writes result_rows, result_columns, result_summary
  │
  ▼
ComplianceProcessor._map_state_to_response()
  │  AgentState → ComplianceOrchestrationResponse
  │
  ▼
Browser  ←  200 OK + sql_query + df_output + result_summary + stages[]
```

---

## 6. Startup Flow

```
python launcher.py
  │
  ├─► subprocess: uvicorn src.main:app  (backend, port CB_BACKEND_PORT=8001)
  │       │
  │       └─► lifespan: _build_processor(model_settings)
  │                  ModelLoaderService.load()    ← connects to DLH or ICA
  │                  build_nl2sql_graph(model_service, db_path)
  │                      └─► StateGraph.compile()
  │                  ComplianceProcessor(compiled_graph)
  │
  ├─► (wait BACKEND_WARMUP_SECONDS=7 for backend to initialise)
  │
  └─► subprocess: python run_ui.py  (UI server, port CDSW_APP_PORT=8100)

# Before first run — seed the compliance database:
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000
```

---

## 7. LangGraph State Design

All data flows through a single `AgentState` TypedDict in
[`src/nl2sql_agents/state.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/state.py).
Each node reads only the keys it needs and writes back only the keys it owns.
LangGraph merges partial dicts automatically; the `stages` list uses an
`operator.add` reducer so every node appends rather than overwrites.

| Field | Written by | Read by |
|---|---|---|
| `question` | Caller | `intent.py` |
| `interpreted_question` | `intent.py` | `schema.py`, `sql_writer_agent.py` |
| `intent_ok` | `intent.py` | Graph router |
| `clarification_needed` | `intent.py` | `ComplianceProcessor` |
| `schema_context` | `schema.py` | `sql_writer_agent.py` |
| `db_path` | `schema.py` | `sql_review.py`, `executor.py` |
| `sql_query` | `sql_writer_agent.py`; `sql_validator.py` (canonical) | `sql_review.py`, `executor.py` |
| `sql_review_passed` | `sql_review.py`; `sql_validator.py` (on failure) | Graph router |
| `review_feedback` | `sql_review.py`; `sql_validator.py` (on failure) | `sql_writer_agent.py` (retry) |
| `retry_count` | `sql_review.py`; `sql_validator.py` (on failure) | `sql_review.py` (ceiling check) |
| `sqlglot_valid` | `sql_validator.py` | Graph router |
| `sqlglot_error` | `sql_validator.py` | `ComplianceProcessor`, UI |
| `result_rows` | `executor.py` | `ComplianceProcessor` |
| `result_columns` | `executor.py` | `ComplianceProcessor`, UI |
| `result_summary` | `executor.py` | `ComplianceProcessor`, UI |
| `stages` | Every node (appended) | `ComplianceProcessor`, UI stage pills |
| `error` | Any node | `ComplianceProcessor` |

---

## 8. Security Design

| Concern | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| SQL injection | `sql_review.py` blocks DML/DDL keywords before execution; `sql_validator.py` adds an independent AST-level gate |
| Unsafe mutation | `sql_review.py` rejects non-SELECT; `executor.py` wraps every query in `BEGIN DEFERRED` + unconditional `rollback()` |
| Malformed SQL | `sql_validator.py` uses sqlglot AST parse — structurally broken queries fail before reaching `executor.py` |
| Non-SELECT at AST level | `sql_validator.py` checks `type(stmt).__name__` in the parsed AST, independent of regex |
| Secret leakage | Provider config loaded from `.env` (git-ignored); no hard-coded credentials |
| Wrong DB selection | Keyword-scored selector in `schema.py` with default fallback |
| Stack-trace exposure | `ComplianceBotError` subclasses expose only `message`, never internal `detail`, to API clients |
| Retry abuse | `sql_review.py` enforces `max_retries=3`; hard-stops with error state after ceiling is reached |

---

## 9. Technology Stack

| Component | Technology |
|---|---|
| API framework | FastAPI 0.111+ |
| Agentic pipeline | LangGraph 0.2+ |
| Data validation | Pydantic v2 |
| LLM providers | DLH (on-prem) and ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage) via OpenAI Python SDK |
| Database | SQLite 3 (bundled with Python stdlib) |
| SQL validation | sqlglot ≥ 25.0.0 — pure-Python AST parser and transpiler |
| Configuration | pydantic-settings + `.env` |
| Logging | Python `logging` module |
| UI server | Python `http.server.HTTPServer` (stdlib only, zero extra deps) |
| UI font | Urbanist (18 weights, self-hosted) |
| Testing | pytest + httpx |
| Python | ≥ 3.11 |

FILE: docs\configuration.md
####################################################################################################

# Compliance Bot — Configuration Reference

All application settings are managed by `config/settings.py` using [pydantic-settings](https://docs.pydantic.dev/latest/concepts/pydantic_settings/). Settings are loaded from environment variables or a `.env` file in the project root.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Setup](#setup)
2. [Environment Variable Reference](#environment-variable-reference)
   - [Application Settings](#application-settings)
   - [Database Settings](#database-settings)
   - [LLM Provider Settings](#llm-provider-settings)
3. [Provider Configurations](#provider-configurations)
   - [DLH (on-prem)](#dlh-on-prem)
   - [ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage)](#ica-ibm-consulting-advantage)
4. [Settings Classes](#settings-classes)
5. [UI Server Settings](#ui-server-settings)
6. [Precedence Rules](#precedence-rules)
7. [Full .env.example](#full-envexample)

---

## Setup

```bash
# Copy the example file
cp .env.example .env

# Edit with your credentials
notepad .env      # Windows
nano .env         # Linux/macOS
```

The `.env` file is read automatically when the application starts. Values defined as environment variables in the shell take precedence over `.env` file values.

---

## Environment Variable Reference

### Application Settings

| Variable | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `APPLICATION_NAME` | string | `COMPLIANCE-BOT (UI + LLM Hybrid)` | Human-readable name shown in logs |
| `PAGE_CONFIG` | string | `SBI COMPLIANCE-BOT` | UI page title |
| `HOST` | string | `0.0.0.0` | FastAPI server bind host |
| `PORT` | integer | `8000` | FastAPI server bind port |
| `DEBUG` | boolean | `false` | Enable uvicorn hot-reload |
| `LOG_LEVEL` | string | `INFO` | Root logging level. Options: `DEBUG`, `INFO`, `WARNING`, `ERROR`, `CRITICAL` |
| `LOG_DIR` | string | `logs` | Directory for rotating log files (created automatically) |

---

### Database Settings

All database settings use the `DATABASE__` prefix (double underscore = nested model separator).

| Variable | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DATABASE__DB_TYPE` | string | `SQLite` | Database engine type (informational only) |
| `DATABASE__DB_FOLDER` | string | `data/comp_db` | Path to the folder containing `.db` files, relative to `compliance_bot/` |
| `DATABASE__DB_NAME` | string | `COMP_REPORTS.db` | Default SQLite database filename |

The resolved absolute path is:
```
<project_root>/compliance_bot/data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db
```

To use a different database as the default:
```env
DATABASE__DB_FOLDER=data/my_db
DATABASE__DB_NAME=MY_DATABASE.db
```

---

### LLM Provider Settings

All LLM settings use the `MODEL__` prefix.

| Variable | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MODEL__PROVIDER` | string | `dlh` | Active provider: `dlh` or `ica` |
| `MODEL__MODEL_NAME` | string | `google/gemma-4-31b-it` | Model name or ICA resource ID |
| `MODEL__MODEL_MAX_NEW_TOKENS` | integer | `512` | Maximum tokens to generate per inference call |
| `MODEL__MODEL_TEMPERATURE` | float | `0.1` | Sampling temperature (lower = more deterministic) |

#### DLH-specific settings

| Variable | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL` | string | `http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1` | Full base URL of the DLH OpenAI-compatible endpoint |
| `MODEL__DLH_API_KEY` | string | `EMPTY` | Bearer token for DLH. Use `EMPTY` if no auth is required. |
| `MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL` | boolean | `false` | Set `true` to enforce TLS certificate verification |

#### ICA-specific settings

| Variable | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL` | string | `https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com` | ICA API host URL |
| `MODEL__ICA_API_KEY` | string | _(empty)_ | ICA developer API key (required when using ICA) |
| `MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE` | string | `chat-models` | ICA namespace. Options: `chat-models`, `agents`, `assistants`, `digital-workforce` |
| `MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE` | boolean | `false` | Set `true` to forward `temperature` and `max_tokens` to ICA calls |

---

## Provider Configurations

### DLH (on-prem)

Minimum `.env` for DLH:

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false
```

The DLH provider:
- Uses the OpenAI Python SDK with a custom `base_url`.
- Always sends `temperature` and `max_tokens`.
- Skips TLS verification by default (self-signed certificates on internal networks).

### ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage)

Minimum `.env` for ICA:

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=ica
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=your-resource-id
MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL=https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com
MODEL__ICA_API_KEY=your-developer-key
MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE=chat-models
MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=false
```

The ICA provider:
- Uses the OpenAI Python SDK with a namespace-specific `base_url` constructed as:
  ```
  {ica_base_url}/ica/v1/{namespace}/
  ```
- Sets `Authorization: Bearer <ica_api_key>` as a default header.
- By default does **not** send `temperature`/`max_tokens` (set `ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=true` to enable).

#### ICA namespace paths

| Namespace | Path suffix |
|---|---|
| `chat-models` | `/ica/v1/chat-models/` |
| `agents` | `/ica/v1/agents/` |
| `assistants` | `/ica/v1/assistants/` |
| `digital-workforce` | `/ica/v1/digital-workforce/` |

---

## Settings Classes

Defined in `config/settings.py`:

### `DatabaseSettings`

```python
class DatabaseSettings(BaseSettings):
    db_type: str      # "SQLite"
    db_folder: str    # "data/comp_db"
    db_name: str      # "COMP_REPORTS.db"

    @property
    def db_path(self) -> Path:
        # Resolves to absolute path on disk
```

### `ModelSettings`

```python
class ModelSettings(BaseSettings):
    provider: str              # "dlh" or "ica"
    model_name: str
    model_max_new_tokens: int
    model_temperature: float
    dlh_base_url: str
    dlh_api_key: str
    dlh_verify_ssl: bool
    ica_base_url: str
    ica_api_key: str
    ica_namespace: str
    ica_send_temperature: bool
```

### `AppSettings`

```python
class AppSettings(BaseSettings):
    model_config = SettingsConfigDict(
        env_file=".env",
        env_file_encoding="utf-8",
        env_nested_delimiter="__",   # ← double underscore for nesting
        extra="ignore",
    )
    application_name: str
    host: str
    port: int
    debug: bool
    log_level: str
    log_dir: str
    database: DatabaseSettings
    model: ModelSettings
```

The singleton accessor `get_settings()` caches one instance for the lifetime of the process.

---

## UI Server Settings

These are **not** read from `.env` — they are environment variables read at runtime by the UI scripts.

### `run_ui.py`

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `CB_UI_PORT` | `5000` | Port the standalone Python UI server binds to |
| `CB_BACKEND_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:8000` | FastAPI backend URL that API calls are proxied to |

```bash
CB_UI_PORT=6000 CB_BACKEND_URL=http://prod-server:8000 python run_ui.py
```

### `gradio_app.py`

| Variable | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `CB_GRADIO_PORT` | `7860` | Gradio server port |
| `CB_BASE_URL` | `http://127.0.0.1:8000` | FastAPI backend URL |

```bash
CB_GRADIO_PORT=8080 CB_BASE_URL=http://prod-server:8000 python gradio_app.py
```

---

## Precedence Rules

1. Shell environment variables (highest priority)
2. `.env` file values
3. Default values defined in `config/settings.py` (lowest priority)

Example — override just the port without editing `.env`:

```bash
PORT=9000 python -m src.main
```

---

## Full .env.example

```env
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Application
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
APPLICATION_NAME="COMPLIANCE-BOT (UI + LLM Hybrid)"
PAGE_CONFIG="SBI COMPLIANCE-BOT"
LOG_LEVEL=INFO
LOG_DIR=logs
HOST=0.0.0.0
PORT=8000
DEBUG=false

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Database
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
DATABASE__DB_TYPE=SQLite
DATABASE__DB_FOLDER=data/comp_db
DATABASE__DB_NAME=COMP_REPORTS.db

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Model / LLM
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__MODEL_MAX_NEW_TOKENS=512
MODEL__MODEL_TEMPERATURE=0.1
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false
MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL=https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com
MODEL__ICA_API_KEY=
MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE=chat-models
MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=false
```

# Compliance Bot — Database Schema Reference

This document describes the structure, column definitions, and seeded sample data for the default compliance database.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Database File](#database-file)
2. [Table: COMP_TBL](#table-comp_tbl)
   - [Full Column Reference](#full-column-reference)
   - [Column Groups](#column-groups)
3. [Seeded Sample Data](#seeded-sample-data)
4. [Database Setup](#database-setup)
5. [Multi-Database Support](#multi-database-support)
6. [Inspecting the Database](#inspecting-the-database)

---

## Database File

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| **File** | `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db` |
| **Engine** | SQLite 3 |
| **Created by** | `data/seed_synthetic_data.py` (run manually before starting the app) |
| **Default table** | `COMP_TBL` |

---

## Table: COMP_TBL

`COMP_TBL` stores term-deposit compliance records for banking accounts. Each row represents one account's compliance snapshot.

### Full Column Reference

| # | Column | SQLite Type | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `BRCD` | `TEXT` | Branch code — short identifier for the branch |
| 2 | `CUST_NBR` | `TEXT` | Customer number |
| 3 | `CUST_NBR_CHKDGT` | `TEXT` | Customer number check digit |
| 4 | `ACCT_NBR` | `TEXT` | Account number |
| 5 | `ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT` | `TEXT` | Account number check digit |
| 6 | `ACCT_BRNCH` | `TEXT` | Account branch identifier |
| 7 | `BRTH_DT` | `TEXT` | Customer date of birth (`YYYY-MM-DD`) |
| 8 | `AGE_YRS` | `INTEGER` | Customer age in years |
| 9 | `SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG` | `INTEGER` | Senior citizen account flag: `1` = senior citizen, `0` = not |
| 10 | `KYC_FLG` | `TEXT` | KYC compliance status: `Y` = compliant, `N` = not compliant |
| 11 | `END_OF_DAY_BAL` | `REAL` | End-of-day account balance in INR |
| 12 | `ACCT_OPN_DT` | `TEXT` | Account opening date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) |
| 13 | `MTRTY_DT` | `TEXT` | Account maturity date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) |
| 14 | `tenor_yr` | `INTEGER` | Tenor in years |
| 15 | `tenor_day` | `INTEGER` | Additional tenor days (beyond whole years) |
| 16 | `MAT_DT` | `TEXT` | Maturity date (may differ from `MTRTY_DT` after modification) |
| 17 | `NEW_EFFCTV_RT` | `REAL` | New effective interest rate (%) |
| 18 | `PRDCT_ID` | `TEXT` | Product identifier code |
| 19 | `PF_FAC_REPT` | `TEXT` | PF facility report flag: `Y` / `N` |
| 20 | `PRODUCT_NAME` | `TEXT` | Full product name (e.g., `PUB IND UNI 181D-10YRS`) |
| 21 | `BRANCHNAME` | `TEXT` | Full branch name (e.g., `VIKRAM SARABHAI BHAWAN`) |
| 22 | `CIRCLENAME` | `TEXT` | Circle name (e.g., `MUMBAI METRO`, `LUCKNOW`) |
| 23 | `CIRCLECD` | `TEXT` | Circle code (e.g., `MM01`) |
| 24 | `EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT` | `REAL` | Expected effective interest rate (%) |
| 25 | `ISMOD` | `TEXT` | Whether the record was modified: `YES` / `NO` |

---

### Column Groups

#### Customer Identity

| Column | Description |
|---|---|
| `BRCD` | Branch code |
| `CUST_NBR` | Customer number |
| `CUST_NBR_CHKDGT` | Check digit for customer number |
| `BRTH_DT` | Date of birth |
| `AGE_YRS` | Age in years |

#### Account Details

| Column | Description |
|---|---|
| `ACCT_NBR` | Account number |
| `ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT` | Check digit for account number |
| `ACCT_BRNCH` | Account branch |
| `ACCT_OPN_DT` | Account opening date |
| `END_OF_DAY_BAL` | End-of-day balance (INR) |

#### Term Deposit / Product

| Column | Description |
|---|---|
| `PRDCT_ID` | Product code |
| `PRODUCT_NAME` | Full product name |
| `MTRTY_DT` | Maturity date |
| `MAT_DT` | Maturity date (post-modification) |
| `tenor_yr` | Tenor years |
| `tenor_day` | Tenor days |
| `NEW_EFFCTV_RT` | New effective rate |
| `EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT` | Expected effective rate |
| `ISMOD` | Modified flag |

#### Compliance Flags

| Column | Values | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG` | `0`, `1` | Senior citizen account |
| `KYC_FLG` | `Y`, `N` | KYC compliance status |
| `PF_FAC_REPT` | `Y`, `N` | PF facility report |

#### Branch / Geography

| Column | Description |
|---|---|
| `BRANCHNAME` | Full branch name |
| `CIRCLENAME` | Circle name (regional grouping) |
| `CIRCLECD` | Circle code |

---

## Seeded Sample Data

Three sample rows are defined below for reference. These are **not** inserted automatically — use [`data/seed_synthetic_data.py`](../data/seed_synthetic_data.py) to populate the database:

| BRCD | CUST_NBR | AGE_YRS | SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG | KYC_FLG | END_OF_DAY_BAL | CIRCLENAME | PRODUCT_NAME | NEW_EFFCTV_RT |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `001` | `C001` | 64 | 1 | `Y` | 250,000 | `MUMBAI METRO` | `PUB IND UNI 181D-10YRS` | 7.25% |
| `002` | `C002` | 52 | 0 | `N` | 120,000 | `MUMBAI METRO` | `TDTDR PUB IND UNI-91-179D-INR` | 6.80% |
| `003` | `C003` | 65 | 1 | `N` | 990,000 | `LUCKNOW` | `SENIOR TERM DEPOSIT` | 7.10% |

These rows are designed to exercise:
- Senior citizen queries (`SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1` → rows 1 and 3)
- KYC non-compliance queries (`KYC_FLG = 'N'` → rows 2 and 3)
- Circle-based filtering (`CIRCLENAME LIKE '%MUMBAI%'` → rows 1 and 2)
- High-balance filtering (`END_OF_DAY_BAL > 500000` → row 3)

---

## Database Setup

The database is **not** seeded automatically at startup. You must run the synthetic data script before the first use:

```bash
# Populate with 2,000 realistic rows (recommended)
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000

# Or any custom count
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 5000
```

The script creates `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db` and `COMP_TBL` if they do not exist, then bulk-inserts the requested number of rows.

> See [synthetic-data.md](synthetic-data.md) for full details, CLI options, and a live snapshot of what was generated.

---

## Multi-Database Support

The application can route queries to multiple databases automatically. The **Schema Agent** (Node 2 in the LangGraph pipeline) handles database discovery and selection at query time — no metadata cache file is required.

To add a second database:

1. Place a `.db` file anywhere under `data/`:
   ```
   data/
   ├── comp_db/
   │   └── COMP_REPORTS.db      ← existing
   └── loan_db/
       └── LOAN_REPORTS.db      ← new
   ```

2. Restart the application. The Schema Agent scans `data/**/*.db` on every request and selects the best match automatically.

3. The routing engine scores each database by matching keywords from the question against:
   - The database filename stem
   - All table names
   - All column names

> See [adding-new-database.md](adding-new-database.md) and [multi-database-routing.md](multi-database-routing.md) for full details.

---

## Inspecting the Database

### Python

```python
import sqlite3
conn = sqlite3.connect("data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db")
rows = conn.execute("SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL").fetchall()
for row in rows:
    print(row)
conn.close()
```

### SQLite CLI

```bash
sqlite3 data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db
sqlite> .tables
sqlite> .schema COMP_TBL
sqlite> SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL;
```

### DB Browser for SQLite

Open `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db` in [DB Browser for SQLite](https://sqlitebrowser.org/) for a GUI view of the schema and data.
