# Compliance Bot v2 — Deployment Guide

This guide covers every deployment scenario: local development, production with uvicorn, Docker, and tips for running all three UI options.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Requirements](#requirements)
2. [The Two `main.py` Files](#the-two-mainpy-files)
3. [Local Development](#local-development)
4. [Production — uvicorn](#production--uvicorn)
5. [Docker](#docker)
6. [Running the UI Options](#running-the-ui-options)
7. [Environment Variable Injection](#environment-variable-injection)
8. [Health Check](#health-check)
9. [Log Management](#log-management)
10. [Database Persistence](#database-persistence)
11. [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting)

---

## Requirements

| Requirement | Minimum version |
|---|---|
| Python | 3.11 |
| pip | 23+ |
| SQLite | Bundled with Python |
| Network access | DLH endpoint or ICA API (for LLM calls) |

---

## The Two `main.py` Files

This project contains two files named `main.py`:

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `main.py` (project root) | **CLI entry-point** — parses `--host`, `--port`, `--reload` and launches uvicorn pointing at `src.main:app` |
| `src/main.py` | **FastAPI application** — defines `app`, all endpoints, lifespan (DB seed + LangGraph build), and static-file serving |

**Always start the application with:**

```bash
python main.py
# or
uvicorn src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000
```

Never run `python src/main.py` directly.

---

## Local Development

### 1. Clone and install

```bash
git clone <repository>
cd COMPLIANCE-BOT-V2-LANGRAPH
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

### 2. Configure

```bash
cp .env.example .env
# Edit .env with your DLH or ICA credentials
```

### 3. Start the backend

```bash
python main.py
```

Or with uvicorn directly (hot-reload):

```bash
uvicorn src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --reload
```

### 4. Verify

```bash
curl http://localhost:8000/health
# {"status":"ok","version":"2.0.0"}
```

### 5. Open the UI

```
http://localhost:8000
```

---

## Production — uvicorn

### Single worker

```bash
uvicorn src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --log-level info
```

### Multiple workers (recommended)

```bash
uvicorn src.main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8000 --workers 4 --log-level info
```

> Each worker loads its own `ModelLoaderService` (lightweight HTTP client) and its own compiled LangGraph pipeline. This is safe for DLH/ICA remote providers.

### With gunicorn (alternative)

```bash
pip install gunicorn
gunicorn src.main:app \
  -k uvicorn.workers.UvicornWorker \
  -w 4 \
  --bind 0.0.0.0:8000 \
  --access-logfile - \
  --error-logfile -
```

---

## Docker

### Dockerfile

```dockerfile
FROM python:3.11-slim

WORKDIR /app

COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

COPY . .

EXPOSE 8000

CMD ["uvicorn", "src.main:app", "--host", "0.0.0.0", "--port", "8000", "--log-level", "info"]
```

### Build and run

```bash
docker build -t compliance-bot:latest .

docker run -d \
  --name compliance-bot \
  -p 8000:8000 \
  -e MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh \
  -e MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://your-dlh-host:9000/api/v1 \
  -e MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=your-key \
  -v $(pwd)/data:/app/data \
  -v $(pwd)/logs:/app/logs \
  compliance-bot:latest
```

### docker-compose.yml

```yaml
version: "3.9"

services:
  compliance-bot:
    build: .
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    env_file:
      - .env
    volumes:
      - ./data:/app/data
      - ./logs:/app/logs
    restart: unless-stopped
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "curl", "-f", "http://localhost:8000/health"]
      interval: 30s
      timeout: 10s
      retries: 3
      start_period: 15s
```

```bash
docker-compose up -d
docker-compose logs -f
```

---

## Running the UI Options

### Option A — FastAPI HTML UI (default)

No extra steps. Served at `GET /` by FastAPI. Open `http://localhost:8000`.

### Option B — Standalone Python UI (`run_ui.py`)

```bash
# Terminal 1 — backend
python main.py

# Terminal 2 — standalone UI server
CB_BACKEND_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python run_ui.py
```

In production:

```bash
CB_UI_PORT=5000 CB_BACKEND_URL=http://prod-backend:8000 python run_ui.py
```

### Option C — Gradio UI (`gradio_app.py`)

```bash
# Terminal 1 — backend
python main.py

# Terminal 2 — Gradio UI
CB_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000 python gradio_app.py
```

---

## Environment Variable Injection

### From a .env file

```bash
python main.py       # reads .env automatically
```

### Shell / CI / Docker

```bash
export MODEL__PROVIDER=ica
export MODEL__ICA_API_KEY=your-key
export MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE=chat-models
python main.py
```

### Kubernetes ConfigMap + Secret example

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: ConfigMap
metadata:
  name: compliance-bot-config
data:
  MODEL__PROVIDER: "ica"
  MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL: "https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com"
  MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE: "chat-models"
  PORT: "8000"
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: compliance-bot-secrets
type: Opaque
stringData:
  MODEL__ICA_API_KEY: "your-secret-key"
```

---

## Health Check

```bash
curl http://localhost:8000/health
# {"status":"ok","version":"2.0.0"}
```

| Platform | Configuration |
|---|---|
| AWS ALB | Target group health check path: `/health` |
| GCP Load Balancer | Backend service health check: `GET /health` |
| Kubernetes | `livenessProbe.httpGet.path: /health` |
| Docker | `HEALTHCHECK CMD curl -f http://localhost:8000/health` |

---

## Log Management

```
logs/
├── compliance_bot.log        ← active file (current write target)
├── compliance_bot.log.1      ← previous file (most recent rotation)
├── compliance_bot.log.2      ← older rotation
└── ...                       ← up to 10 backup files
```

Rotation is **size-based** (`RotatingFileHandler`, 10 MB per file, 10 backup files). Size-based rotation is used because Windows does not allow renaming an open file at midnight, which `TimedRotatingFileHandler` requires.

Log level is controlled by `LOG_LEVEL` in `.env` (default: `INFO`).

---

## Database Persistence

The SQLite database at `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db` is created and seeded automatically at startup. In containerised deployments, mount `data/` as a volume:

```bash
docker run -v /host/data:/app/data compliance-bot:latest
```

To reset to seed data:

```bash
del data\comp_db\COMP_REPORTS.db   # Windows
rm data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db    # Linux/macOS
python main.py
```

---

## Troubleshooting

### "Compliance processor is not initialised" (HTTP 503)

The LangGraph pipeline failed to build at startup because the LLM provider connection failed.

**Resolution:**
1. Check `MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL` or `MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL`.
2. Verify network access to the endpoint.
3. Use `POST /api/v1/config/llm` from the UI to reconnect and rebuild the pipeline.

### "Cannot open database" (`DatabaseConnectionError`)

```bash
mkdir -p data/comp_db
python main.py
```

### "Unknown ICA namespace" (`ModelLoadError`)

Set `MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE` to one of: `chat-models`, `agents`, `assistants`, `digital-workforce`.

### Port already in use

```bash
netstat -ano | findstr :8000   # Windows
lsof -i :8000                  # Linux/macOS
PORT=9000 python main.py
```

### LLM returns no SQL tags

The model output does not contain `<begin_sql_response>` / `<end_sql_response>` tags. The `SQLWriterAgent` logs a warning and returns an empty `sql_query`, which the `SQLReviewAgent` then rejects and retries.

**Resolution:** Lower temperature (`MODEL__MODEL_TEMPERATURE=0.05`) or try a different model.



####################################################################################################
FILE: docs\dlh-and-database-notes.md
####################################################################################################

# DLH, ICA, and Operational Notes

Operational notes for running the Compliance Bot with DLH or ICA providers and managing the SQLite database.

---

## LLM Providers

The Compliance Bot uses [`ModelLoaderService`](../src/services/model_loader_service.py) to manage the LLM provider lifecycle. Only remote providers are supported — there is no local model inference (no `torch`, no `transformers`, no HuggingFace).

The LLM is used by the **SQL Writer Agent** (Node 3) to generate SQL from the natural-language question and the live schema context. The other four agent nodes (Intent, Schema, SQL Review, Executor) are deterministic and do not call the LLM.

---

## DLH (on-prem)

### Connection

`_load_dlh_client()` initialises the OpenAI Python SDK client with:

```python
OpenAI(
    api_key=settings.dlh_api_key,   # typically "EMPTY" for no-auth setups
    base_url=settings.dlh_base_url,  # e.g. http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1
    timeout=30.0,
    http_client=httpx.Client(verify=False),  # when dlh_verify_ssl=false
)
```

### TLS verification

TLS verification is disabled by default (`MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false`) because DLH endpoints typically use self-signed certificates on internal networks. Set `MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=true` in production environments with valid certificates.

### Sampling parameters

DLH always receives `temperature` and `max_tokens` in every request. These are controlled by `MODEL__MODEL_TEMPERATURE` (default `0.1`) and `MODEL__MODEL_MAX_NEW_TOKENS` (default `512`).

### `.env` minimum

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false
```

---

## ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage)

### Connection

`_load_ica_client()` constructs the base URL as:

```
{ica_base_url}/ica/v1/{namespace}/
```

For example, with `ica_base_url=https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com` and `namespace=chat-models`:

```
https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com/ica/v1/chat-models/
```

Sets `Authorization: Bearer <ica_api_key>` as a default header.

### Namespaces

| Namespace | URL path |
|---|---|
| `chat-models` | `/ica/v1/chat-models/` |
| `agents` | `/ica/v1/agents/` |
| `assistants` | `/ica/v1/assistants/` |
| `digital-workforce` | `/ica/v1/digital-workforce/` |

### Sampling parameters

By default, `temperature` and `max_tokens` are **not** sent to ICA (some ICA namespaces reject these parameters). Set `MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=true` to enable them.

### `.env` minimum

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=ica
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=your-resource-id
MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL=https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com
MODEL__ICA_API_KEY=your-developer-key
MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE=chat-models
MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=false
```

---

## Reconnecting at runtime

If the provider connection fails at startup, the application logs an error but continues running. Subsequent chat requests return HTTP 503.

To reconnect without restarting, use the **Test & Save** button in the UI or call:

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"provider":"ica","model_name":"your-id","ica_api_key":"your-key","ica_namespace":"chat-models"}'
```

This replaces the active provider for all subsequent requests in the running process.

---

## SQL delimiters

The LLM is prompted by the SQL Writer Agent to wrap the SQL statement between:

```
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT ...
<end_sql_response>
```

`ModelLoaderService.extract_sql()` uses a regex to extract the SQL between these tags. If the tags are missing, a `SqlGenerationError` is raised. The SQL is then validated by the SQL Review Agent before execution.

---

## Database operations

### Populating the database

Database seeding is **manual** — run the seeder before first use:

```bash
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000
```

> See [synthetic-data.md](synthetic-data.md) for full options.

### Resetting the database

To reset `COMP_REPORTS.db` to seed data:

```bash
# Delete the file and restart — it is recreated automatically
del data\comp_db\COMP_REPORTS.db   # Windows
rm data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db    # Linux/macOS
python main.py
```

### Inspecting the database

```bash
sqlite3 data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db
sqlite> .tables
sqlite> .schema COMP_TBL
sqlite> SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL;
```

---

## Common error messages

| Error | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `HTTP 503 — Compliance processor is not initialised` | Provider failed to connect at startup | Use `POST /api/v1/config/llm` to reconnect |
| `ModelLoadError: ICA API key is required` | `MODEL__ICA_API_KEY` is empty | Set the key in `.env` |
| `ModelLoadError: Unknown ICA namespace` | `MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE` has a typo | Use `chat-models`, `agents`, `assistants`, or `digital-workforce` |
| `SqlGenerationError: SQL delimiter tags not found` | LLM did not wrap the SQL in the expected tags | Lower temperature; try a different model |
| `[Errno 10048] address already in use` | Port 8000 is occupied by another process | Kill the process or change `PORT` in `.env` |
| `DatabaseConnectionError` | `data/comp_db/` directory missing | `mkdir -p data/comp_db` then restart |
| `sql_review_agent:fail` in stages | SQL Writer produced invalid/unsafe SQL three times | Rephrase the question or check the LLM temperature setting |



####################################################################################################
FILE: docs\feature-guide.md
####################################################################################################

# Feature Guide — Compliance Bot v2 (LangGraph)

A detailed walkthrough of every feature.

---

## 1. Provider Configuration

The application supports two remote LLM providers configured through `POST /api/v1/config/llm` or through the UI **Test & Save** button. Saving a new configuration **rebuilds the entire LangGraph pipeline** using the new `ModelLoaderService` instance.

### DLH (on-prem)

Uses the OpenAI Python SDK with a custom `base_url` pointing to the DLH inference server. Always sends `temperature` and `max_tokens`. TLS verification is disabled by default (self-signed certs on internal networks).

### ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage)

Uses the OpenAI Python SDK with a namespace-specific `base_url`:

```
{ica_base_url}/ica/v1/{namespace}/
```

Sets `Authorization: Bearer <key>` as a default header. Does **not** send `temperature`/`max_tokens` by default (enable with `MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=true`).

Supported namespaces: `chat-models`, `agents`, `assistants`, `digital-workforce`.

---

## 2. LangGraph Agentic Pipeline

Every request to `POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate` passes through a compiled LangGraph `StateGraph` with six nodes. The pipeline is defined in [`src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py).

```
[START]
   │
   ▼
Node 1 — IntentAgent  (intent.py)
   • Normalises the question
   • ★ Check 1: disallowed action words (DELETE, DROP, INSERT, UPDATE …)
   │     └─► If found → intent_ok=False → ClarifyNode [END]
   │             UI shows a RED blocked card with the offending keyword
   • ★ Check 2: vague/ambiguous phrasing ("show", "details", "show me this" …)
   │     └─► If matched → intent_ok=False → ClarifyNode [END]
   │             UI shows a BLUE clarification card with example questions
   • If both pass → intent_ok=True
   │
   ▼  [intent_ok=True]
Node 2 — SchemaAgent  (schema.py)
   • Scans data/**/*.db for available databases
   • Scores each by keyword overlap with the question
   • Reads actual SQLite PRAGMA table_info
   • Builds Markdown schema context for the LLM prompt
   │
   ▼
Node 3 — SQLWriterAgent  (sql_writer_agent.py)  ★ ONLY LLM CALL
   • Calls the LLM with a structured few-shot prompt
   • Injects live schema context + few-shot examples
   • Extracts SQL from <begin_sql_response>…<end_sql_response> tags
   │
   ▼
Node 4 — SQLReviewAgent  (sql_review.py)
   • ★ Checks LLM-generated SQL for disallowed keywords
   │     └─► If found → sets error (user-facing RED card) + retries LLM
   • Requires query to start with SELECT
   • Requires FROM clause
   • Checks every referenced table exists in the selected database
   │
   ├─► [review failed, retry_count < 3] ──► SQLWriterAgent (with feedback)
   │
   ▼  [review passed]
Node 4.5 — SQLValidatorAgent  (sql_validator.py)
   • sqlglot AST parse — catches structurally broken SQL
   • Statement-type gate — SELECT only at AST level
   • SQLite dialect round-trip canonicalisation
   │
   ▼  [valid]
Node 5 — ExecutorAgent  (executor.py)
   • Opens SQLite connection with BEGIN DEFERRED (read-only)
   • Executes the SQL; fetchmany(500)
   • Always rolls back and closes connection
   • Builds result_summary string
   │
   ▼
[END]
```

The `stages` list in the response carries the raw trace of every step that ran.

---

## 3. Read-Only SQL Enforcement — Three Layers

### Layer 0 — IntentAgent (before LLM call)

The very first check — happens **before** the schema is read or the LLM is called.

`intent.py` checks the user's raw question for disallowed action words:

```
delete  drop  insert  update  truncate  alter  create  replace
pragma  attach  detach  vacuum  modify  remove  erase  wipe  purge  destroy
```

If any of these words appear as whole tokens, the pipeline terminates immediately with `intent_ok=False`. The UI shows a **red blocked card** naming the offending keyword and directing the user to ask a read-only question.

### Layer 1 — SQLReviewAgent (LLM-generated SQL)

Even if the user's question passes Layer 0, the LLM might still produce SQL containing a disallowed keyword. `sql_review.py` catches this with a whole-word regex check on the generated SQL:

```
INSERT  UPDATE  DELETE  DROP  TRUNCATE  ALTER  CREATE  REPLACE  PRAGMA  ATTACH  DETACH  VACUUM
```

If found, the agent sets `sql_review_passed=False`, sets a user-facing `error` (shown as a **red blocked card** in the UI), increments `retry_count`, and routes back to `SQLWriterAgent` with corrective feedback. After 3 failed retries the pipeline surfaces the error to the user.

### Layer 2 — ExecutorAgent (at execution)

Even if everything above passes, every database connection is wrapped in:

```python
conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")
cursor.execute(sql_query)
# ... fetchmany ...
conn.rollback()   # always — even on success
conn.close()
```

`BEGIN DEFERRED` prevents any accidental write from taking effect; `rollback()` in the `finally` block ensures this even if an exception is raised mid-execution.

---

## 4. Clarification Flow

When the Intent Agent detects a vague or ambiguous question (e.g., `"show"`, `"details"`, `"show me this"`):

1. Sets `intent_ok = False`
2. Sets `clarification_needed` to a friendly prompt with example questions
3. Graph routes to `ClarifyNode` (terminal) — no schema read, no SQL, no execution
4. Response returns `needs_clarification: true` + `clarification_question`

The UI displays the clarification message as a **blue clarification card** with formatted bullet-point examples. The user types a more specific follow-up and sends again.

**What triggers clarification:**
- Bare vague phrases: `"show"`, `"details"`, `"info"`, `"data"`, `"give me"`, `"tell me"`
- Ambiguous pronouns without a domain term: `"show me this"`, `"what about that"`

---

## 6. Multi-Database Routing (via SchemaAgent)

The `SchemaAgent` handles database discovery and selection on every request — no startup-time cache needed.

**How it works:**
1. Scans `data/**/*.db` for all SQLite files
2. For each file, connects and reads table/column names via `PRAGMA table_info`
3. Scores each database: +1 per matching column name in the question, +2 per matching table name
4. Selects the highest-scoring database; falls back to the configured default if no match

Adding a new database is as simple as placing a `.db` file under `data/` — no restart needed beyond the next query.

---

## 7. SQLite Database Setup

The database is **not** seeded automatically. Before first use, run:

```bash
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000
```

This creates `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db`, creates `COMP_TBL` with the full 25-column schema, and inserts 2,000 realistic synthetic rows. See [`docs/synthetic-data.md`](synthetic-data.md) for full details.

---

## 8. UI Features

### Blocked keyword card

When a disallowed keyword is detected (in the user's question or in the LLM-generated SQL), the UI renders a **red notice card** instead of a normal bot message:

- Clearly names the blocked keyword in bold
- States this is a read-only interface
- Provides three example valid questions
- No stage pills are lit for queries blocked at the intent layer

### Clarification card

When the question is too vague or ambiguous, the UI renders a **blue clarification card**:

- Explains the question needs more detail
- Shows four formatted example questions as bullet points
- Asks the user to rephrase

### Live LangGraph stage trail

After each response, the five agent node pills update visually:

```
[ Intent Agent ]  [ Schema Agent ]  [ SQL Writer ]  [ SQL Review ]  [ Executor ]
```

Pills turn blue as the corresponding agent runs. The raw stage trace (e.g., `intent_agent:ok → schema_agent:db=COMP_REPORTS.db → ...`) is also shown as a compact text line in the bot reply.

### Result summary

The `ExecutorAgent` produces a `result_summary` string for the response:

- Scalar aggregation (`COUNT`, `SUM`): `Result: **record_count** = \`1\``
- Multi-row query: `Query returned **N record(s)**. Columns: COL1, COL2, ...`
- Empty result: `The query executed successfully but returned no matching records.`

### Loading animation

A three-dot bounce animation appears as soon as a message is sent and disappears when the response arrives. The Send button is disabled and shows `Sending…` during this time.

### Scrollable chat

Fixed-height (480 px) scrollable container with `scroll-behavior: smooth`. New messages auto-scroll into view.

### Full result rendering

SQL results are rendered as a full JSON code block — no truncation. Long values wrap naturally using `white-space: pre-wrap; word-break: break-all`.

### Local storage persistence

| Key | Stored value |
|---|---|
| `cb_sidebar_width` | Sidebar width in pixels |
| `cb_sidebar_collapsed` | Collapsed state |
| `cb_active_page` | Last active page |
| `cb_llm_config` | All LLM configuration fields |

---

## 9. Logging

Rotating log files are written to `logs/compliance_bot.log` using size-based rotation (`RotatingFileHandler` — 10 MB per file, 10 backup files). Size-based rotation is used instead of time-based rotation because Windows does not allow renaming an open file, which `TimedRotatingFileHandler` requires at midnight.

```
2026-07-29 11:05:54 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.intent_agent | IntentAgent — intent resolved: 'How many ...?'
2026-07-29 11:05:54 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.schema_agent | SchemaAgent — selected db=COMP_REPORTS.db
2026-07-29 11:05:54 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.sql_writer_agent | SQLWriterAgent — rule-based SQL: SELECT COUNT(*)...
```

Log level is controlled by `LOG_LEVEL` in `.env` (default: `INFO`). Set to `DEBUG` for verbose output.

## Prerequisites

| Requirement | Minimum version | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Python | 3.11 | 3.12 / 3.14 also tested |
| pip | 23+ | Included with Python |
| An LLM endpoint | — | DLH (on-prem) **or** IBM ICA |
| SQLite | 3.x | Bundled with Python — no install needed |

No Docker, no Redis, no Node.js required.

---

## Installation

```bash
# 1. Clone the repository
git clone <repo-url>
cd COMPLIANCE-BOT-V2-LANGRAPH

# 2. Create and activate a virtual environment (recommended)
python -m venv .venv

# Windows
.venv\Scripts\activate

# Linux / macOS
source .venv/bin/activate

# 3. Install dependencies
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

---

## Configuration

### Step 1 — Create your `.env` file

Copy the example and edit it:

```bash
# Windows
copy .env.example .env
notepad .env

# Linux / macOS
cp .env.example .env
nano .env
```

The only required edits are the LLM provider credentials. Everything else has sensible defaults.

### Step 2 — Choose your LLM provider

#### DLH (on-premises OpenAI-compatible endpoint)

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://<your-dlh-host>:<port>/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false
```

- Set `MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY` if your DLH endpoint requires no authentication.
- Set `MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=true` only for endpoints with valid TLS certificates.

#### ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage)

```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=ica
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=your-resource-id
MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL=https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com
MODEL__ICA_API_KEY=your-developer-key
MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE=chat-models
MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=false
```

- `MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE` options: `chat-models` · `agents` · `assistants` · `digital-workforce`
- Leave `MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=false` unless your namespace explicitly accepts sampling params.

> See [`docs/configuration.md`](configuration.md) for the complete variable reference and
> [`docs/dlh-and-database-notes.md`](dlh-and-database-notes.md) for connection troubleshooting.

---

## Database Setup

The database is **not** populated automatically. Run the seeder before starting the app for the first time:

```bash
# Create the DB file, create COMP_TBL, and insert 2,000 synthetic rows
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000
```

Expected output:

```
[create] Database file created: ...data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db
[create] COMP_TBL created.
[insert] 2,000 rows inserted into COMP_TBL.

=======================================================
  Database : ...COMP_REPORTS.db
  Inserted : 2,000 rows
  Total    : 2,000 rows in COMP_TBL
=======================================================
  Senior citizens  (SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG=1) : 759
  KYC non-compliant (KYC_FLG='N')        : 267
  ...
=======================================================
```

The seeder is **safe to re-run** — it creates only what is missing:

```bash
# Re-run on an existing database — no harm done
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 500    # append 500 more rows

# Wipe and start fresh
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000

# Create DB + table only, no rows (useful for CI)
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 0
```

> See [`docs/synthetic-data.md`](synthetic-data.md) for full details on what the seeder generates.

---

## Starting the Application

There are three independent ways to run the UI. All of them require the FastAPI backend to be running first (Options A and B/C use different ports).

### Option A — FastAPI HTML UI (default)

This is the **recommended** way. One command starts both the backend and the embedded HTML UI.

```bash
python main.py
```

Then open **http://localhost:8080** (or whatever `PORT` is set to in your `.env`).

```
python main.py
    │
    └─► uvicorn src.main:app
            │
            ├─ build_nl2sql_graph()   ← LangGraph pipeline compiled
            └─ ComplianceProcessor    ← ready to accept requests

Swagger UI  →  http://localhost:8080/docs
Health      →  http://localhost:8080/health
```

Override host/port without editing `.env`:

```bash
python main.py --port 9000
python main.py --host 127.0.0.1 --port 9000
python main.py --reload            # hot-reload for development
```

---

### Option B — Standalone Python UI

Runs a lightweight pure-stdlib HTTP server that proxies API calls to the FastAPI backend.
Useful when you want the UI on a different machine or port.

```bash
# Terminal 1 — start the FastAPI backend
python main.py

# Terminal 2 — start the standalone UI
python run_ui.py
```

Open **http://localhost:5000**.

Override the ports without editing any file:

```bash
# Windows PowerShell
$env:CB_UI_PORT = "6000"
$env:CB_BACKEND_URL = "http://127.0.0.1:8080"
python run_ui.py

# Linux / macOS
CB_UI_PORT=6000 CB_BACKEND_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080 python run_ui.py
```

---

### Option C — Gradio UI

Requires `gradio` and `httpx` (already in `requirements.txt`).

```bash
# Terminal 1 — start the FastAPI backend
python main.py

# Terminal 2 — start Gradio
python gradio_app.py
```

Open **http://localhost:7860**.

Override the ports:

```bash
# Windows PowerShell
$env:CB_GRADIO_PORT = "7861"
$env:CB_BASE_URL = "http://127.0.0.1:8080"
python gradio_app.py

# Linux / macOS
CB_GRADIO_PORT=7861 CB_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8080 python gradio_app.py
```

---

## Asking Questions

### What kinds of questions work

The bot is trained on `COMP_TBL` — a banking term-deposit compliance table. Ask questions about:

| Category | Example questions |
|---|---|
| **Counts** | `How many senior citizens are there?` |
| | `How many accounts have KYC flag N?` |
| | `How many records are in the Mumbai Metro circle?` |
| **Filters** | `Show accounts where age is more than 60 and KYC is N` |
| | `List all accounts with balance greater than 500000` |
| | `Show senior citizens in the Lucknow circle` |
| **Aggregates** | `What is the average balance of senior citizens?` |
| | `Show total balance by circle` |
| | `Which product has the most accounts?` |
| **Lookups** | `Show all accounts for branch VIKRAM SARABHAI BHAWAN` |
| | `List modified records (ISMOD = YES)` |
| | `Show accounts with PF facility` |

### What the five agent stages mean

After each response, the UI shows a stage trail — one badge per agent that ran:

| Stage badge | Agent | What it did |
|---|---|---|
| **Intent Agent** | `IntentAgent` | Parsed your question, checked it was specific enough to query |
| **Schema Agent** | `SchemaAgent` | Scanned `data/**/*.db`, selected the best database, built the schema context |
| **SQL Writer** | `SQLWriterAgent` | Generated the SQL (rule-based fast path first, LLM fallback) |
| **SQL Review** | `SQLReviewAgent` | Checked for DML/DDL, verified SELECT + FROM, confirmed table exists |
| **Executor** | `ExecutorAgent` | Ran the query read-only against SQLite, formatted the result rows |

If **Intent Agent** fires a **Clarification** badge instead, your question was too vague — rephrase it with a specific metric or filter.

### Understanding the response

```json
{
  "interpreted_question": "Show accounts where AGE_YRS > 60 and KYC_FLG = 'N'",
  "sql_query":            "SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE AGE_YRS > 60 AND KYC_FLG = 'N';",
  "df_output": [
    {"BRCD": "042", "CUST_NBR": "C000031", "AGE_YRS": 74, "KYC_FLG": "N", "...": "..."}
  ],
  "result_summary": "Found 43 rows matching your query.",
  "stages": [
    "intent_agent:ok",
    "schema_agent:db=COMP_REPORTS.db",
    "sql_writer_agent:ok",
    "sql_review_agent:pass",
    "executor_agent:ok"
  ],
  "needs_clarification": false,
  "error": null
}
```

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `interpreted_question` | How the Intent Agent understood your question after normalisation |
| `sql_query` | The exact SQL run against the database |
| `df_output` | Result rows as a list of `{column: value}` dicts |
| `result_summary` | One-line plain-English summary of the result |
| `stages` | Ordered list of agent steps — useful for debugging |
| `needs_clarification` | `true` when the Intent Agent asked a follow-up question |
| `clarification_question` | The follow-up question text (when `needs_clarification` is `true`) |
| `error` | Non-null only when an unrecoverable pipeline error occurred |

---

## Using the API directly

The FastAPI backend exposes a Swagger UI at **http://localhost:8080/docs**.

### Health check

```bash
curl http://localhost:8080/health
```

```json
{"status": "ok", "version": "2.0.0"}
```

### Ask a compliance question

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "How many senior citizens have KYC flag N?"}'
```

For a simpler response (SQL + rows only, no stage trace):

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/v1/compliance/chat \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "Show all accounts in Mumbai Metro"}'
```

### Reconfigure the LLM at runtime

Switch to a different provider without restarting:

```bash
# Switch to ICA
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "provider": "ica",
    "model_name": "your-resource-id",
    "ica_base_url": "https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com",
    "ica_api_key": "your-key",
    "ica_namespace": "chat-models"
  }'
```

```bash
# Switch back to DLH
curl -X POST http://localhost:8080/api/v1/config/llm \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "provider": "dlh",
    "model_name": "google/gemma-4-31b-it",
    "dlh_base_url": "http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1",
    "dlh_api_key": "EMPTY"
  }'
```

This rebuilds the LangGraph pipeline in-memory. No restart required.

---

## Changing the Port

**Quickest** — CLI flag (one run only):

```bash
python main.py --port 9000
```

**Permanent** — set `PORT` in your `.env` file:

```env
PORT=8080
```

**Shell override** (one terminal session):

```powershell
# Windows PowerShell
$env:PORT = "9000"
python main.py
```

```bash
# Linux / macOS
PORT=9000 python main.py
```

If port 8080 is already in use by a leftover process, find and kill it:

```powershell
# Windows — find what is on port 8080
netstat -ano | findstr ":8080"
# Kill by PID
taskkill /PID <PID> /F
```

```bash
# Linux / macOS
lsof -ti tcp:8080 | xargs kill -9
```

---

## Adding More Data

```bash
# Append rows to the existing database
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 1000

# Completely replace all rows
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 5000

# Use a fixed seed for reproducible data
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000 --seed 123

# Target a different database file
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --db data/other_db/OTHER.db --count 2000
```

To add an entirely new database for multi-DB routing:

1. Place any `.db` file under `data/` (e.g., `data/loan_db/LOAN_REPORTS.db`).
2. The Schema Agent discovers it automatically on the next query — no restart needed.
3. Ask a question that mentions column names or table names from the new database.

> See [`docs/adding-new-database.md`](adding-new-database.md) for the full walkthrough.

---

## Logs

Structured logs are written to the `logs/` directory (created automatically):

```
logs/
└── compliance_bot.log    ← rotating log file
```

Each line is formatted as:

```
2026-07-29 19:06:20 | INFO | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.graph | graph:208 | NL2SQL LangGraph compiled successfully.
```

Change the verbosity in `.env`:

```env
LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG     # DEBUG | INFO | WARNING | ERROR | CRITICAL
```

---

## Troubleshooting

| Symptom | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `[Errno 10048] address already in use` | Port is occupied by a leftover process | Kill the process or change `PORT` in `.env` |
| `HTTP 503 — Compliance processor is not initialised` | LLM connection failed at startup | Use `POST /api/v1/config/llm` or check `.env` credentials |
| `no such table: COMP_TBL` | Database not seeded | Run `python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000` |
| `ModelLoadError: ICA API key is required` | `MODEL__ICA_API_KEY` is empty | Add your ICA key to `.env` |
| `ModelLoadError: Unknown ICA namespace` | Typo in `MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE` | Use `chat-models`, `agents`, `assistants`, or `digital-workforce` |
| `SqlGenerationError: SQL delimiter tags not found` | LLM did not wrap SQL in expected tags | Lower `MODEL__MODEL_TEMPERATURE`; try a different model |
| Clarification badge fires for every question | Question too vague | Add a specific filter — column name, circle name, flag value |
| `sql_review_agent:fail` in stages | SQL Writer failed review 3 times | Rephrase the question; check LLM temperature |
| Gradio UI shows a blank page | Backend not running | Start `python main.py` first, then `python gradio_app.py` |
| `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'langgraph'` | Dependencies not installed | Run `pip install -r requirements.txt` |

## The Six-Node Agent Pipeline

```
User question
     │
     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 1 — IntentAgent                                           │
│  Rule-based. Normalises question. Checks for domain vocabulary. │
│  intent_ok=True → proceed  │  intent_ok=False → clarify + END  │
└────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 2 — SchemaAgent                                           │
│  Scans data/**/*.db. Scores databases by keyword overlap.       │
│  Reads PRAGMA table_info. Builds Markdown schema + sample rows. │
└────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 3 — SQLWriterAgent                                        │
│  Calls LLM with structured few-shot prompt.                     │
│  Falls back to LLM with few-shot prompt + live schema context.  │
└────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 4 — SQLReviewAgent                                        │
│  Blocks DML/DDL keywords. Requires SELECT + FROM.              │
│  Checks table existence. Retries writer up to 3× with feedback.│
└────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 4.5 — SQLValidatorAgent  ← NEW                           │
│  sqlglot AST parse check. Statement-type gate (SELECT only).   │
│  SQLite dialect round-trip canonicalisation. Graceful skip if  │
│  sqlglot not installed.                                         │
└────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                                     │
                                     ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  Node 5 — ExecutorAgent                                         │
│  BEGIN DEFERRED transaction. fetchmany(500). Rollback.         │
│  Returns result_rows, result_columns, result_summary.          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
     │
     ▼
ComplianceOrchestrationResponse (all stages, SQL, rows, summary)
```

# Multi-Database Runtime Routing

This document describes how the Compliance Bot discovers and selects databases at runtime using the **Schema Agent** (Node 2 of the LangGraph NL2SQL pipeline).

---

## How it works

1. **Schema Agent invoked** — On every chat request, `SchemaAgent.run()` is called as the second node in the LangGraph graph.
2. **Live scan** — The agent calls `pathlib.Path.glob("**/*.db")` on the configured `data_root` directory to find all SQLite databases.
3. **Live schema introspection** — For each `.db` file, the agent opens a read-only connection and reads all table names and column names via `PRAGMA table_info`.
4. **Keyword scoring** — Each database is scored by counting keyword overlaps between the user's question and the database filename stem, table names, and column names.
5. **Selection** — The highest-scoring database path is written into `AgentState.db_path`.
6. **Schema context build** — A Markdown-formatted schema description is built from the selected database and written into `AgentState.schema_context` for prompt injection.
7. **SQL generation** — The SQL Writer Agent (Node 3) uses `schema_context` to generate accurate SQL for the selected database.
8. **SQL execution** — The Executor Agent (Node 5) opens the selected `db_path` in read-only mode to run the query.

> There is no static metadata cache file. Schema introspection happens fresh on every request, so it is always in sync with the actual database files.

---

## Scoring algorithm

```
score(db, question) = Σ keyword_matches(question_tokens, db_keywords)
```

Where:

| Match source | Weight | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Database filename stem | +1 per token match | `comp` matches `comp_reports.db` |
| Table name (full token) | +2 per match | `comp_tbl` in question gets +2 |
| Column name (full token) | +1 per match | `age_yrs` in question gets +1 |

`question_tokens` are the lowercased words extracted from the user's question.

The database with the highest score is selected. On a tie, the first candidate (alphabetically sorted by path) wins. If all scores are zero, the configured default (`DATABASE__DB_FOLDER` + `DATABASE__DB_NAME`) is used as fallback.

### Example

Question: `"How many senior citizens are in the Mumbai circle?"`

Tokens: `how`, `many`, `senior`, `citizens`, `are`, `in`, `the`, `mumbai`, `circle`

| Database | Keyword matches | Score |
|---|---|---|
| `COMP_REPORTS` | `circlename` (circle ✓), `sr_ctzn_acct_flg` (senior ✓, citizens ✓) | 3 |
| `LOAN_REPORTS` | none | 0 |

→ `COMP_REPORTS` selected. `stages` will include `schema_agent:db=COMP_REPORTS.db`.

---

## Schema context format

The schema context injected into the SQL Writer prompt looks like:

```markdown
## Database: `COMP_REPORTS`

### Table: `COMP_TBL`

| # | Column | Type | PK | Not Null |
|---|--------|------|----|----------|
| 0 | `BRCD` | TEXT |  |  |
| 1 | `CUST_NBR` | TEXT |  |  |
| 7 | `AGE_YRS` | INTEGER |  |  |
| 8 | `SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG` | INTEGER |  |  |
| 9 | `KYC_FLG` | TEXT |  |  |
...
```

This is generated live from `PRAGMA table_info` and is always accurate.

---

## Extending the routing logic

The current algorithm is intentionally lightweight. Future improvements can be made inside `SchemaAgent._select_database()` in [`src/nl2sql_agents/schema_agent.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/schema_agent.py):

| Improvement | Benefit |
|---|---|
| Per-database domain description file | Richer semantic scoring beyond column names |
| LLM-based DB classifier | Handles paraphrased or domain-ambiguous questions |
| Confidence threshold + clarification | Asks the user which database to query when the score is too low |
| Per-table few-shot examples in schema context | Better SQL generation for the selected table |

---

## Modules involved

| Module | Role |
|---|---|
| [`src/nl2sql_agents/schema_agent.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/schema_agent.py) | Scans, scores, selects, and describes the database |
| [`src/nl2sql_agents/sql_writer_agent.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/sql_writer_agent.py) | Consumes `schema_context` to generate SQL |
| [`src/nl2sql_agents/executor_agent.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/executor_agent.py) | Opens `db_path` in read-only mode to execute the query |
| [`src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py) | Wires the nodes together in a `StateGraph` |
| [`src/nl2sql_agents/state.py`](../src/nl2sql_agents/state.py) | `AgentState` fields: `db_path`, `schema_context` |



####################################################################################################
FILE: docs\sqlglot-validation.md
####################################################################################################

# sqlglot SQL Validation — Technical Reference

> **Node 4.5** in the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.  
> Performs deep AST-level SQL validation using [sqlglot](https://github.com/tobymao/sqlglot) before any query reaches the SQLite executor.

---

## Why sqlglot?

The `SQLReviewAgent` (Node 4) uses fast regex matching to block DML/DDL and verify structural patterns (`SELECT`, `FROM`, table names). Regex is intentionally lightweight — it does not understand SQL grammar. sqlglot adds a second, independent validation layer by parsing the SQL into an **Abstract Syntax Tree (AST)**:

| Check | SQLReviewAgent (regex) | SQLValidatorAgent (sqlglot AST) |
|---|---|---|
| Block INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE / DROP | ✓ Keyword match | ✓ AST statement type |
| Require `SELECT` prefix | ✓ String startswith | ✓ AST node type |
| Require `FROM` clause | ✓ Regex pattern | ✓ AST structure |
| Table existence (live DB) | ✓ PRAGMA query | — |
| Structurally malformed SQL | ✗ Not detectable | ✓ `ParseError` |
| Wrong dialect constructs | ✗ Not detectable | ✓ Dialect-aware transpilation |
| Subquery / CTE safety | ✗ Not detectable | ✓ Full AST traversal |
| SQL normalisation / canonicalisation | ✗ | ✓ Round-trips to SQLite dialect |

---

## Pipeline Position

```
[START]
   │
   ▼
IntentAgent (Node 1)
   │
   ▼
SchemaAgent (Node 2)
   │
   ▼
SQLWriterAgent (Node 3) ◄───────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                                         │ retry (review OR validator failed)
   ▼                                                         │
SQLReviewAgent (Node 4)                                      │
   │                                                         │
   ├─ review failed ──────────────────────────────────────► (retry)
   │
   │ review passed
   ▼
SQLValidatorAgent (Node 4.5)   ← sqlglot AST validation
   │
   ├─ sqlglot_valid=False ───────────────────────────────► (retry)
   │
   │ sqlglot_valid=True
   ▼
ExecutorAgent (Node 5) ──► [END]
```

---

## Three Validation Steps

### Step 1 — Parse

```python
parsed = sqlglot.parse(sql, dialect="sqlite", error_level=ErrorLevel.RAISE)
```

sqlglot tokenises and parses the full SQL statement into an AST.  A `ParseError` means the statement is structurally invalid and *would* raise `sqlite3.OperationalError` at execution time.

**Example failures caught:**

```sql
-- Unbalanced parentheses
SELECT COUNT(*) FROM COMP_TBL WHERE (KYC_FLG = 'N'

-- Incomplete expression
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE

-- Invalid token sequence
SELECT ,, FROM COMP_TBL
```

### Step 2 — Statement Type Check

```python
stmt_type = type(parsed[0]).__name__.lower()  # "select", "insert", "update", …
assert stmt_type == "select"
```

Even if a DML keyword somehow escaped the regex in `SQLReviewAgent`, sqlglot's AST-level type check provides an independent gate. Only `Select` AST nodes are allowed.

### Step 3 — SQLite Dialect Round-Trip (Canonicalisation)

```python
canonical = stmt.sql(dialect="sqlite", pretty=False)
```

The parsed AST is re-serialised back to SQL using sqlglot's SQLite dialect emitter.  This:

- Normalises quoting (backticks → double quotes or no quotes)
- Normalises function names (`ILIKE` → `LIKE` for SQLite)
- Normalises `LIMIT x, y` → `LIMIT y OFFSET x` (SQLite-compatible)

If the canonical form differs from the input, `sql_query` in the pipeline state is silently updated so the `ExecutorAgent` always receives clean SQLite.

---

## AgentState Fields

Two new fields are added to `AgentState` by this node:

| Field | Type | Written by | Read by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sqlglot_valid` | `bool` | `SQLValidatorAgent` | Graph router (`_route_after_validator`) |
| `sqlglot_error` | `Optional[str]` | `SQLValidatorAgent` | UI (surfaced via `review_feedback`) |

On failure, the agent also writes:
- `sql_review_passed = False` — so the routing logic correctly triggers a writer retry
- `review_feedback` — human-readable reason passed to the SQL Writer on the next cycle
- `retry_count += 1`

---

## Stage Strings

The `stages` list in `AgentState` will contain one of:

| Stage string | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `sql_validator_agent:passed dialect=sqlite` | All checks passed; SQL is valid SQLite |
| `sql_validator_agent:parse_error` | sqlglot could not build an AST |
| `sql_validator_agent:empty_ast` | Parser returned no statements |
| `sql_validator_agent:disallowed_stmt_type=<type>` | Non-SELECT statement detected |
| `sql_validator_agent:skipped_no_sqlglot` | sqlglot not installed; check skipped |

---

## Graceful Degradation

If `sqlglot` is not installed, the agent logs a warning and marks `sqlglot_valid=True` with stage `sql_validator_agent:skipped_no_sqlglot`. This means the pipeline continues to work even without the dependency — the regex-based `SQLReviewAgent` remains the only guard.

Install sqlglot to enable AST validation:

```bash
pip install sqlglot>=25.0.0
# or
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

---

## Configuration

`SQLValidatorAgent` accepts two constructor arguments, configurable via `build_nl2sql_graph()`:

| Parameter | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `dialect` | `"sqlite"` | sqlglot source + target dialect |
| `strict_mode` | `False` | Reserved for future use |

`build_nl2sql_graph()` exposes `sqlglot_dialect`:

```python
compiled_graph = build_nl2sql_graph(
    model_service=model_service,
    db_path=db_path,
    max_review_retries=3,
    sqlglot_dialect="sqlite",   # ← new parameter
)
```

---

## Testing

```python
# tests/test_sql_validator_agent.py
import sqlite3
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_validator_agent import SQLValidatorAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


@pytest.fixture()
def agent() -> SQLValidatorAgent:
    return SQLValidatorAgent(dialect="sqlite")


class TestSQLValidatorAgent:

    def test_valid_select_passes(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT COUNT(*) AS total FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is True
        assert result.get("sqlglot_error") is None

    def test_malformed_sql_fails(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE (KYC_FLG = 'N'",  # unclosed paren
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is False
        assert result["sql_review_passed"] is False
        assert "sqlglot" in result["review_feedback"].lower()

    def test_insert_blocked_at_ast_level(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="INSERT INTO COMP_TBL VALUES ('N', 60);",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is False
        assert "INSERT" in result["review_feedback"].upper()

    def test_retry_count_incremented_on_failure(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="NOT VALID SQL AT ALL !!!",
            retry_count=1,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["retry_count"] == 2

    def test_sql_canonicalised_on_success(self, agent):
        # sqlglot may normalise whitespace / quoting
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="select   count(*)  from COMP_TBL",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is True
        # canonical form should be a clean string
        assert result["sql_query"].strip() != ""

    def test_stage_string_on_pass(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL;",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert any("sql_validator_agent:passed" in s for s in result["stages"])

    def test_stage_string_on_failure(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="DELETE FROM COMP_TBL;",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert any("sql_validator_agent:" in s for s in result["stages"])
```

sqlglot has **zero runtime dependencies** and is a pure-Python wheel — it does not require any native libraries or build tools.

```
pip install sqlglot>=25.0.0
```

# Synthetic Data Seeding

This document explains the [`data/seed_synthetic_data.py`](../data/seed_synthetic_data.py) script — what it generates, how to run it, how to customise it, and what the current database contains.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Purpose](#purpose)
2. [Quick start](#quick-start)
3. [CLI reference](#cli-reference)
4. [What the script generates](#what-the-script-generates)
   - [Generation rules per column](#generation-rules-per-column)
   - [Reference data pools](#reference-data-pools)
5. [Current database snapshot](#current-database-snapshot)
   - [Row counts](#row-counts)
   - [Balance distribution](#balance-distribution)
   - [Rows by circle](#rows-by-circle)
   - [Rows by product](#rows-by-product)
   - [Rows by tenor](#rows-by-tenor)
6. [Sample queries to verify the data](#sample-queries-to-verify-the-data)
7. [Reproducibility](#reproducibility)
8. [Dependencies](#dependencies)

---

## Purpose

The application ships with only 3 auto-seeded rows (enough for a smoke test). The synthetic data script inserts thousands of **realistic, banking-domain rows** so that:

- NL2SQL queries return meaningful result sets rather than 1–3 rows.
- Aggregate queries (COUNT, SUM, AVG, GROUP BY) produce non-trivial answers.
- Multi-filter compliance queries (senior citizen AND KYC non-compliant AND circle = Mumbai) return plausible sub-sets.
- LLM prompt tuning and UI demos have representative data to work with.

All values are **synthetic** — no real customer data is used.

---

## Quick start

Run from the **project root**:

```bash
# Wipe existing rows and insert 2,000 fresh rows (recommended first run)
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset

# Append another 1,000 rows to existing data
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 1000

# Insert 5,000 rows with a fixed seed for reproducibility
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --count 5000 --reset --seed 99
```

The script prints a summary on completion:

```
=======================================================
  Database : ...data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db
  Inserted : 2,000 rows
  Total    : 2,000 rows in COMP_TBL
=======================================================
  Senior citizens (SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG=1) : 759
  KYC non-compliant (KYC_FLG='N')       : 267

  Top 5 circles by row count:
    MUMBAI METRO            306
    BENGALURU               216
    ...
=======================================================
```

---

## CLI reference

| Flag | Type | Default | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| `--count` | `int` | `2000` | Number of rows to insert |
| `--reset` | flag | off | Delete **all** existing rows before inserting |
| `--db` | `Path` | `data/comp_db/COMP_REPORTS.db` | Path to the target SQLite file |
| `--seed` | `int` | `42` | Random seed — same seed produces identical data |

---

## What the script generates

### Generation rules per column

| Column | Rule |
|---|---|
| `BRCD` | Random 3-digit branch code `001`–`999` |
| `CUST_NBR` | Sequential `C000001`, `C000002`, … (no collisions on append) |
| `ACCT_NBR` | Sequential `A000001`, `A000002`, … |
| `CUST_NBR_CHKDGT` / `ACCT_NBR_CHKDGT` | Random digit `1`–`9` |
| `ACCT_BRNCH` | Random `M001`–`M099` |
| `AGE_YRS` | Uniform random `18`–`85` |
| `SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG` | `1` if `AGE_YRS >= 60`, else `0` |
| `BRTH_DT` | Derived from age — random date within the correct birth year |
| `KYC_FLG` | `Y` with probability **80%** for seniors, **90%** for others |
| `END_OF_DAY_BAL` | Log-normal distribution (mean ≈ ₹98k, range ₹10k–₹50L) |
| `ACCT_OPN_DT` | Random date `2005-01-01` – `2024-06-30` |
| `tenor_yr` | Randomly from `{1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 10, 15}` years |
| `tenor_day` | Uniform random `0`–`364` additional days |
| `MTRTY_DT` | `ACCT_OPN_DT + tenor` — shifted ±30 days if `ISMOD = YES` |
| `MAT_DT` | `ACCT_OPN_DT + tenor` (unmodified maturity) |
| `NEW_EFFCTV_RT` | Base rate ± 0.75% — base is `7.0%` for seniors, `6.5%` for others |
| `EXPECTED_EFFCTV_RT` | Same base + 0.25% ± 0.75% |
| `PRDCT_ID` / `PRODUCT_NAME` | Random choice from 11 products |
| `BRANCHNAME` | Picked from the branch list for the selected circle code |
| `CIRCLENAME` / `CIRCLECD` | Random choice from 20 circle/code pairs across 13 cities |
| `PF_FAC_REPT` | `Y` with probability **30%** |
| `ISMOD` | `YES` with probability **15%** |

### Reference data pools

#### Circles (20 entries across 13 cities)

| City | Codes |
|---|---|
| Mumbai Metro | `MM01`, `MM02`, `MM03` |
| Delhi | `DL01`, `DL02` |
| Lucknow | `LK01`, `LK02` |
| Chennai | `CH01`, `CH02` |
| Kolkata | `KO01`, `KO02` |
| Hyderabad | `HY01` |
| Bengaluru | `BN01`, `BN02` |
| Ahmedabad | `AH01` |
| Pune | `PN01` |
| Jaipur | `JP01` |
| Bhopal | `BP01` |
| Chandigarh | `CG01` |
| Patna | `PT01` |

#### Products (11 entries)

| Code | Product name |
|---|---|
| `P100` | PUB IND UNI 181D-10YRS |
| `P101` | TDTDR PUB IND UNI-91-179D-INR |
| `P102` | SENIOR TERM DEPOSIT |
| `P103` | PUB IND REINVESTMENT 1-2YRS |
| `P104` | TDTDR REINVESTMENT 2-5YRS |
| `P105` | RECURRING DEPOSIT SCHEME |
| `P106` | FLEXI DEPOSIT PLAN |
| `P107` | TAX SAVER FIXED DEPOSIT |
| `P108` | SUPER SENIOR DEPOSIT |
| `P109` | SHORT TERM DEPOSIT 7-30D |
| `P110` | MEDIUM TERM DEPOSIT 180D-1YR |

---

## Sample queries to verify the data

Paste these into the chat UI or API to confirm the seeded data is working end-to-end with the NL2SQL pipeline:

```
How many senior citizen accounts are there?
```
→ Expected: ~1,518

```
Show all accounts in Mumbai Metro circle where KYC flag is N
```
→ Expected: ~80–90 rows

```
What is the average balance of senior citizens with KYC flag Y?
```
→ Expected: a value in the ₹1,50,000–₹2,50,000 range

```
How many accounts have a TAX SAVER FIXED DEPOSIT product?
```
→ Expected: ~398

```
Show top 5 circles by number of modified records
```
→ Expected: a GROUP BY result across the 13 circles

```
List accounts where balance is more than 1000000 and KYC flag is N
```
→ Expected: a small subset — high-balance non-compliant accounts

---

## Reproducibility

The default `--seed 42` produces the same data every time. To regenerate the exact current 2,000-row dataset:

```bash
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000 --seed 42
```

To generate a different but still deterministic dataset:

```bash
python data/seed_synthetic_data.py --reset --count 2000 --seed 123
```

---

## Dependencies

The script uses **only the Python standard library**:

| Module | Usage |
|---|---|
| `sqlite3` | Database connection and bulk insert |
| `random` | Random sampling and log-normal balance generation |
| `datetime` | Date arithmetic for DOB, account open, and maturity dates |
| `pathlib` | Cross-platform file path handling |
| `argparse` | CLI argument parsing |

No `pip install` required.

# Compliance Chatbot — Testing Guide

This document covers how to run the test suite, what to test, how to write agent-node tests, and CI integration.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [Prerequisites](#prerequisites)
2. [Running Tests](#running-tests)
3. [Test Configuration](#test-configuration)
4. [What to Test](#what-to-test)
5. [Agent Node Test Patterns](#agent-node-test-patterns)
6. [Code Coverage](#code-coverage)
7. [Test Design Principles](#test-design-principles)
8. [Writing New Tests](#writing-new-tests)
9. [CI Integration](#ci-integration)

---

## Prerequisites

All test dependencies are included in `requirements.txt`:

```
pytest>=8.2.0
pytest-asyncio>=0.23.0
pytest-cov>=5.0.0
httpx>=0.27.0
```

Install with:

```bash
pip install -r requirements.txt
```

---

## Running Tests

Run from the project root directory.

### Run all tests

```bash
pytest
```

### Run with verbose output

```bash
pytest -v
```

### Run a specific test file

```bash
pytest tests/test_intent_agent.py -v
```

### Run a specific test class or function

```bash
pytest tests/test_sql_review_agent.py::TestSQLReviewAgent::test_blocks_insert -v
```

### Run with full traceback

```bash
pytest --tb=long
```

---

## Test Configuration

`pyproject.toml` configures pytest as follows:

```toml
[tool.pytest.ini_options]
testpaths = ["tests"]
addopts = "--strict-markers -v --tb=short"
asyncio_mode = "auto"
```

| Option | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `testpaths = ["tests"]` | Only look for tests in `tests/` |
| `--strict-markers` | Error on unrecognised pytest marks |
| `-v` | Verbose output by default |
| `--tb=short` | Short tracebacks on failure |
| `asyncio_mode = "auto"` | Async test functions run without `@pytest.mark.asyncio` |

---

## What to Test

The LangGraph pipeline is composed of six independent agent node classes, each with a single `.run(state) -> AgentState` method. This makes them straightforward to unit test in isolation.

| Module | What to test |
|---|---|
| `src/nl2sql_agents/intent.py` | Clear intent → `intent_ok=True`; vague/ambiguous → `intent_ok=False`; empty question; normalisation |
| `src/nl2sql_agents/schema.py` | DB selection scoring; schema Markdown output; missing db_path fallback |
| `src/nl2sql_agents/sql_writer_agent.py` | LLM called for all questions (mocked); SQL extracted correctly; empty LLM output triggers extraction_failed stage; retry feedback included in prompt |
| `src/nl2sql_agents/sql_review.py` | DML keyword blocking; missing SELECT; missing FROM; unknown tables; retry counter; max retries |
| `src/nl2sql_agents/sql_validator.py` | Valid SELECT passes; malformed SQL fails; INSERT blocked at AST level; retry counter; SQL canonicalisation; dialect round-trip |
| `src/nl2sql_agents/executor.py` | Successful execution returns rows; empty result; SQL error; missing db_path; rollback on error |
| `src/nl2sql_agents/graph.py` | End-to-end graph invoke with mocked LLM; clarification branch; retry branch; sqlglot validation branch |
| `src/business_logic/compliance_processor.py` | Maps `AgentState` to response models correctly; empty question; graph error handling |
| `src/services/model_loader_service.py` | DLH/ICA client init; `generate()`/`extract_sql()` with mocked HTTP |
| `src/main.py` (FastAPI) | `/health` endpoint; `/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate` with mocked processor |

---

## Agent Node Test Patterns

Each agent node is a plain Python class. No special test setup is required.

### IntentAgent

```python
from src.nl2sql_agents.intent_agent import IntentAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


class TestIntentAgent:

    def setup_method(self):
        self.agent = IntentAgent()

    def test_clear_question_sets_intent_ok(self):
        state = AgentState(question="Show accounts where KYC flag is N", stages=[])
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["intent_ok"] is True
        assert result["clarification_needed"] is None

    def test_vague_question_requests_clarification(self):
        state = AgentState(question="show", stages=[])
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["intent_ok"] is False
        assert result["clarification_needed"] is not None

    def test_empty_question_requests_clarification(self):
        state = AgentState(question="", stages=[])
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["intent_ok"] is False
```

### SQLWriterAgent (mocked LLM)

```python
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_writer_agent import SQLWriterAgent  # only LLM node
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


class TestSQLWriterAgent:

    def setup_method(self):
        self.mock_llm = MagicMock()
        self.mock_llm.generate.return_value = (
            "<begin_sql_response>SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';<end_sql_response>"
        )
        self.mock_llm.extract_sql.return_value = "SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';"
        self.agent = SQLWriterAgent(model_service=self.mock_llm)

    def test_llm_called_for_every_question(self):
        state = AgentState(
            question="How many accounts in Mumbai circle?",
            interpreted_question="How many accounts in Mumbai circle?",
            schema_context="## Database: COMP_REPORTS\n### Table: COMP_TBL",
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["sql_query"] == "SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';"
        self.mock_llm.generate.assert_called_once()

    def test_llm_called_for_complex_question(self):
        state = AgentState(
            question="Show KYC non-compliant accounts",
            interpreted_question="Show KYC non-compliant accounts?",
            schema_context="## Database: COMP_REPORTS\n### Table: COMP_TBL",
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["sql_query"] == "SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';"
        self.mock_llm.generate.assert_called_once()

    def test_empty_llm_output_returns_extraction_failed_stage(self):
        self.mock_llm.extract_sql.return_value = ""
        state = AgentState(
            question="Show all accounts",
            interpreted_question="Show all accounts",
            schema_context="## Database: COMP_REPORTS\n### Table: COMP_TBL",
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["sql_query"] == ""
        assert "sql_writer_agent:extraction_failed" in result["stages"]
```

### SQLReviewAgent

```python
import sqlite3
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_review_agent import SQLReviewAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


class TestSQLReviewAgent:

    def setup_method(self):
        self.agent = SQLReviewAgent(max_retries=3)
        # Create a temp DB with COMP_TBL for table-existence checks
        tmp = tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".db", delete=False)
        self.db_path = Path(tmp.name)
        conn = sqlite3.connect(str(self.db_path))
        conn.execute("CREATE TABLE COMP_TBL (KYC_FLG TEXT)")
        conn.commit()
        conn.close()

    def test_valid_select_passes(self):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';",
            db_path=str(self.db_path),
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["sql_review_passed"] is True

    def test_insert_is_blocked(self):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="INSERT INTO COMP_TBL VALUES ('N');",
            db_path=str(self.db_path),
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["sql_review_passed"] is False
        assert "INSERT" in result["review_feedback"].upper()
```

### SQLValidatorAgent

```python
# tests/test_sql_validator_agent.py
import pytest
from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_validator_agent import SQLValidatorAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


@pytest.fixture()
def agent() -> SQLValidatorAgent:
    return SQLValidatorAgent(dialect="sqlite")


class TestSQLValidatorAgent:

    def test_valid_select_passes(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT COUNT(*) AS total FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is True
        assert result.get("sqlglot_error") is None

    def test_malformed_sql_fails(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE (KYC_FLG = 'N'",  # unclosed paren
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is False
        assert result["sql_review_passed"] is False
        assert "sqlglot" in result["review_feedback"].lower()

    def test_insert_blocked_at_ast_level(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="INSERT INTO COMP_TBL VALUES ('N', 60);",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is False
        assert "INSERT" in result["review_feedback"].upper()

    def test_retry_count_incremented_on_failure(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="NOT VALID SQL AT ALL !!!",
            retry_count=1,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["retry_count"] == 2

    def test_sql_canonicalised_on_success(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="select   count(*)  from COMP_TBL",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["sqlglot_valid"] is True
        assert result["sql_query"].strip() != ""

    def test_stage_string_on_pass(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL;",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert any("sql_validator_agent:passed" in s for s in result["stages"])

    def test_stage_string_on_failure(self, agent):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="DELETE FROM COMP_TBL;",
            retry_count=0,
            stages=[],
        )
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert any("sql_validator_agent:" in s for s in result["stages"])
```

### ExecutorAgent (with real temp SQLite)

```python
import sqlite3
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
from src.nl2sql_agents.executor_agent import ExecutorAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


class TestExecutorAgent:

    def setup_method(self):
        self.agent = ExecutorAgent()
        tmp = tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(suffix=".db", delete=False)
        self.db_path = Path(tmp.name)
        conn = sqlite3.connect(str(self.db_path))
        conn.execute("CREATE TABLE COMP_TBL (KYC_FLG TEXT, AGE_YRS INTEGER)")
        conn.execute("INSERT INTO COMP_TBL VALUES ('N', 64)")
        conn.commit()
        conn.close()

    def test_returns_rows(self):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='N';",
            db_path=str(self.db_path),
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert len(result["result_rows"]) == 1
        assert result["result_rows"][0]["KYC_FLG"] == "N"
        assert result["result_columns"] == ["KYC_FLG", "AGE_YRS"]
        assert result.get("error") is None

    def test_empty_result(self):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG='Y';",
            db_path=str(self.db_path),
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["result_rows"] == []
        assert result["result_columns"] == []
        assert "no matching records" in result["result_summary"].lower()

    def test_scalar_summary(self):
        state = AgentState(
            sql_query="SELECT COUNT(*) AS total FROM COMP_TBL;",
            db_path=str(self.db_path),
            stages=[],
        )
        result = self.agent.run(state)
        assert result["result_columns"] == ["total"]
        assert "Result:" in result["result_summary"]
```

---

## Code Coverage

### Terminal report (recommended for development)

```bash
pytest --cov=src --cov-report=term-missing
```

### HTML report

```bash
pytest --cov=src --cov-report=html
# Open htmlcov/index.html in your browser
```

### XML report (for CI)

```bash
pytest --cov=src --cov-report=xml
```

### Coverage targets

| Module | Target |
|---|---|
| `src/nl2sql_agents/` | ≥ 90% |
| `src/business_logic/` | ≥ 90% |
| `src/services/` | ≥ 85% |
| `src/models/` | ≥ 95% |
| `src/exceptions/` | ≥ 100% |
| `src/utils/` | ≥ 80% |

---

## Test Design Principles

1. **No real LLM calls** — `ModelLoaderService` must always be mocked; tests must not require a running DLH or ICA endpoint.
2. **No real network calls** — FastAPI layer tests use `httpx.AsyncClient` with ASGI transport.
3. **No side effects on `COMP_REPORTS.db`** — agent tests that need a real database use a `tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile`.
4. **Each agent node is tested in isolation** — no need to run the full graph for unit tests.
5. **One assertion concept per test** — each test verifies one specific behaviour.
6. **Fixtures over setup/teardown** — reusable fixtures at module level in each test file.

---

## Writing New Tests

### Unit test template (agent node)

```python
# tests/test_my_agent.py
import pytest
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from src.nl2sql_agents.my_agent import MyAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState


@pytest.fixture()
def agent() -> MyAgent:
    return MyAgent()


class TestMyAgent:

    def test_happy_path(self, agent: MyAgent) -> None:
        state = AgentState(question="Show KYC=N accounts", stages=[])
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result["some_key"] == "expected_value"

    def test_handles_empty_input(self, agent: MyAgent) -> None:
        state = AgentState(question="", stages=[])
        result = agent.run(state)
        assert result.get("error") is not None or result.get("clarification_needed") is not None
```

### FastAPI integration test template

```python
# tests/test_api.py
import pytest
from httpx import AsyncClient, ASGITransport
from src.main import app


async def test_health_endpoint() -> None:
    async with AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app), base_url="http://test") as client:
        response = await client.get("/health")
    assert response.status_code == 200
    assert response.json()["status"] == "ok"
    assert response.json()["version"] == "2.0.0"
```


The `--cov-fail-under=80` flag causes the CI step to fail if overall coverage drops below 80%.
