<!-- ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 11 — FEATURE GUIDE
     ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

# Part 11 · Feature Guide

## 1. Provider Configuration

Saving a new configuration via **Test & Save** or `POST /api/v1/config/llm` **rebuilds the entire LangGraph pipeline** using the new `ModelLoaderService` instance.

- **DLH** — OpenAI SDK with custom `base_url`; always sends `temperature` + `max_tokens`; TLS disabled by default.
- **ICA** — OpenAI SDK with `{ica_base_url}/ica/v1/{namespace}/`; `Authorization: Bearer <key>`; does **not** send temperature by default.

---

## 2. Pure-LLM SQL Generation (SQLWriterAgent)

`sql_writer_agent.py` is the **only node that calls the LLM**. There is no rule-based fast path — every question goes through the LLM:

1. Builds a structured few-shot prompt with the live schema context from SchemaAgent
2. Calls `model_service.generate(prompt)`
3. Extracts SQL from `<begin_sql_response>…<end_sql_response>` tags via `extract_sql()`
4. If extraction fails (tags missing), sets stage `sql_writer_agent:extraction_failed` and returns empty SQL

Stage trace for a normal call: `sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry=0`.

---

## 3. Read-Only SQL Enforcement — Three Layers

### Layer 0 — IntentAgent (before any LLM call)

`intent.py` Check 2 scans the **user's raw question** for disallowed action words:
```
delete  drop  insert  update  truncate  alter  create  replace
pragma  attach  detach  vacuum  modify  remove  erase  wipe  purge  destroy
```
If found: `intent_ok=False` → pipeline halts immediately; UI shows a **red blocked card** naming the keyword.

### Layer 1 — SQLReviewAgent (LLM-generated SQL)

`sql_review.py` catches disallowed keywords in the **LLM-generated SQL** (whole-word, case-insensitive):
```
INSERT  UPDATE  DELETE  DROP  TRUNCATE  ALTER  CREATE  REPLACE  PRAGMA  ATTACH  DETACH  VACUUM
```
Sets `sql_review_passed=False`, writes user-facing `error` (shown as red card in UI), increments retry counter. After 3 failed retries, surfaces the error.

### Layer 2 — ExecutorAgent (at execution)

```python
conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")
cursor.execute(sql_query)
# ... fetchmany(500) ...
conn.rollback()   # always — even on success
conn.close()
```

---

## 4. IntentAgent — Four-Check Pipeline

Node 1 (`intent.py`) runs four ordered checks before any schema read or LLM call:

| Check | Trigger | Stage string |
|---|---|---|
| 1 — Empty question | `question == ""` | `intent_agent:empty` |
| 2 — Disallowed action word | Whole-token match in `_DISALLOWED_ACTION_WORDS` | `intent_agent:disallowed_action_word=<KW>` |
| 3 — Too short / meaningless | `meaningful_tokens < 3` and no domain metric term | `intent_agent:too_short` |
| 4 — Vague / ambiguous | Pure vague phrase **or** (ambiguous pronoun + no metric term + **≤3 meaningful tokens**) | `intent_agent:clarification_required` |

**Key rule for Check 4:** The ambiguous-pronoun sub-check is **skipped when `len(meaningful_tokens) > 3`**. Rich, multi-word questions (e.g., `"How many people from kolkata branch having more than 70000?"`) always pass to the LLM regardless of pronoun presence.

---

## 5. Clarification Flow

When IntentAgent detects a vague or ambiguous question (Check 3 or Check 4):
1. Sets `intent_ok = False`
2. Sets `clarification_needed` to a friendly prompt with example questions
3. Graph routes to `ClarifyNode` (terminal) — no schema read, no SQL, no execution
4. Response returns `needs_clarification: true` + `clarification_question`
5. UI displays a **blue clarification card** with bullet-point examples

---

## 6. Logging

```
2026-08-05 23:46:10 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.intent_agent | intent:188 | IntentAgent — analysing question: '...'
2026-08-05 23:46:10 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.intent_agent | intent:281 | IntentAgent — intent resolved: '...'
2026-08-05 23:46:10 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.schema_agent | schema:73  | SchemaAgent — selected db=COMP_REPORTS.db
2026-08-05 23:46:10 | INFO  | compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.sql_writer_agent | sql_writer_agent:86 | SQLWriterAgent — generating SQL (retry=0) for: '...'
```

`LOG_LEVEL=DEBUG` in `.env` for verbose output.

---

<!-- ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 12 — ADDING A NEW DATABASE
     ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

# Part 12 · Adding a New Database

The Schema Agent auto-discovers any `.db` file placed under `data/` on the next request. No restart required.

## Step-by-Step

### Step 1 — Place the `.db` file

```
data/
├── comp_db/
│   └── COMP_REPORTS.db       ← existing
└── loan_db/
    └── LOAN_REPORTS.db       ← new
```

Use descriptive folder and file names — they are used as routing keywords.

### Step 2 — (Optional) Add a schema reference file

```
data/loan_db/db_details.txt
```

Include: database purpose, table names, column descriptions, example questions.

### Step 3 — (Optional) Add schema metadata to `compliance_schema.py`

```python
"LOAN_TBL": {
    "TABLE_DESCRIPTION": "...",
    "COLUMNS": {
        "LOAN_ID": "Unique loan identifier",
        "BORROWER_NAME": "Name of the borrower",
    },
    "FEW_SHOT_EXAMPLES": [
        {"question": "Show all active loans", "sql": "SELECT * FROM LOAN_TBL WHERE STATUS = 'ACTIVE';"},
    ],
}
```

### Step 4 — Test routing

```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:8000/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"question": "Show all active loans"}'
```

The `stages` field will include `schema_agent:db=LOAN_REPORTS.db`.

---

## Checklist

- [ ] `.db` file placed under `data/<folder>/`
- [ ] `db_details.txt` schema reference created (optional but recommended)
- [ ] Schema metadata entry added in `src/models/compliance_schema.py` (if needed)
- [ ] End-to-end routing tested with a sample question
- [ ] `stages` confirms `schema_agent:db=<DB_NAME>.db`

---

<!-- ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 13 — MULTI-DATABASE ROUTING
     ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

# Part 13 · Multi-Database Routing

## How It Works

On every chat request, `SchemaAgent.run()`:

1. Calls `pathlib.Path.glob("**/*.db")` on `data_root`
2. Opens each `.db` in read-only mode; reads all table and column names via `PRAGMA table_info`
3. Scores each database against the question tokens
4. Writes the highest-scoring `db_path` into `AgentState`
5. Builds a live Markdown schema context injected into the SQL Writer prompt

> No static metadata cache — schema introspection is always live.

---

## Scoring Algorithm

```
score(db, question) = Σ keyword_matches(question_tokens, db_keywords)
```

| Match source | Weight | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Database filename stem | +1 per token match | `comp` matches `comp_reports.db` |
| Table name (full token) | +2 per match | `comp_tbl` in question gets +2 |
| Column name (full token) | +1 per match | `age_yrs` in question gets +1 |

On a tie, the first candidate (alphabetically by path) wins. If all scores are zero, falls back to the configured default.

---

## Example Routing

Question: `"How many senior citizens are in the Mumbai circle?"`

| Database | Keyword matches | Score |
|---|---|---|
| `COMP_REPORTS` | `circlename` (circle ✓), `sr_ctzn_acct_flg` (senior ✓, citizens ✓) | 3 |
| `LOAN_REPORTS` | none | 0 |

→ `COMP_REPORTS` selected.

---

## Common Routing Rules

| Question keywords | Database selected |
|---|---|
| "senior citizen", "term deposit", "Mumbai circle" | `COMP_REPORTS` |
| "loan repayment", "EMI", "borrower" | `LOAN_REPORTS` |
| "KYC status", "AML", "sanctions" | `KYC_REPORTS` |

---

<!-- ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 14 — DLH & DATABASE NOTES
     ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

# Part 14 · DLH & Database Notes

## LLM Providers

`ModelLoaderService` manages the provider lifecycle. Only remote providers are supported — no local model inference (`torch`, `transformers`, HuggingFace`).

The LLM is used **only** by the SQL Writer Agent (Node 3 — `sql_writer_agent.py`). Nodes 1 (`intent.py`), 2 (`schema.py`), 4 (`sql_review.py`), 4.5 (`sql_validator.py`), and 5 (`executor.py`) are fully deterministic — no LLM calls.

---

## DLH (on-prem)

```python
OpenAI(
    api_key=settings.dlh_api_key,    # typically "EMPTY"
    base_url=settings.dlh_base_url,   # e.g. http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1
    timeout=30.0,
    http_client=httpx.Client(verify=False),  # when dlh_verify_ssl=false
)
```

- Always sends `temperature` and `max_tokens`
- TLS disabled by default (self-signed certs on internal networks)

**Minimum `.env`:**
```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=dlh
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=google/gemma-4-31b-it
MODEL__DLH_BASE_URL=http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1
MODEL__DLH_API_KEY=EMPTY
MODEL__DLH_VERIFY_SSL=false
```

---

## ICA (IBM Consulting Advantage)

Base URL constructed as:
```
{ica_base_url}/ica/v1/{namespace}/
```

Sets `Authorization: Bearer <ica_api_key>`. Does **not** send `temperature`/`max_tokens` by default.

**Minimum `.env`:**
```env
MODEL__PROVIDER=ica
MODEL__MODEL_NAME=your-resource-id
MODEL__ICA_BASE_URL=https://api.nextgen-beta.ica.ibm.com
MODEL__ICA_API_KEY=your-developer-key
MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE=chat-models
MODEL__ICA_SEND_TEMPERATURE=false
```

---

## SQL Delimiters

The LLM is prompted to wrap SQL between:
```
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT ...
<end_sql_response>
```

`ModelLoaderService.extract_sql()` uses a regex to extract SQL between these tags. If tags are missing, a `SqlGenerationError` is raised.

---

## Common Error Messages

| Error | Cause | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| `HTTP 503 — Compliance processor is not initialised` | Provider failed to connect | Use `POST /api/v1/config/llm` to reconnect |
| `ModelLoadError: ICA API key is required` | `MODEL__ICA_API_KEY` is empty | Set the key in `.env` |
| `ModelLoadError: Unknown ICA namespace` | Typo in `MODEL__ICA_NAMESPACE` | Use `chat-models`, `agents`, `assistants`, or `digital-workforce` |
| `SqlGenerationError: SQL delimiter tags not found` | LLM did not wrap SQL in tags | Lower temperature; try a different model |
| `DatabaseConnectionError` | `data/comp_db/` directory missing | `mkdir -p data/comp_db` then restart |
| `sql_review_agent:fail` in stages | SQL Writer failed 3× | Rephrase the question or check LLM temperature |

---

<!-- ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
     SECTION 15 — SQLGLOT VALIDATION
     ═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════ -->

# Part 15 · sqlglot Validation

> **Node 4.5** — Deep AST-level SQL validation before any query reaches the SQLite executor.

## Why sqlglot?

| Check | SQLReviewAgent (regex) | SQLValidatorAgent (sqlglot AST) |
|---|---|---|
| Block INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE / DROP | ✓ Keyword match | ✓ AST statement type |
| Require `SELECT` prefix | ✓ String startswith | ✓ AST node type |
| Structurally malformed SQL | ✗ Not detectable | ✓ `ParseError` |
| Wrong dialect constructs | ✗ Not detectable | ✓ Dialect-aware transpilation |
| SQL normalisation / canonicalisation | ✗ | ✓ Round-trips to SQLite dialect |

---

## Pipeline Position

```
SQLWriterAgent (Node 3) ◄─────────────────────────┐
   │                                               │ retry
   ▼                                               │
SQLReviewAgent (Node 4)                            │
   │ review passed                                 │
   ▼                                               │
SQLValidatorAgent (Node 4.5)  ← sqlglot AST        │
   │ sqlglot_valid=False ──────────────────────────┘
   │ sqlglot_valid=True
   ▼
ExecutorAgent (Node 5)
```

---

## Three Validation Steps

### Step 1 — Parse

```python
parsed = sqlglot.parse(sql, dialect="sqlite", error_level=ErrorLevel.RAISE)
```

Catches: unbalanced parentheses, incomplete expressions, invalid token sequences.

### Step 2 — Statement Type Check

```python
stmt_type = type(parsed[0]).__name__.lower()  # "select", "insert", "update", …
assert stmt_type == "select"
```

Independent of regex — blocks any non-SELECT at AST level.

### Step 3 — SQLite Dialect Round-Trip

```python
canonical = stmt.sql(dialect="sqlite", pretty=False)
```

Normalises:
- Quoting (backticks → double quotes or no quotes)
- Function names (`ILIKE` → `LIKE`)
- `LIMIT x, y` → `LIMIT y OFFSET x`

If the canonical form differs from the input, `sql_query` in the state is silently updated.

---

## AgentState Fields

| Field | Type | Written by | Read by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sqlglot_valid` | `bool` | `SQLValidatorAgent` | Graph router |
| `sqlglot_error` | `Optional[str]` | `SQLValidatorAgent` | UI |

On failure also writes: `sql_review_passed = False`, `review_feedback`, `retry_count += 1`.

---

## Stage Strings

| Stage string | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `sql_validator_agent:passed dialect=sqlite` | All checks passed |
| `sql_validator_agent:parse_error` | sqlglot could not build an AST |
| `sql_validator_agent:empty_ast` | Parser returned no statements |
| `sql_validator_agent:disallowed_stmt_type=<type>` | Non-SELECT statement detected |
| `sql_validator_agent:skipped_no_sqlglot` | sqlglot not installed; check skipped |

---

## Graceful Degradation

If `sqlglot` is not installed, the agent logs a warning and marks `sqlglot_valid=True` with stage `sql_validator_agent:skipped_no_sqlglot`. The pipeline continues — the regex-based `SQLReviewAgent` remains the only guard.

```bash
pip install sqlglot>=25.0.0
```

---

## Configuration

```python
compiled_graph = build_nl2sql_graph(
    model_service=model_service,
    db_path=db_path,
    max_review_retries=3,
    sqlglot_dialect="sqlite",   # new parameter
)
```

---

## Dependency

| Package | Minimum version | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `sqlglot` | `>=25.0.0` | Pure-Python SQL parser and transpiler |

sqlglot has **zero runtime dependencies** — pure-Python wheel, no native libraries.

---

## References

- [sqlglot GitHub](https://github.com/tobymao/sqlglot)
- [sqlglot documentation](https://sqlglot.com/sqlglot.html)
- [sqlglot SQLite dialect](https://sqlglot.com/sqlglot/dialects/sqlite.html)

---

*Compliance Chatbot — SBI AI Team · All 15 documentation files merged in canonical order.*



####################################################################################################
FILE: extra\app.py
####################################################################################################

"""
app.py  —  Compliance Chatbot  (root-level Streamlit entry-point)
=================================================================

Run from the project root:
    streamlit run app.py

What this file does, top-to-bottom
-----------------------------------
1.  Page config  — must be the VERY FIRST Streamlit call in the script.
2.  Font CSS     — builds @font-face rules that load Urbanist from the local
                   assets/fonts/ directory via Streamlit's static file server.
                   (.streamlit/config.toml  enableStaticServing = true  is
                   required — already set up in this project.)
3.  CSS injection — all other visual styling (sidebar, cards, pills, buttons).
4.  Constants    — backend URL, config file path, default LLM settings.
5.  State helpers — load/save JSON config to disk; initialise session_state.
6.  API helpers  — thin wrappers around requests.post() for the two endpoints.
7.  Sidebar      — navigation radio (LLM Configuration | Chat).
8.  Page header  — title + subtitle card.
9.  render_config_page()  — two-column LLM config form + saved-config viewer.
10. format_stages()       — stage list → coloured pill HTML.
    KEY: sql_validator_agent pills are highlighted green/amber/red so the
    sqlglot validation result is immediately visible in the chat.
11. render_chat_message() — one message with Stages, SQL, summary, results.
12. build_transcript()    — serialise chat to plain text for Export.
13. render_chat_page()    — message loop + chat input + action buttons.
14. Router               — calls the correct page render function.

How Urbanist fonts are loaded (important)
------------------------------------------
Streamlit's st.markdown() sanitiser strips @font-face rules that contain
large base64 data-URIs.  The only reliable solution is Streamlit's built-in
static file server (available since v1.31):

  1. .streamlit/config.toml  →  enableStaticServing = true
  2. Font files copied to    →  .streamlit/static/fonts/*.ttf
  3. CSS references them as  →  /app/static/fonts/Urbanist-Regular.ttf

The _font_face_css() function below builds the @font-face rules using those
/app/static/… URLs.  This works in local dev, Docker, and CML deployments
as long as the app is served by Streamlit's own HTTP server.

sqlglot stage pill highlight rules (see format_stages below)
-------------------------------------------------------------
Stage string contains …          →  pill colour
"sql_validator_agent:passed"     →  green  (validation passed ✓)
"sql_validator_agent:skipped"    →  amber  (sqlglot not installed, skipped)
any other "sql_validator_agent…" →  red    (parse error / disallowed stmt)
all other stages                 →  default grey
"""

import json
import os
import shutil
from pathlib import Path

import requests
import streamlit as st


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 1.  PAGE CONFIG
#     Must be the very first Streamlit call — Streamlit raises an error if any
#     other st.* function is called before set_page_config().
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

st.set_page_config(
    page_title="Compliance Chatbot",
    page_icon="CB",
    layout="wide",
    initial_sidebar_state="expanded",
)


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 2.  CONSTANTS
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# Persisted LLM configuration file  (lives inside .streamlit/ — git-ignored)
CONFIG_PATH = Path(".streamlit/compliance_bot_config.json")
CONFIG_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)   # auto-create dir on first run

# Backend base URL — override via env-var without touching source code
# Example:  CB_BACKEND_URL=http://10.1.2.3:8000 streamlit run app.py
API_BASE_URL = os.getenv("CB_BACKEND_URL", os.getenv("API_BASE_URL", "http://localhost:8000"))

# Source font directory (master copies)
FONTS_SRC_DIR = Path("assets/fonts")

# Streamlit static directory — files here are served at /app/static/<name>
# This is the ONLY approach that survives Streamlit's HTML sanitiser.
# Requires  enableStaticServing = true  in .streamlit/config.toml  (already set).
FONTS_STATIC_DIR = Path(".streamlit/static/fonts")


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 2a. FONT SETUP
#
#     HOW FONTS WORK IN STREAMLIT (important background)
#     ---------------------------------------------------
#     Streamlit's st.markdown(unsafe_allow_html=True) passes HTML through a
#     DOMPurify-based sanitiser.  Since Streamlit 1.27 that sanitiser strips
#     @font-face rules whose src contains large base64 data-URIs — exactly
#     what we had before.  The result: @font-face rules silently disappear
#     from the injected <style> block and the browser falls back to the
#     system font.
#
#     The correct solution (since Streamlit 1.31) is the built-in static file
#     server.  With  enableStaticServing = true  in .streamlit/config.toml,
#     any file placed in  .streamlit/static/  is served at:
#
#         /app/static/<relative-path-inside-static/>
#
#     So  .streamlit/static/fonts/Urbanist-Regular.ttf  becomes:
#
#         /app/static/fonts/Urbanist-Regular.ttf
#
#     That URL can be used inside a normal @font-face src without any
#     base64 encoding — the browser fetches the TTF directly.
#
#     _ensure_fonts_static() copies the TTF files from assets/fonts/ into
#     .streamlit/static/fonts/ on every startup so the two directories stay
#     in sync even if the user reinstalls or cleans the repo.
#
#     _font_face_css() then builds the @font-face CSS block using those
#     /app/static/fonts/ URLs.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# All 18 Urbanist weight/style variants
_FONT_FILES: list[tuple[str, str, str]] = [
    # (CSS font-weight, CSS font-style, filename in assets/fonts/)
    ("100", "normal", "Urbanist-Thin.ttf"),
    ("100", "italic", "Urbanist-ThinItalic.ttf"),
    ("200", "normal", "Urbanist-ExtraLight.ttf"),
    ("200", "italic", "Urbanist-ExtraLightItalic.ttf"),
    ("300", "normal", "Urbanist-Light.ttf"),
    ("300", "italic", "Urbanist-LightItalic.ttf"),
    ("400", "normal", "Urbanist-Regular.ttf"),
    ("400", "italic", "Urbanist-Italic.ttf"),
    ("500", "normal", "Urbanist-Medium.ttf"),
    ("500", "italic", "Urbanist-MediumItalic.ttf"),
    ("600", "normal", "Urbanist-SemiBold.ttf"),
    ("600", "italic", "Urbanist-SemiBoldItalic.ttf"),
    ("700", "normal", "Urbanist-Bold.ttf"),
    ("700", "italic", "Urbanist-BoldItalic.ttf"),
    ("800", "normal", "Urbanist-ExtraBold.ttf"),
    ("800", "italic", "Urbanist-ExtraBoldItalic.ttf"),
    ("900", "normal", "Urbanist-Black.ttf"),
    ("900", "italic", "Urbanist-BlackItalic.ttf"),
]


def _ensure_fonts_static() -> int:
    """
    Copy all Urbanist TTF files from assets/fonts/ into .streamlit/static/fonts/
    so Streamlit's built-in static server can serve them at /app/static/fonts/*.

    This function is called once at startup.  It is idempotent — it only copies
    a file if the destination is missing or smaller than the source (i.e. the
    source was updated).

    Returns:
        Number of files copied (0 means all were already up-to-date).
    """
    FONTS_STATIC_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    copied = 0
    for _weight, _style, fname in _FONT_FILES:
        src  = FONTS_SRC_DIR / fname
        dest = FONTS_STATIC_DIR / fname
        if not src.exists():
            continue   # source file missing — skip gracefully
        # Copy if dest is missing or stale (smaller than src)
        if not dest.exists() or dest.stat().st_size != src.stat().st_size:
            shutil.copy2(src, dest)
            copied += 1
    return copied


def _font_face_css() -> str:
    """
    Build CSS @font-face rules for all 18 Urbanist weights/styles.

    Each rule references the font via its Streamlit static URL
    (/app/static/fonts/<filename>) — a normal HTTP URL that the browser can
    fetch without any base64 encoding.

    This approach works because .streamlit/config.toml has:
        [server]
        enableStaticServing = true

    Files missing from .streamlit/static/fonts/ are silently skipped;
    the browser falls back to the next font in the stack (Segoe UI / Arial).

    Returns:
        Multi-line CSS string of @font-face rules ready for a <style> tag.
    """
    rules: list[str] = []
    for weight, style, fname in _FONT_FILES:
        dest = FONTS_STATIC_DIR / fname
        if not dest.exists():
            continue   # not available — skip
        # Use the Streamlit static serving URL — NO base64, just a normal path
        url = f"/app/static/fonts/{fname}"
        rules.append(
            f"  @font-face {{\n"
            f'    font-family: "Urbanist";\n'
            f'    src: url("{url}") format("truetype");\n'
            f"    font-weight: {weight};\n"
            f"    font-style: {style};\n"
            f"    font-display: swap;\n"
            f"  }}"
        )
    return "\n".join(rules)


# ── Startup: ensure fonts are in the static directory, then build CSS ─────────
# _ensure_fonts_static() copies TTFs from assets/fonts/ → .streamlit/static/fonts/
# so they are reachable at /app/static/fonts/<filename>.
_ensure_fonts_static()

# Build the @font-face CSS block once.  Streamlit re-runs this entire script on
# every user interaction, but _font_face_css() is fast (no file I/O — the files
# were already copied above) so the overhead is negligible.
_FONT_FACE_BLOCK = _font_face_css()


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 3.  CSS INJECTION
#     All CSS is in one f-string <style> block so there is one place to retheme.
#     The font is embedded via base64 data URIs (see _FONT_FACE_BLOCK above).
#
#     NOTE: because this is an f-string, every CSS brace must be doubled: {{ }}
#     Section contents:
#       • _FONT_FACE_BLOCK — 18 @font-face rules, each a base64 data-URI TTF
#       • html/body        — Urbanist as primary font family
#       • .main            — light grey page background
#       • stSidebar        — dark navy sidebar (#111827)
#       • .stage-pill*     — four pill variants (grey / green / amber / red)
#       • .code-block      — dark code display
#       • .success-box / .error-box — inline status banners
#       • .stButton        — rounded buttons with Urbanist font
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

st.markdown(
    f"""
    <style>

    /* ── Urbanist font — all 18 weights/styles ──────────────────────────────
       Served from .streamlit/static/fonts/ via Streamlit's static file server.
       URLs are /app/static/fonts/<filename> — requires enableStaticServing=true
       in .streamlit/config.toml (already configured in this project).
       _ensure_fonts_static() copies the TTFs from assets/fonts/ on startup.   */
{_FONT_FACE_BLOCK}

    /* Apply Urbanist as the primary font across all Streamlit elements.
       The stack falls back to Segoe UI → Arial if the font failed to load. */
    html, body, [class*="css"] {{
        font-family: "Urbanist", "Segoe UI", Arial, sans-serif;
    }}

    /* ── Page background ── */
    .main {{ background-color: #f4f6f8; }}

    /* ── Dark sidebar ── */
    section[data-testid="stSidebar"] {{ background-color: #111827; }}
    section[data-testid="stSidebar"] * {{ color: #ffffff; }}

    /* ── Sidebar brand block ── */
    .brand-box {{
        padding: 12px 10px 20px 10px;
        border-bottom: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.1);
        margin-bottom: 20px;
    }}
    .brand-row  {{ display:flex; align-items:center; gap:12px; }}
    .brand-mark {{
        width:38px; height:38px; border-radius:10px;
        background:rgba(255,255,255,0.1);
        display:flex; align-items:center; justify-content:center;
        font-weight:700; color:white;
    }}
    .brand-title    {{ margin:0; font-size:20px; font-weight:700; color:white; }}
    .brand-subtitle {{ margin-top:6px; color:#9ca3af; font-size:13px; line-height:1.4; }}

    /* ── White content cards ── */
    .top-card, .panel {{
        background:#ffffff; border:1px solid #e5e7eb;
        border-radius:16px; padding:22px;
        box-shadow:0 1px 2px rgba(16,24,40,0.05);
        margin-bottom:20px;
    }}
    .panel-title    {{ font-size:20px; font-weight:700; margin-bottom:4px; color:#111827; }}
    .panel-subtitle {{ color:#6b7280; font-size:14px; margin-bottom:18px; }}

    /* ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
       STAGE PILLS — four variants so the sqlglot result stands out:
         .stage-pill        grey   — intent / schema / sql_writer / sql_review / executor
         .stage-pill-valid  green  — sql_validator_agent:passed …
         .stage-pill-skip   amber  — sql_validator_agent:skipped…
         .stage-pill-fail   red    — sql_validator_agent:parse_error / disallowed_stmt_type
    ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────── */

    /* Default grey pill (all standard stages) */
    .stage-pill {{
        display:inline-block;
        border:1px solid #e5e7eb;
        background:#f8fafc;
        border-radius:999px;
        padding:6px 14px;
        font-size:12px;
        font-family:"Urbanist","Segoe UI",sans-serif;
        color:#6b7280;
        margin-right:6px;
        margin-bottom:6px;
        white-space:nowrap;
    }}

    /* GREEN — sqlglot validation PASSED (happy path) */
    .stage-pill-valid {{
        display:inline-block;
        border:1.5px solid #bbf7d0;
        background:#ecfdf5;
        border-radius:999px;
        padding:6px 14px;
        font-size:12px;
        font-family:"Urbanist","Segoe UI",sans-serif;
        color:#15803d;
        font-weight:600;
        margin-right:6px;
        margin-bottom:6px;
        white-space:nowrap;
    }}

    /* AMBER — sqlglot not installed, check was skipped */
    .stage-pill-skip {{
        display:inline-block;
        border:1.5px solid #fde68a;
        background:#fffbeb;
        border-radius:999px;
        padding:6px 14px;
        font-size:12px;
        font-family:"Urbanist","Segoe UI",sans-serif;
        color:#92400e;
        font-weight:600;
        margin-right:6px;
        margin-bottom:6px;
        white-space:nowrap;
    }}

    /* RED — sqlglot parse error or disallowed statement type */
    .stage-pill-fail {{
        display:inline-block;
        border:1.5px solid #fecaca;
        background:#fef2f2;
        border-radius:999px;
        padding:6px 14px;
        font-size:12px;
        font-family:"Urbanist","Segoe UI",sans-serif;
        color:#b91c1c;
        font-weight:600;
        margin-right:6px;
        margin-bottom:6px;
        white-space:nowrap;
    }}

    /* ── Dark code block (saved-config viewer) ── */
    .code-block {{
        background:#0f172a; color:#e2e8f0;
        padding:14px; border-radius:10px;
        font-family:"Cascadia Code",Consolas,monospace;
        font-size:13px; white-space:pre-wrap; overflow-x:auto;
    }}

    /* ── Inline status banners ── */
    .success-box {{
        background:#ecfdf3; color:#166534;
        border:1px solid #bbf7d0;
        padding:12px 14px; border-radius:12px; margin-top:12px;
    }}
    .error-box {{
        background:#fef2f2; color:#b91c1c;
        border:1px solid #fecaca;
        padding:12px 14px; border-radius:12px; margin-top:12px;
    }}

    /* ── File attachment chip ── */
    .file-chip {{
        display:inline-flex; align-items:center; gap:6px;
        padding:8px 10px; border:1px solid #e5e7eb;
        background:#f8fafc; border-radius:999px;
        font-size:12px; margin-top:8px; color:#111827;
    }}

    /* ── Chat message bubbles ── */
    div[data-testid="stChatMessage"] {{ border-radius:14px; }}

    /* ── Buttons ── */
    .stButton > button {{
        border-radius:12px; font-weight:600; padding:0.5rem 1rem;
        font-family:"Urbanist","Segoe UI",sans-serif;
    }}
    .stDownloadButton > button {{
        border-radius:12px; font-weight:600; padding:0.5rem 1rem;
        font-family:"Urbanist","Segoe UI",sans-serif;
    }}

    </style>
    """,
    unsafe_allow_html=True,
)


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 4.  DEFAULT CONFIG & STATE HELPERS
#
#     DEFAULT_CONFIG  — used when no saved config exists on disk yet.
#     load_config_from_disk()  — reads JSON from CONFIG_PATH; returns None if
#                                the file is missing or corrupt.
#     save_config_to_disk()   — writes config dict as pretty JSON.
#     init_state()             — initialises all session_state keys exactly
#                                once per browser tab (idempotent).
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

# Factory defaults — these ship with the app and point to the on-prem DLH
DEFAULT_CONFIG = {
    "provider":     "dlh",
    "model_name":   "google/gemma-4-31b-it",
    "dlh_base_url": "http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1",
    "dlh_api_key":  "EMPTY",
}


def load_config_from_disk() -> dict | None:
    """
    Load the persisted LLM config from .streamlit/compliance_bot_config.json.

    Returns the config dict on success, or None if:
      - the file does not exist yet (first run)
      - the file is corrupt / not valid JSON
    The caller falls back to DEFAULT_CONFIG in both cases.
    """
    if not CONFIG_PATH.exists():
        return None
    try:
        with CONFIG_PATH.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
            return json.load(fh)
    except Exception:
        # Corrupt file — silently fall back to defaults
        return None


def save_config_to_disk(config: dict) -> None:
    """
    Persist the LLM config dict to disk as indented JSON.
    Called after a successful Test & Save so settings survive a page refresh.
    """
    with CONFIG_PATH.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        json.dump(config, fh, indent=2)


def init_state() -> None:
    """
    Initialise all Streamlit session_state keys that the app relies on.

    This function is called once at module level (see the call below).
    Streamlit re-runs the entire script on every interaction, so each key
    is guarded with `if key not in st.session_state` to avoid resetting
    values that the user has already set.

    Keys initialised here:
      messages           — list of chat message dicts (role + content + meta)
      active_page        — which page is currently shown ("LLM Configuration" | "Chat")
      attached_file_name — name of an uploaded file (UI-only feature, not sent to backend)
      config             — active LLM configuration dict
    """
    if "messages" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.messages = []         # start with an empty conversation

    if "active_page" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.active_page = "LLM Configuration"  # open config page first

    if "attached_file_name" not in st.session_state:
        st.session_state.attached_file_name = None  # no file attached yet

    if "config" not in st.session_state:
        # Try to restore a previously saved config; fall back to factory defaults
        saved = load_config_from_disk()
        st.session_state.config = saved or DEFAULT_CONFIG.copy()


# Run init_state() once when the script loads (or re-loads after a code change)
init_state()


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 5.  API HELPERS
#
#     post_llm_config()    — POST /api/v1/config/llm
#                            Sends the chosen provider settings to the backend,
#                            which validates the connection and rebuilds the
#                            LangGraph pipeline.  Raises RuntimeError on failure.
#
#     post_chat_question() — POST /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate
#                            Sends the user's natural-language question through
#                            the full 6-node agentic pipeline and returns the
#                            structured response (stages, SQL, rows, summary).
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def post_llm_config(payload: dict) -> dict:
    """
    Send LLM provider settings to the backend and validate the connection.

    Args:
        payload: dict with keys provider, model_name, dlh_base_url, etc.

    Returns:
        JSON response dict from the backend (contains 'provider', 'message').

    Raises:
        RuntimeError: if the HTTP request fails or the backend returns an error.
    """
    url = f"{API_BASE_URL}/api/v1/config/llm"
    response = requests.post(url, json=payload, timeout=60)

    try:
        data = response.json()
    except Exception:
        # Non-JSON response (e.g. 502 Bad Gateway HTML page)
        data = {"error": response.text}

    if not response.ok:
        # Surface the most specific error message available
        raise RuntimeError(
            data.get("error") or data.get("detail") or "Connection failed"
        )

    return data


def post_chat_question(question: str) -> dict:
    """
    Send the user's question to the LangGraph NL2SQL pipeline.

    The backend runs these nodes in order:
      Node 1  IntentAgent        — classify intent, normalise question
      Node 2  SchemaAgent        — select database, read live schema
      Node 3  SQLWriterAgent     — generate SELECT statement
      Node 4  SQLReviewAgent     — regex-level safety + table-existence check
      Node 4.5 SQLValidatorAgent — sqlglot AST-level validation (NEW)
      Node 5  ExecutorAgent      — read-only SQLite execution

    The response includes a 'stages' list that records what each node did,
    including the sqlglot validation result (e.g. "sql_validator_agent:passed
    dialect=sqlite").  format_stages() below renders that list as visual pills.

    Args:
        question: raw natural-language compliance question from the user.

    Returns:
        JSON response dict with keys: stages, sql_query, df_output,
        result_summary, needs_clarification, clarification_question, error.

    Raises:
        RuntimeError: on HTTP / network errors.
    """
    url = f"{API_BASE_URL}/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate"
    response = requests.post(url, json={"question": question}, timeout=120)

    try:
        data = response.json()
    except Exception:
        data = {"error": response.text}

    if not response.ok:
        raise RuntimeError(
            data.get("error") or data.get("detail") or "Chat failed"
        )

    return data


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 6.  SIDEBAR
#     Dark navy sidebar with brand name and a radio-button page selector.
#     The radio widget value is stored in session_state.active_page so the
#     router at the bottom of the file knows which page to render.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

with st.sidebar:
    # Brand header block
    st.markdown(
        """
        <div class="brand-box">
            <div class="brand-row">
                <div>
                    <div class="brand-title">Compliance Chatbot</div>
                    <div class="brand-subtitle">
                        DLH powered compliance workspace.
                    </div>
                </div>
            </div>
        </div>
        """,
        unsafe_allow_html=True,
    )

    st.caption("WORKSPACE")

    # Page selector — selecting a different option triggers a full Streamlit rerun
    # which re-executes this script; the router at the bottom then calls the
    # correct render function.
    page = st.radio(
        label="Navigation",
        options=["LLM Configuration", "Chat"],
        index=0 if st.session_state.active_page == "LLM Configuration" else 1,
        label_visibility="collapsed",
    )
    st.session_state.active_page = page   # keep session_state in sync


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 7.  PAGE HEADER
#     A white card above the page content with a title and subtitle.
#     The text changes depending on which page is active.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

if st.session_state.active_page == "LLM Configuration":
    page_title    = "LLM Configuration"
    page_subtitle = "Configure providers, test connections, and persist runtime settings."
else:
    page_title    = "Chat"
    page_subtitle = "Use the configured provider for conversational compliance analysis."

st.markdown(
    f"""
    <div class="top-card">
        <h2 style="margin:0;font-size:26px;">{page_title}</h2>
        <p style="margin:4px 0 0;color:#6b7280;font-size:15px;">
            {page_subtitle}
        </p>
    </div>
    """,
    unsafe_allow_html=True,
)


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 8.  LLM CONFIGURATION PAGE
#     Two-column layout:
#       Left  — form to set DLH model name and API keys.
#               "Test & Save" button POSTs to the backend to validate the
#               connection and then persists the config to disk.
#       Right — read-only JSON viewer showing the last persisted config.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def render_config_page() -> None:
    """Render the full LLM Configuration page (left form + right config viewer)."""

    col1, col2 = st.columns([0.95, 1.3], gap="large")

    # ── Left column: configuration form ───────────────────────────────────────
    with col1:
        st.markdown(
            """
            <div class="panel-title">LLM Configuration</div>
            <div class="panel-subtitle">
                Configure DLH provider and persist settings locally between sessions.
            </div>
            """,
            unsafe_allow_html=True,
        )

        cfg = st.session_state.config  # currently active config dict

        # Wrap all form widgets in a single st.form so that the submit button
        # collects all values in one Streamlit rerun (avoids partial updates).
        with st.form("llm_config_form"):

            # Model name — Hugging Face model path (e.g. "google/gemma-4-31b-it")
            model_name = st.text_input(
                "Model / Resource ID",
                value=cfg.get("model_name", DEFAULT_CONFIG["model_name"]),
            )

            # Pre-fill DLH fields from the active config
            dlh_base_url = cfg.get("dlh_base_url", DEFAULT_CONFIG["dlh_base_url"])
            dlh_api_key  = cfg.get("dlh_api_key",  DEFAULT_CONFIG["dlh_api_key"])

            dlh_base_url = st.text_input(
                "DLH Base URL",
                value=dlh_base_url,
                placeholder="http://10.190.236.15:9000/api/v1",
            )
            dlh_api_key = st.text_input(
                "DLH API Key",
                value=dlh_api_key,
                # Mask the key if it's been set to something real
                type="password" if dlh_api_key and dlh_api_key != "EMPTY" else "default",
            )

            submitted = st.form_submit_button("Test & Save", type="primary")

        # "Load Saved Config" sits outside the form because it needs its own
        # click handler that reruns the page without submitting the form.
        load_saved = st.button("Load Saved Config")

        if load_saved:
            saved = load_config_from_disk()
            if saved:
                st.session_state.config = saved
                st.success("Saved configuration loaded.")
                st.rerun()
            else:
                st.warning("No saved configuration found on disk yet.")

        # Handle form submission: test the connection, save, then navigate to Chat
        if submitted:
            payload = {
                "provider":     "dlh",
                "model_name":   model_name,
                "dlh_base_url": dlh_base_url,
                "dlh_api_key":  dlh_api_key,
            }
            try:
                with st.spinner("Testing provider connection and rebuilding LangGraph pipeline…"):
                    data = post_llm_config(payload)

                # Persist to disk so config survives a browser refresh
                save_config_to_disk(payload)
                st.session_state.config = payload

                # Add a system message to the chat confirming the new provider
                st.session_state.messages.append({
                    "role":    "assistant",
                    "content": f"Connected to {data.get('provider', 'dlh')}. "
                               "You can start chatting now.",
                })

                st.success(data.get("message", "Configuration saved successfully."))
                # Automatically switch to the Chat page after a successful save
                st.session_state.active_page = "Chat"
                st.rerun()

            except Exception as exc:
                st.error(str(exc))

    # ── Right column: saved-config viewer ─────────────────────────────────────
    with col2:
        st.markdown(
            """
            <div class="panel-title">Saved Configuration</div>
            <div class="panel-subtitle">
                Settings stored in a local JSON file so they remain available after refresh.
            </div>
            """,
            unsafe_allow_html=True,
        )
        saved = load_config_from_disk()
        if saved:
            # st.code gives syntax highlighting and a one-click copy button
            st.code(json.dumps(saved, indent=2), language="json")
        else:
            st.markdown(
                '<div class="code-block">No saved configuration yet.</div>',
                unsafe_allow_html=True,
            )


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 9.  format_stages()  —  STAGE PILL RENDERING
#
#     The backend returns a list of plain strings like:
#       ["intent_agent:ok",
#        "schema_agent:db=COMP_REPORTS.db",
#        "sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry=0",
#        "sql_review_agent:passed",
#        "sql_validator_agent:passed dialect=sqlite",   ← Node 4.5 (sqlglot)
#        "executor_agent:ok rows=1"]
#
#     This function converts that list into coloured pill HTML.
#
#     Pill colouring rules for the sqlglot validator stage (Node 4.5):
#       "sql_validator_agent:passed…"  → green  (.stage-pill-valid)
#       "sql_validator_agent:skipped…" → amber  (.stage-pill-skip)
#       any other "sql_validator_agent…" → red  (.stage-pill-fail)
#       all other stages                → grey  (.stage-pill)
#
#     Why highlight the sqlglot stage separately?
#       The sqlglot validator is a new Node 4.5 that sits between the
#       SQLReviewAgent and the ExecutorAgent.  By giving it a distinct
#       colour we make its pass/fail result immediately obvious in the
#       chat UI without having to read the raw stage string carefully.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def _pill_class_for_stage(stage: str) -> str:
    """
    Determine which CSS class to use for a given stage string.

    The sqlglot validator is the only stage that gets a coloured pill;
    everything else uses the default grey .stage-pill class.

    Args:
        stage: a single stage string from the backend response.

    Returns:
        CSS class name (one of: "stage-pill", "stage-pill-valid",
        "stage-pill-skip", "stage-pill-fail").
    """
    if stage.startswith("sql_validator_agent"):
        # Node 4.5 — sqlglot AST validation result
        if "passed" in stage:
            # sqlglot parsed the SQL, confirmed it is a SELECT, and
            # canonicalised it to the SQLite dialect — all good ✓
            return "stage-pill-valid"
        elif "skipped" in stage:
            # sqlglot is not installed; the check was gracefully bypassed.
            # Amber = warning, not failure.
            return "stage-pill-skip"
        else:
            # parse_error / disallowed_stmt_type / empty_ast — SQL rejected
            return "stage-pill-fail"

    # All other stages (intent, schema, sql_writer, sql_review, executor)
    # use the default neutral grey pill.
    return "stage-pill"


def format_stages(stages: list) -> str:
    """
    Convert the backend's stages list to an HTML string of pill elements.

    Each stage string becomes one <span> pill.  The pill colour is chosen
    by _pill_class_for_stage() so the sqlglot validation result is visually
    distinct from the other pipeline stages (see docstring above).

    Args:
        stages: list of stage strings from the API response, e.g.
                ["intent_agent:ok", "sql_validator_agent:passed dialect=sqlite"]

    Returns:
        HTML string ready to pass to st.markdown(unsafe_allow_html=True),
        or an empty string if there are no stages.
    """
    if not stages:
        return ""

    return "".join(
        f'<span class="{_pill_class_for_stage(stage)}">{stage}</span>'
        for stage in stages
    )


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 10. render_chat_message()
#
#     Renders a single message dict from st.session_state.messages.
#
#     User messages  — plain st.chat_message("user") with just the question text.
#     Bot messages   — st.chat_message("assistant") with:
#                       • "Query completed successfully." / clarification text
#                       • Stages block — pill row showing every pipeline step,
#                         including the highlighted sqlglot validator pill
#                       • SQL block — syntax-highlighted SELECT statement
#                       • Row count
#                       • Result summary (natural-language explanation)
#                       • Raw results (JSON) — for debugging / advanced users
#
#     The meta dict has this shape (set by render_chat_page → post_chat_question):
#       {
#         "stages":         list[str],   ← all pipeline stage strings
#         "sql_query":      str,
#         "row_count":      int,
#         "result_summary": str,
#         "results":        list[dict],  ← raw df_output rows
#         "clarification":  str,         ← set only on needs_clarification=True
#       }
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def render_chat_message(message: dict) -> None:
    """
    Render one message (user or assistant) inside its chat bubble.

    User messages show only the question text.
    Assistant messages additionally render the pipeline metadata block
    (stages, SQL, row count, summary, raw results).
    """
    role    = message.get("role", "assistant")
    content = message.get("content", "")

    with st.chat_message("user" if role == "user" else "assistant"):
        # The main message text (question for user, summary for assistant)
        st.markdown(content)

        # Only assistant messages carry the pipeline metadata block
        meta = message.get("meta")
        if not meta:
            return   # user message — nothing more to show

        stages         = meta.get("stages", [])
        sql_query      = meta.get("sql_query")
        row_count      = meta.get("row_count")
        result_summary = meta.get("result_summary")
        results        = meta.get("results")
        clarification  = meta.get("clarification")

        # ── Stage pills ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        # Render the pipeline stage trace as a row of pills.
        # The sqlglot validator pill will be green/amber/red depending on its
        # outcome (see format_stages() above).  All other pills are grey.
        if stages:
            st.markdown(
                f"""
                <strong>Stages</strong>
                <div style="margin-top:8px;margin-bottom:12px;">
                    {format_stages(stages)}
                </div>
                """,
                unsafe_allow_html=True,
            )

        # ── Clarification request ────────────────────────────────────────────
        # If the backend could not classify the intent it returns a clarifying
        # question instead of SQL results.
        if clarification:
            st.info(clarification)

        # ── Generated SQL ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        # st.code() gives syntax highlighting + copy-to-clipboard button.
        if sql_query:
            st.markdown("**SQL**")
            st.code(sql_query, language="sql")

        # ── Result metadata ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
        if row_count is not None:
            st.markdown(f"**Rows returned:** {row_count}")

        if result_summary:
            st.markdown("**Summary**")
            st.markdown(result_summary)

        # ── Raw results (collapsible for large result sets) ──────────────────
        if results is not None:
            st.markdown("**Results**")
            st.code(json.dumps(results, indent=2), language="json")


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 11. build_transcript()
#
#     Serialises the full conversation history to a plain-text string.
#     Used by the "Export Chat" download button so the user can save the
#     conversation locally.  Includes metadata (stages, SQL, summary) for
#     each assistant turn so the export is fully self-contained.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def build_transcript() -> str:
    """
    Convert st.session_state.messages to a plain-text string for download.

    Format:
        User:
        <question>

        Assistant:
        <summary>
        Metadata:
        { stages, sql_query, row_count, result_summary, results }

    Returns:
        Multi-line string.  Returns "No conversation available." if the
        message list is empty.
    """
    lines = []
    for msg in st.session_state.messages:
        role = "User" if msg["role"] == "user" else "Assistant"
        lines.append(f"{role}:\n{msg.get('content', '')}")

        meta = msg.get("meta")
        if meta:
            lines.append("Metadata:")
            lines.append(json.dumps(meta, indent=2))

    return "\n\n".join(lines) or "No conversation available."


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 12. render_chat_page()
#
#     Renders the Chat page:
#       • Static pill row showing the 5 conceptual pipeline stages
#         (informational header, not the live per-query stages)
#       • Message history loop — calls render_chat_message() for each msg
#       • Action bar — "New Chat" button + "Export Chat" download button
#       • st.chat_input() — the main question text box
#       • On question submit — calls post_chat_question(), stores the
#         structured result in session_state.messages, then reruns.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

def render_chat_page() -> None:
    """Render the full Chat page."""

    # ── Informational stage pills (page header decoration) ──────────────────
    # These pills show the pipeline stages as a fixed header decoration.
    # They do NOT change colour — they are just labels showing the workflow.
    # The live, per-query coloured pills appear inside each assistant message
    # (rendered by render_chat_message → format_stages).
    st.markdown(
        """
        <div class="panel-title">Conversational Chat</div>
        <div class="panel-subtitle">
            Ask compliance questions and continue the live conversation
            with markdown-style output.
        </div>
        <div style="margin-bottom:16px;">
            <span class="stage-pill">Intent Analysis</span>
            <span class="stage-pill">Clarification</span>
            <span class="stage-pill">DB Selection</span>
            <span class="stage-pill">SQL Generation</span>
            <span class="stage-pill">SQL Validation</span>
            <span class="stage-pill">Execution</span>
        </div>
        """,
        unsafe_allow_html=True,
    )

    # ── Message history ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # Iterate the full message list and render each one.
    # Each assistant message will display its own live stage pills (with
    # the sqlglot pill highlighted) via render_chat_message().
    for message in st.session_state.messages:
        render_chat_message(message)

    # ── Action bar (New Chat | Export Chat) ──────────────────────────────────
    c1, c2, c3 = st.columns([1, 1, 5])

    with c1:
        # "New Chat" clears the message history and resets the attached file
        if st.button("New Chat"):
            st.session_state.messages          = []
            st.session_state.attached_file_name = None
            st.rerun()

    with c2:
        # "Export Chat" downloads the full transcript as a .txt file
        transcript = build_transcript()
        st.download_button(
            label="Export Chat",
            data=transcript,
            file_name="conversation.txt",
            mime="text/plain",
        )

    # ── Chat input ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # st.chat_input() renders the text box at the bottom of the chat area.
    # It returns None unless the user has just pressed Enter / clicked Send.
    question = st.chat_input("Example: How many persons from Mumbai circle?")

    if question:
        # 1. Record the user's message immediately so it appears in the history
        st.session_state.messages.append({"role": "user", "content": question})

        # 2. If a file was attached (UI-only feature), add a note about it
        if st.session_state.attached_file_name:
            st.session_state.messages.append({
                "role":    "assistant",
                "content": f"Attached file: {st.session_state.attached_file_name}",
                "meta":    {"clarification": "File attachment captured in UI only."},
            })

        # 3. Call the backend — the 6-node LangGraph pipeline runs here
        try:
            with st.spinner("Assistant is thinking…"):
                data = post_chat_question(question)

            # The 'stages' list records what each pipeline node did, including
            # the sqlglot validator result ("sql_validator_agent:passed dialect=sqlite").
            # render_chat_message() will display these as coloured pills.
            stages = data.get("stages", [])

            if data.get("needs_clarification"):
                # Backend could not determine intent — asks the user to clarify
                st.session_state.messages.append({
                    "role":    "assistant",
                    "content": data.get(
                        "clarification_question",
                        "Please clarify your question.",
                    ),
                    "meta": {"stages": stages},
                })

            else:
                # Normal success path — store SQL, rows, summary, and stages
                df_output = data.get("df_output") or []
                row_count = len(df_output) if isinstance(df_output, list) else 0

                st.session_state.messages.append({
                    "role":    "assistant",
                    "content": "Query completed successfully.",
                    "meta": {
                        # 'stages' contains the live per-query stage strings.
                        # The sqlglot pill (e.g. "sql_validator_agent:passed dialect=sqlite")
                        # will be rendered in green by format_stages() inside
                        # render_chat_message().
                        "stages":         stages,
                        "sql_query":      data.get("sql_query", "N/A"),
                        "row_count":      row_count,
                        "result_summary": data.get("result_summary"),
                        "results":        df_output,
                    },
                })

        except Exception as exc:
            # Surface any backend / network error as an assistant message
            # so the user sees it in context rather than as a Streamlit banner.
            st.session_state.messages.append({
                "role":    "assistant",
                "content": f"Error: {exc}",
            })

        # 4. Rerun so the new message(s) appear in the rendered history
        st.rerun()


# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
# 13. PAGE ROUTER
#
#     Called on every Streamlit rerun (every user interaction).
#     Reads session_state.active_page and delegates to the correct render fn.
# ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

if st.session_state.active_page == "LLM Configuration":
    render_config_page()
else:
    render_chat_page()



####################################################################################################
FILE: extra\gradio_app.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Gradio front-end for Compliance Chatbot v2 (LangGraph).

Mirrors every feature of the embedded HTML UI:
  • Tab 1 — LLM Configuration  (Provider selector, DLH/ICA fields, Test & Save, Load Saved)
  • Tab 2 — Chat               (Live LangGraph stage pills, chat window, composer, Copy/Export)

The app talks to the same FastAPI backend endpoints as the HTML UI.
Set BASE_URL below (or export CB_BASE_URL env var) to point at the running FastAPI server.

LangGraph stage trace is rendered after each /api/v1/compliance/orchestrate call.
Stage pill labels map to the five agent node prefixes:
  intent_agent | schema_agent | sql_writer_agent | sql_review_agent | executor_agent
"""

import json
import os
import time
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Optional

import gradio as gr
import httpx

# FastAPI backend URL — override with the CB_BASE_URL environment variable.
BASE_URL: str = os.getenv("CB_BASE_URL", "http://127.0.0.1:8000")

# Path to the JSON file that persists LLM settings between sessions.
_CONFIG_FILE = Path(__file__).parent / ".cb_saved_config.json"

# LangGraph agent node prefix -> human-readable pill label.
# The prefix is matched against the start of each stage string returned by the API.
STAGE_PREFIX_MAP: list[tuple[str, str]] = [
    ("intent_agent",     "Intent Agent"),
    ("schema_agent",     "Schema Agent"),
    ("sql_writer_agent", "SQL Writer"),
    ("sql_review_agent", "SQL Review"),
    ("executor_agent",   "Executor"),
    ("clarify_node",     "Clarification"),
]
STAGES: list[str] = [label for _, label in STAGE_PREFIX_MAP]

# Small utility helpers shared across the module.

def _save_config(config: dict) -> None:
    _CONFIG_FILE.write_text(json.dumps(config, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

def _load_config() -> Optional[dict]:
    if _CONFIG_FILE.exists():
        try:
            return json.loads(_CONFIG_FILE.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        except Exception:
            return None
    return None

def _config_preview_text(config: Optional[dict]) -> str:
    if config is None:
        return "No saved configuration yet."
    return json.dumps(config, indent=2)

def _timestamp() -> str:
    return time.strftime("%H:%M:%S")

# HTTP helpers for calling the FastAPI backend.

def _post_json(path: str, payload: dict, timeout: float = 30.0) -> dict:
    """POST JSON to the backend and return the parsed response dict."""
    url = BASE_URL.rstrip("/") + path
    with httpx.Client(verify=False, timeout=timeout) as client:
        resp = client.post(url, json=payload)
    try:
        data = resp.json()
    except Exception:
        data = {"error": resp.text}
    if not resp.is_success:
        raise RuntimeError(data.get("error") or data.get("detail") or f"HTTP {resp.status_code}")
    return data

# Functions that handle provider toggle, connect and load-config UI events.

def _toggle_provider(provider: str):
    """Show/hide DLH vs ICA field groups."""
    dlh_vis = gr.update(visible=(provider == "dlh"))
    ica_vis = gr.update(visible=(provider == "ica"))
    return dlh_vis, ica_vis

def _connect(
    provider, model_name,
    dlh_base_url, dlh_api_key,
    ica_base_url, ica_api_key, ica_namespace,
):
    payload = {
        "provider": provider,
        "model_name": model_name or "google/gemma-4-31b-it",
        "dlh_base_url": dlh_base_url or "",
        "dlh_api_key": dlh_api_key or "",
        "dlh_verify_ssl": False,
        "ica_base_url": ica_base_url or "",
        "ica_api_key": ica_api_key or "",
        "ica_namespace": ica_namespace or "chat-models",
        "ica_send_temperature": False,
    }
    try:
        data = _post_json("/api/v1/config/llm", payload)
        _save_config(payload)
        status_msg = f"✅  {data.get('message', 'Connection successful.')}"
        config_preview = _config_preview_text(payload)
        log_msg = f"[{_timestamp()}] Provider connection successful: {data.get('provider')}."
        return status_msg, config_preview, log_msg
    except Exception as exc:
        error_msg = f"❌  {exc}"
        log_msg = f"[{_timestamp()}] Provider connection failed: {exc}"
        return error_msg, gr.update(), log_msg

def _load_config_ui():
    cfg = _load_config()
    if cfg is None:
        return (
            gr.update(), gr.update(), gr.update(), gr.update(),
            gr.update(), gr.update(), gr.update(),
            gr.update(visible=True), gr.update(visible=False),
            "No saved configuration yet.",
            f"[{_timestamp()}] No saved config found.",
        )
    provider = cfg.get("provider", "dlh")
    dlh_vis = provider == "dlh"
    ica_vis = provider == "ica"
    return (
        gr.update(value=provider),
        gr.update(value=cfg.get("model_name", "google/gemma-4-31b-it")),
        gr.update(value=cfg.get("dlh_base_url", "")),
        gr.update(value=cfg.get("dlh_api_key", "")),
        gr.update(value=cfg.get("ica_base_url", "")),
        gr.update(value=cfg.get("ica_api_key", "")),
        gr.update(value=cfg.get("ica_namespace", "chat-models")),
        gr.update(visible=dlh_vis),
        gr.update(visible=ica_vis),
        _config_preview_text(cfg),
        f"[{_timestamp()}] Loaded saved LLM configuration.",
    )

# Functions that render stage pills, chat bubbles, send messages and export chat.

def _build_stage_html(completed_stages: list[str]) -> str:
    """
    Render the LangGraph agent stage pills row as HTML.

    Each pill is highlighted when any element of *completed_stages* starts with
    the corresponding agent node prefix.

    Args:
        completed_stages: Raw stage strings from the orchestrate endpoint
                          e.g. ``["intent_agent:ok", "schema_agent:db=COMP_REPORTS.db", ...]``.

    Returns:
        HTML string containing coloured pill spans.
    """
    pills = []
    for prefix, label in STAGE_PREFIX_MAP:
        done = any(s.startswith(prefix) for s in completed_stages)
        color = "#2563eb" if done else "#e5e7eb"
        text_color = "#ffffff" if done else "#57606a"
        weight = "700" if done else "500"
        pills.append(
            f'<span style="background:{color};color:{text_color};font-weight:{weight};'
            f'padding:4px 12px;border-radius:20px;font-size:12px;'
            f'margin:2px 4px 2px 0;display:inline-block">{label}</span>'
        )
    return "<div style='margin-bottom:8px'>" + "".join(pills) + "</div>"

def _chat_message_html(role: str, text: str) -> str:
    """Return an HTML snippet for a single chat bubble."""
    if role == "user":
        bg, align, label = "#dbeafe", "right", "You"
        label_color = "#1d4ed8"
    else:
        bg, align, label = "#f0fdf4", "left", "Bot"
        label_color = "#15803d"
    # Basic markdown-ish render
    rendered = (
        text
        .replace("&", "&amp;")
        .replace("<", "&lt;")
        .replace(">", "&gt;")
        .replace("\n", "<br>")
    )
    # Bold
    import re
    rendered = re.sub(r"\*\*(.*?)\*\*", r"<strong>\1</strong>", rendered)
    # Inline code
    rendered = re.sub(r"`([^`]+)`", r"<code>\1</code>", rendered)
    return (
        f'<div style="text-align:{align};margin:6px 0">'
        f'<span style="font-size:11px;font-weight:700;color:{label_color}">{label}</span><br>'
        f'<span style="display:inline-block;background:{bg};padding:8px 14px;'
        f'border-radius:10px;max-width:85%;text-align:left;font-size:14px">{rendered}</span>'
        f"</div>"
    )

def _wrap_chat_html(bubbles: str) -> str:
    """
    Wrap raw bubble HTML in a fixed-height scrollable container and append a
    small inline script that scrolls it to the bottom after every render.

    Args:
        bubbles: Concatenated chat bubble HTML strings.

    Returns:
        Self-contained HTML string with its own scroll box and auto-scroll script.
    """
    return (
        '<div class="cb-chat-scroll" id="cb-chat-inner">'
        + (bubbles or '<span style="color:#94a3b8;font-size:13px">No messages yet. Ask a compliance question to get started.</span>')
        + "</div>"
        + "<script>"
        + "(function(){"
        + "var el=document.getElementById('cb-chat-inner');"
        + "if(el){el.scrollTop=el.scrollHeight;}"
        + "})();"
        + "</script>"
    )


def _send_message(question: str, raw_bubbles: str, stages_html: str, attached_file):
    """
    Send a message to the LangGraph orchestrate endpoint and return updated UI state.

    Args:
        question:      User's typed question.
        raw_bubbles:   Accumulated raw bubble HTML (stored in gr.State — never wrapped).
        stages_html:   Current stage pills HTML.
        attached_file: Optional uploaded file object (UI-only preview).

    Returns:
        Tuple of (raw_bubbles, display_html, stages_html, cleared_question, cleared_file, log_msg).
        raw_bubbles and display_html are separate: raw_bubbles feeds back into gr.State,
        display_html (wrapped) goes to the gr.HTML component.
    """
    question = (question or "").strip()
    if not question:
        return raw_bubbles, _wrap_chat_html(raw_bubbles), stages_html, "", None, f"[{_timestamp()}] Empty message — not sent."

    # Accumulate into raw bubbles; display wrapper is added only at return time.
    bubbles = (raw_bubbles or "") + _chat_message_html("user", question)

    if attached_file is not None:
        fname = Path(attached_file).name if isinstance(attached_file, str) else attached_file.name
        bubbles += _chat_message_html("bot", f"Attached file: **{fname}** (UI only)")

    try:
        data = _post_json("/api/v1/compliance/orchestrate", {"question": question})

        completed: list[str] = data.get("stages", [])
        stages_html = _build_stage_html(completed)
        stage_trace = " -> ".join(completed) if completed else "—"

        if data.get("needs_clarification"):
            clarification = data.get("clarification_question", "Could you clarify your question?")
            bot_text = (
                f"{clarification}\n\n"
                f"**Agent trace:** `{stage_trace}`"
            )
            bubbles += _chat_message_html("bot", bot_text)
            log_msg = f"[{_timestamp()}] LangGraph requested clarification."

        elif data.get("error"):
            bot_text = (
                f"**Pipeline error:** {data['error']}\n\n"
                f"**Agent trace:** `{stage_trace}`"
            )
            bubbles += _chat_message_html("bot", bot_text)
            log_msg = f"[{_timestamp()}] Pipeline error: {data['error']}"

        else:
            sql = data.get("sql_query") or "N/A"
            rows = data.get("df_output") or []
            row_count = len(rows) if isinstance(rows, list) else 0
            summary = data.get("result_summary") or f"{row_count} row(s) returned."
            bot_text = (
                f"**Query completed.**\n\n"
                f"**Summary:** {summary}\n\n"
                f"**SQL:**\n```\n{sql}\n```\n\n"
                f"**Results ({row_count} rows):**\n```\n{json.dumps(rows, indent=2)}\n```\n\n"
                f"**Agent trace:** `{stage_trace}`"
            )
            bubbles += _chat_message_html("bot", bot_text)
            log_msg = f"[{_timestamp()}] Pipeline complete — {row_count} row(s) returned."

    except Exception as exc:
        bubbles += _chat_message_html("bot", f"Error: {exc}")
        log_msg = f"[{_timestamp()}] Chat execution failed: {exc}"
        stages_html = _build_stage_html([])

    return bubbles, _wrap_chat_html(bubbles), stages_html, "", None, log_msg

def _clear_chat():
    # Returns (raw_bubbles, display_html, stages_html, question, attached_file, log)
    return (
        "",
        _wrap_chat_html(""),
        _build_stage_html([]),
        "",
        None,
        f"[{_timestamp()}] Started a new chat session.",
    )

def _copy_chat(history_html: str) -> str:
    """Strip HTML tags and return plain text (Gradio JS will handle clipboard)."""
    import re
    text = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", "", history_html or "")
    return text.strip() or "No conversation available."

def _export_chat(history_html: str):
    """Write conversation to a temp file and return a downloadable gr.File path."""
    import re
    import tempfile

    text = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", "", history_html or "")
    text = text.strip() or "No conversation available."
    tmp = tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(
        mode="w", suffix=".txt", delete=False, encoding="utf-8", prefix="compliance_chat_"
    )
    tmp.write(text)
    tmp.flush()
    tmp.close()
    return tmp.name, f"[{_timestamp()}] Conversation exported."

# Custom CSS injected into the Gradio Blocks layout.

CUSTOM_CSS = """
