# Few-shot examples (table → prompt string)

COMP_FEW_SHOT_EXAMPLES: Dict[str, str] = {
    "COMP_TBL": """\
Question: Show the customers whose age is more than 60 and senior-citizen account flag is 1.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE SR_CTZN_ACCT_FLG = 1 AND AGE_YRS > 60;
<end_sql_response>

Question: Show the customers whose age is less than 60 and product name contains 'SR CT'.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE UPPER(PRODUCT_NAME) LIKE '%SR CT%' AND AGE_YRS < 60;
<end_sql_response>

Question: Show the customers whose maturity date is in December 2025.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE MTRTY_DT BETWEEN '2025-12-01' AND '2025-12-31';
<end_sql_response>

Question: List all accounts where KYC flag is N.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL WHERE KYC_FLG = 'N';
<end_sql_response>

Question: Show the top 10 accounts with the highest end-of-day balance.
Response:
<begin_sql_response>
SELECT * FROM COMP_TBL ORDER BY END_OF_DAY_BAL DESC LIMIT 10;
<end_sql_response>
"""
}



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\models\schemas.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Pydantic schemas (request / response models) for the Compliance Chatbot API.

These models are the single source of truth for the API contract, ensuring
strict validation of all inbound payloads and outbound responses.
"""

from typing import Dict, List, Optional

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field, field_validator


class ComplianceChatRequest(BaseModel):
    """Payload accepted by the chat and orchestrate endpoints."""

    question_id: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Optional caller-supplied idempotency key.",
        examples=["req-001"],
    )
    question: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Natural-language compliance question.",
        examples=["Show customers whose age is more than 60 and KYC flag is Y."],
    )

    @field_validator("question")
    @classmethod
    def question_must_not_be_blank(cls, value: Optional[str]) -> Optional[str]:
        """Reject blank strings; strip valid non-null values."""
        if value is not None and not value.strip():
            raise ValueError("'question' must not be a blank string.")
        return value.strip() if value else value


class ComplianceChatResponse(BaseModel):
    """Simplified response returned by the /chat endpoint."""

    question_id: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Echo of the request question_id.",
    )
    sql_query: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Generated SQL statement.",
    )
    df_output: Optional[List[Dict[str, object]]] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Query result rows as a list of column → value dicts.",
    )
    result_columns: List[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list,
        description="Ordered list of column names for the result set.",
    )
    result_summary: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Human-readable summary of the query result.",
    )
    error: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Error detail when the request failed.",
    )


class LLMConnectionRequest(BaseModel):
    """Payload for configuring and testing an LLM provider connection."""

    provider: str = Field(description="LLM provider: 'dlh' or 'ica'.")
    model_name: Optional[str] = Field(default=None, description="Remote model or resource id.")
    dlh_base_url: Optional[str] = Field(default=None, description="DLH inference server base URL.")
    dlh_api_key: Optional[str] = Field(default=None, description="DLH bearer token.")
    dlh_verify_ssl: bool = Field(default=False, description="Whether to verify DLH TLS certificates.")
    ica_base_url: Optional[str] = Field(default=None, description="ICA API host URL.")
    ica_api_key: Optional[str] = Field(default=None, description="ICA developer API key.")
    ica_namespace: Optional[str] = Field(default=None, description="ICA namespace.")
    ica_send_temperature: bool = Field(
        default=False,
        description="Whether to forward temperature/max_tokens to ICA.",
    )


class LLMConnectionResponse(BaseModel):
    """Response payload for the LLM configuration and connection-test endpoint."""

    success: bool = Field(description="Whether the connection test succeeded.")
    provider: str = Field(description="Configured LLM provider name.")
    message: str = Field(description="Human-readable connection status message.")


class ComplianceOrchestrationResponse(BaseModel):
    """
    Extended response returned by the /orchestrate endpoint.

    Includes the full agent stage audit trail, interpreted question, generated
    SQL, result rows with column metadata, and any clarification or error detail.
    """

    question_id: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Echo of the request question_id.",
    )
    interpreted_question: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Normalised question after Intent Agent analysis.",
    )
    needs_clarification: bool = Field(
        default=False,
        description="True when the user must provide more detail before a query can be run.",
    )
    clarification_question: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Follow-up question to surface to the user when intent is ambiguous.",
    )
    sql_query: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Generated SQLite SELECT statement.",
    )
    df_output: Optional[List[Dict[str, object]]] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Query result rows as a list of column → value dicts.",
    )
    result_columns: List[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list,
        description="Ordered list of column names for the result set.",
    )
    result_summary: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Human-readable summary of the query result produced by the Executor Agent.",
    )
    stages: List[str] = Field(
        default_factory=list,
        description="Ordered audit trail of completed agent stage names.",
    )
    error: Optional[str] = Field(
        default=None,
        description="Error detail when the pipeline failed.",
    )


class HealthResponse(BaseModel):
    """Response payload for the /health liveness probe."""

    status: str = Field(default="ok", description="Service health status.")
    version: str = Field(default="2.0.0", description="Application version string.")



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\__init__.py
####################################################################################################

"""
LangGraph-based NL2SQL agentic pipeline for the Compliance Chatbot.

This package contains six nodes wired into a LangGraph ``StateGraph``.
Only the SQL Writer is an agent in the strict sense (it calls an LLM);
the remaining nodes are deterministic processors:

- **SQLWriterAgent** (``sql_writer_agent.py``)
    The only LLM-calling node.  Builds a structured few-shot prompt and
    calls the configured DLH or ICA provider to generate a SQLite SELECT.

- **IntentAgent** (``intent.py``)
    Pure-Python classifier — keyword matching against domain vocabulary.
    No network calls.  Decides whether the question is clear enough to
    proceed or needs clarification.

- **SchemaAgent** (``schema.py``)
    Pure-Python + SQLite.  Scores available ``.db`` files by keyword
    overlap, reads live ``PRAGMA table_info``, and builds a Markdown
    schema context string for the SQL Writer prompt.

- **SQLReviewAgent** (``sql_review.py``)
    Pure-Python + SQLite.  Regex-based DML/DDL blocking, SELECT/FROM
    checks, and live table-existence verification.

- **SQLValidatorAgent** (``sql_validator.py``)
    Pure-Python (sqlglot).  AST-level parse, statement-type gate, and
    SQLite dialect round-trip canonicalisation.  No network calls.

- **ExecutorAgent** (``executor.py``)
    Pure-Python + SQLite.  Runs the approved SELECT inside a
    ``BEGIN DEFERRED`` transaction that is always rolled back.

Entry-point::

    from src.nl2sql_agents.graph import build_nl2sql_graph

    graph = build_nl2sql_graph(llm_service, db_path)
    result = graph.invoke({"question": "Show KYC-pending accounts."})
"""



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\executor.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Executor — Node 5 of 5 in the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Responsibility
--------------
Execute the validated SQL ``SELECT`` statement against the target SQLite
database, convert the raw cursor output into a list of column→value
dictionaries, and produce a concise human-readable summary.

The executor operates in strict read-only mode by opening every connection
inside a ``BEGIN DEFERRED`` transaction that is rolled back unconditionally
after execution — this provides a second safety net even if a disallowed
statement somehow escaped the SQL Review Agent.
"""

import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.executor_agent")

# Hard cap on the number of rows returned to the API caller.
_MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS: int = 500


class ExecutorAgent:
    """
    LangGraph node that executes the approved SQL query and formats results.

    Every connection is wrapped in a deferred read-only transaction that is
    rolled back after execution regardless of success or failure, providing
    an extra layer of data-mutation protection.
    """

    # LangGraph node entry-point — called by the graph executor.

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """
        Execute the SQL query and return formatted results.

        Args:
            state: Pipeline state with ``sql_query`` and ``db_path``.

        Returns:
            Partial ``AgentState`` with ``result_rows``, ``result_columns``,
            ``result_summary``, and ``stages``.  On failure, ``error`` is
            populated and ``result_rows`` / ``result_columns`` are empty.
        """
        sql: str = state.get("sql_query", "").strip()
        db_path_str: str = state.get("db_path", "")

        LOGGER.info("ExecutorAgent — executing SQL: %s", sql)

        if not sql:
            msg = "ExecutorAgent received an empty SQL query."
            LOGGER.error(msg)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                result_rows=[],
                result_columns=[],
                result_summary="No query to execute.",
                error=msg,
                stages=["executor_agent:empty_sql"],
            )

        if not db_path_str:
            msg = "ExecutorAgent: database path is not set in the pipeline state."
            LOGGER.error(msg)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                result_rows=[],
                result_columns=[],
                result_summary="Database path missing.",
                error=msg,
                stages=["executor_agent:no_db_path"],
            )

        db_path = Path(db_path_str)
        if not db_path.exists():
            msg = f"ExecutorAgent: database file not found at {db_path}."
            LOGGER.error(msg)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                result_rows=[],
                result_columns=[],
                result_summary="Database file not found.",
                error=msg,
                stages=["executor_agent:db_not_found"],
            )

        rows, columns, error_msg = self._execute_read_only(sql, db_path)

        if error_msg:
            LOGGER.error("ExecutorAgent — SQL execution error: %s", error_msg)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                result_rows=[],
                result_columns=[],
                result_summary="Query execution failed.",
                error=error_msg,
                stages=["executor_agent:error"],
            )

        summary = self._build_summary(rows, columns, sql)
        LOGGER.info("ExecutorAgent — %d rows returned.", len(rows))

        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            result_rows=rows,
            result_columns=columns,
            result_summary=summary,
            error=None,
            stages=[f"executor_agent:ok rows={len(rows)}"],
        )

    # Runs the SQL in a read-only deferred transaction and returns rows + columns.

    @staticmethod
    def _execute_read_only(
        sql: str,
        db_path: Path,
    ) -> Tuple[List[Dict[str, object]], List[str], Optional[str]]:
        """
        Execute *sql* inside a read-only deferred transaction.

        Args:
            sql:     Validated SELECT statement.
            db_path: Path to the target SQLite database file.

        Returns:
            A 3-tuple of ``(rows, column_names, error_message)``.
            On success ``error_message`` is ``None``; on failure ``rows`` and
            ``column_names`` are empty lists.
        """
        conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
        try:
            conn = sqlite3.connect(str(db_path), check_same_thread=False)
            conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
            conn.execute("BEGIN DEFERRED")

            cursor = conn.execute(sql)
            col_names: List[str] = [d[0] for d in (cursor.description or [])]
            raw_rows = cursor.fetchmany(_MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS)
            rows: List[Dict[str, object]] = [dict(zip(col_names, row)) for row in raw_rows]
            return rows, col_names, None

        except sqlite3.OperationalError as op_err:
            return [], [], f"SQL operational error: {op_err} | Query: {sql}"
        except sqlite3.DatabaseError as db_err:
            return [], [], f"SQLite database error: {db_err} | Query: {sql}"
        finally:
            if conn is not None:
                try:
                    conn.rollback()
                except sqlite3.Error:
                    pass
                conn.close()

    # Produces a concise human-readable summary of the query results.

    @staticmethod
    def _build_summary(
        rows: List[Dict[str, object]],
        columns: List[str],
        sql: str,
    ) -> str:
        """
        Produce a human-readable summary of the query results.

        Handles three special cases:
        - No rows returned.
        - Single-row scalar aggregation (COUNT, SUM, AVG …).
        - General multi-row result set.

        Args:
            rows:    Result rows as column → value dicts.
            columns: Ordered list of column names.
            sql:     The executed SQL statement (used for context).

        Returns:
            Summary string suitable for display in the UI.
        """
        count = len(rows)

        if count == 0:
            return "The query executed successfully but returned no matching records."

        # Scalar aggregation: single row, single column.
        if count == 1 and len(rows[0]) == 1:
            col, val = next(iter(rows[0].items()))
            return f"Result: **{col}** = `{val}`"

        limit_note = f" (first {_MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS} shown)" if count == _MAX_DISPLAY_ROWS else ""
        col_list = ", ".join(columns) if columns else ""
        return (
            f"Query returned **{count} record(s)**{limit_note}. "
            f"Columns: {col_list}."
        )



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\graph.py
####################################################################################################

"""
LangGraph NL2SQL graph factory.

Assembles the six agent nodes into a compiled ``StateGraph`` and exposes a
single factory function ``build_nl2sql_graph`` that is called once at startup.

Pipeline topology
-----------------

    [START]
       │
       ▼
  intent_agent  ──(intent_ok=False)──► clarify_node ──► [END]
       │
       │ (intent_ok=True)
       ▼
  schema_agent
       │
       ▼
  sql_writer_agent ◄──────────────────────────────────────────┐
       │                                                       │ (review OR validator failed, retry)
       ▼                                                       │
  sql_review_agent ──(passed)──► sql_validator_agent ─────────┤
       │                              │                        │
       │ (max_retries / error)        │ (sqlglot valid)        │ (sqlglot invalid → retry)
       ▼                              ▼
  executor_agent ◄──────────────── executor_agent ──► [END]

Simplified:

    sql_review_agent
        ├── passed     → sql_validator_agent
        │                   ├── valid   → executor_agent → [END]
        │                   └── invalid → sql_writer_agent (retry)
        └── max_retries/error → executor_agent → [END]
"""

from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

from langgraph.graph import END, START, StateGraph

from src.nl2sql_agents.executor import ExecutorAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.intent import IntentAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.schema import SchemaAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_review import SQLReviewAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_validator import SQLValidatorAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.sql_writer_agent import SQLWriterAgent
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.services.model_loader_service import ModelLoaderService
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_chatbot.nl2sql_agents.graph")

# Node name constants — single source of truth for all edge definitions.
NODE_INTENT:       str = "intent_agent"
NODE_SCHEMA:       str = "schema_agent"
NODE_SQL_WRITER:   str = "sql_writer_agent"
NODE_SQL_REVIEW:   str = "sql_review_agent"
NODE_SQL_VALIDATOR: str = "sql_validator_agent"
NODE_EXECUTOR:     str = "executor_agent"
NODE_CLARIFY:      str = "clarify_node"


# ── Conditional routing functions ─────────────────────────────────────────────


def _route_after_intent(
    state: AgentState,
) -> Literal["schema_agent", "clarify_node"]:
    """
    Route to the Schema Agent when intent is clear; otherwise ask for clarification.

    Args:
        state: Current pipeline state after the Intent Agent has run.

    Returns:
        Node name string for the next hop.
    """
    if state.get("intent_ok", False):
        return NODE_SCHEMA
    return NODE_CLARIFY


def _route_after_review(
    state: AgentState,
) -> Literal["sql_validator_agent", "sql_writer_agent", "executor_agent"]:
    """
    Route after the SQL Review Agent.

    - ``sql_review_passed=True``  → forward to ``sql_validator_agent``
    - ``error`` set (max retries) → short-circuit to ``executor_agent``
    - Otherwise                   → retry via ``sql_writer_agent``

    Args:
        state: Current pipeline state after the SQL Review Agent has run.

    Returns:
        Node name string for the next hop.
    """
    if state.get("error"):
        # Max retries exceeded — surface the error through the executor.
        return NODE_EXECUTOR
    if state.get("sql_review_passed", False):
        return NODE_SQL_VALIDATOR
    return NODE_SQL_WRITER


def _route_after_validator(
    state: AgentState,
) -> Literal["executor_agent", "sql_writer_agent"]:
    """
    Route after the SQL Validator Agent (sqlglot AST check).

    - ``sqlglot_valid=True``  → proceed to ``executor_agent``
    - ``sqlglot_valid=False`` → retry via ``sql_writer_agent``

    Args:
        state: Current pipeline state after the SQL Validator Agent has run.

    Returns:
        Node name string for the next hop.
    """
    if state.get("sqlglot_valid", False):
        return NODE_EXECUTOR
    return NODE_SQL_WRITER


# ── Terminal clarification node ───────────────────────────────────────────────


def _clarify_node(state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
    """
    Terminal node reached when the intent is too ambiguous to proceed.

    The clarification message is already set in ``state['clarification_needed']``
    by the Intent Agent; this node simply appends a stage label and returns.

    Args:
        state: Current pipeline state (passed through unchanged).

    Returns:
        Partial ``AgentState`` with a ``clarify_node:terminal`` stage appended.
    """
    LOGGER.info("ClarifyNode — clarification requested; ending graph.")
    return AgentState(stages=["clarify_node:terminal"])  # type: ignore[return-value]


# ── Factory ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────


def build_nl2sql_graph(
    model_service: ModelLoaderService,
    db_path: Path,
    max_review_retries: int = 3,
    sqlglot_dialect: str = "sqlite",
) -> "CompiledStateGraph":  # type: ignore[name-defined]
    """
    Construct and compile the six-node NL2SQL ``StateGraph``.

    Nodes
    -----
    1. ``intent``          — rule-based intent classification
    2. ``schema``          — live SQLite schema introspection
    3. ``sql_writer_agent`` — LLM SQL generation with few-shot prompting
    4. ``sql_review``      — DML/DDL blocking + SELECT/FROM + table existence
    4.5 ``sql_validator``  — sqlglot AST parse + dialect validation
    5. ``executor``        — read-only SQLite execution

    Args:
        model_service:      Loaded ``ModelLoaderService`` for LLM inference.
        db_path:            Absolute path to the primary SQLite database file.
        max_review_retries: Maximum writer → reviewer retry cycles.
        sqlglot_dialect:    sqlglot dialect used by ``SQLValidatorAgent``
                            (default: ``"sqlite"``).

    Returns:
        Compiled LangGraph ``StateGraph`` ready to ``invoke`` or ``stream``.
    """
    LOGGER.info(
        "Building NL2SQL LangGraph — db=%s, max_review_retries=%d, sqlglot_dialect=%s",
        db_path,
        max_review_retries,
        sqlglot_dialect,
    )

    data_root: Path = db_path.parent

    # ── Instantiate the six agent nodes ───────────────────────────────────────
    intent_agent      = IntentAgent()
    schema_agent      = SchemaAgent(data_root=data_root, default_db_path=db_path)
    sql_writer_agent  = SQLWriterAgent(model_service=model_service)
    sql_review_agent  = SQLReviewAgent(max_retries=max_review_retries)
    sql_validator_agent = SQLValidatorAgent(dialect=sqlglot_dialect)
    executor_agent    = ExecutorAgent()

    # ── Build the StateGraph ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
    graph: StateGraph = StateGraph(AgentState)

    # Register nodes.
    graph.add_node(NODE_INTENT,        intent_agent.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_SCHEMA,        schema_agent.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_SQL_WRITER,    sql_writer_agent.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_SQL_REVIEW,    sql_review_agent.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_SQL_VALIDATOR, sql_validator_agent.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_EXECUTOR,      executor_agent.run)
    graph.add_node(NODE_CLARIFY,       _clarify_node)

    # ── Wire edges ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    # Entry: START → IntentAgent.
    graph.add_edge(START, NODE_INTENT)

    # IntentAgent → SchemaAgent (proceed) or ClarifyNode (ambiguous).
    graph.add_conditional_edges(
        NODE_INTENT,
        _route_after_intent,
        {NODE_SCHEMA: NODE_SCHEMA, NODE_CLARIFY: NODE_CLARIFY},
    )

    # SchemaAgent → SQLWriterAgent (always).
    graph.add_edge(NODE_SCHEMA, NODE_SQL_WRITER)

    # SQLWriterAgent → SQLReviewAgent (always).
    graph.add_edge(NODE_SQL_WRITER, NODE_SQL_REVIEW)

    # SQLReviewAgent → SQLValidatorAgent (passed) | ExecutorAgent (max retries)
    #               | SQLWriterAgent (retry).
    graph.add_conditional_edges(
        NODE_SQL_REVIEW,
        _route_after_review,
        {
            NODE_SQL_VALIDATOR: NODE_SQL_VALIDATOR,
            NODE_EXECUTOR:      NODE_EXECUTOR,
            NODE_SQL_WRITER:    NODE_SQL_WRITER,
        },
    )

    # SQLValidatorAgent → ExecutorAgent (valid) | SQLWriterAgent (invalid retry).
    graph.add_conditional_edges(
        NODE_SQL_VALIDATOR,
        _route_after_validator,
        {NODE_EXECUTOR: NODE_EXECUTOR, NODE_SQL_WRITER: NODE_SQL_WRITER},
    )

    # Terminal edges.
    graph.add_edge(NODE_EXECUTOR, END)
    graph.add_edge(NODE_CLARIFY,  END)

    compiled = graph.compile()
    LOGGER.info("NL2SQL LangGraph (with SQLValidatorAgent) compiled successfully.")
    return compiled



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\intent.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Intent — Node 1 of 5 in the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Responsibility
--------------
Parse the raw user question, normalise whitespace and punctuation, then
decide whether the intent is specific enough to proceed.  If the question
is too vague the agent sets ``intent_ok = False`` and fills
``clarification_needed`` so the graph routes to the clarify terminal node.

Also performs an early-exit check for disallowed action words (DELETE,
DROP, INSERT, UPDATE …) in the raw question itself.  When such a word is
found the pipeline is halted immediately with a clear user-facing message —
no LLM call is made and no SQL is generated.

Short / meaningless inputs (1–2 words that contain no domain vocabulary,
typos like "thi?", random characters, etc.) are also caught and returned
as clarification requests — they never reach the LLM.

Design decision: this node is deliberately rule-based (no LLM call) so it
is fast, deterministic, and immune to hallucination risk.
"""

import re
from typing import Final

from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.intent_agent")

# Domain vocabulary — presence of any term indicates a specific enough intent.
_METRIC_TERMS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "age",
        "kyc",
        "balance",
        "maturity",
        "product",
        "branch",
        "circle",
        "senior",
        "account",
        "rate",
        "deposit",
        "customer",
        "flag",
        "date",
        "interest",
        "tenor",
        "brcd",
        "count",
        "sum",
        "total",
        "list",
        "show",
        "top",
        "max",
        "min",
        "average",
    }
)

# Bare vague phrases that provide no actionable information on their own.
_VAGUE_PHRASES: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "tell me something",
        "give me data",
        "get data",
        "details",
        "info",
        "information",
        "data",
        "give me",
        "tell me",
    }
)

# Ambiguous pronouns that require a metric term to be meaningful.
_AMBIGUOUS_TOKENS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {"this", "that", "those", "these", "it", "something"}
)

# Minimum number of meaningful (non-punctuation, non-stop-word) tokens
# required before a question is considered worth passing to the pipeline.
_MIN_MEANINGFUL_TOKENS: Final[int] = 3

# Common English stop-words that carry no compliance-domain meaning on their own.
_STOP_WORDS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "a", "an", "the", "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being",
        "have", "has", "had", "do", "does", "did", "will", "would", "could",
        "should", "may", "might", "shall", "can", "need", "dare", "ought",
        "used", "to", "of", "in", "on", "at", "by", "for", "with", "about",
        "against", "between", "through", "during", "before", "after", "above",
        "below", "from", "up", "down", "into", "out", "off", "over", "under",
        "again", "then", "once", "and", "but", "or", "nor", "so", "yet",
        "both", "either", "neither", "not", "no", "only", "own", "same",
        "than", "too", "very", "just", "because", "as", "until", "while",
        "i", "me", "my", "we", "our", "you", "your", "he", "she", "they",
        "him", "her", "his", "its", "their", "what", "which", "who", "whom",
        "how", "when", "where", "why", "all", "any", "each", "every", "some",
        "few", "more", "most", "other", "such", "get", "give", "tell", "let",
        "please", "hi", "hello", "hey", "ok", "okay", "thanks", "thank",
    }
)

# Compiled pattern to strip all punctuation from a token for clean comparison.
_PUNCT_STRIP: re.Pattern[str] = re.compile(r"[^\w]")

# Destructive / write action words that must never be allowed in a
# read-only compliance query interface.
_DISALLOWED_ACTION_WORDS: Final[frozenset[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "delete",
        "drop",
        "insert",
        "update",
        "truncate",
        "alter",
        "create",
        "replace",
        "pragma",
        "attach",
        "detach",
        "vacuum",
        "modify",
        "remove",
        "erase",
        "wipe",
        "purge",
        "destroy",
    }
)

_CLARIFICATION_MESSAGE: Final[str] = (
    "Your question is a bit unclear. Could you please rephrase or provide "
    "more detail?\n\n"
    "For example, try asking:\n"
    "• \"How many accounts have KYC flag N?\"\n"
    "• \"Show all accounts in the Mumbai circle.\"\n"
    "• \"What is the maturity date for senior citizen accounts in Thane branch?\"\n"
    "• \"List accounts with balance above 100000.\"\n\n"
    "Please ask a specific compliance question and I'll be happy to help!"
)


class IntentAgent:
    """
    Stateless LangGraph node that classifies user intent.

    The agent normalises the raw question and decides whether the pipeline
    should proceed (``intent_ok = True``) or surface a clarification request
    to the user (``intent_ok = False``).

    Usage::

        agent = IntentAgent()
        partial_state = agent.run(state)
    """

    # LangGraph node entry-point — called by the graph executor.

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """
        Analyse the raw question and classify intent.

        Performs four checks in order:

        1. Empty question — halt immediately.
        2. Disallowed action words (DELETE, DROP, INSERT …) — halt immediately;
           no LLM call is made.
        3. Short / meaningless input — 1 or 2 tokens after stripping punctuation
           and stop-words, with no domain vocabulary present (e.g. "thi?", "ok",
           "abc xyz", random characters) — halt with clarification request.
        4. Vague / ambiguous phrasing (known vague phrases, or ambiguous
           pronouns without a metric term) — halt with clarification request.
           The ambiguous-pronoun sub-check is **skipped** when the question
           already has more than 3 meaningful tokens (rich enough to send
           directly to the LLM).

        Args:
            state: Current pipeline state carrying at least ``question``.

        Returns:
            Partial ``AgentState`` with ``interpreted_question``,
            ``intent_ok``, ``clarification_needed``, and ``stages``.
        """
        question: str = state.get("question", "").strip()

        LOGGER.info("IntentAgent — analysing question: %r", question)

        # ── Check 1: empty question ───────────────────────────────────────────
        if not question:
            LOGGER.warning("IntentAgent — empty question received.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question="",
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=(
                    "No question was provided. Please ask a compliance query, "
                    "for example: \"How many accounts have KYC flag N?\""
                ),
                stages=["intent_agent:empty"],
            )

        lower_q = question.lower()
        # Build a clean token set — strip punctuation from each word.
        raw_tokens = lower_q.split()
        clean_tokens = {_PUNCT_STRIP.sub("", t) for t in raw_tokens if _PUNCT_STRIP.sub("", t)}
        lower_tokens = clean_tokens  # used for all subsequent set-intersection checks

        # ── Check 2: disallowed action words in the user's raw question ───────
        matched_action = _DISALLOWED_ACTION_WORDS & lower_tokens
        if matched_action:
            blocked_word = sorted(matched_action)[0].upper()
            LOGGER.warning(
                "IntentAgent — disallowed action word in question: %s", blocked_word
            )
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question=self._normalise(question),
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=(
                    f"⚠ Sorry, the keyword **{blocked_word}** is not permitted in this "
                    f"compliance interface.\n\n"
                    f"This system is **read-only** — only data retrieval queries are "
                    f"supported (SELECT).\n\n"
                    f"Please ask a valid read-only question, for example:\n"
                    f"• \"How many accounts have KYC flag N?\"\n"
                    f"• \"Show accounts in the Mumbai circle.\"\n"
                    f"• \"List senior citizen accounts in Thane branch.\""
                ),
                stages=[f"intent_agent:disallowed_action_word={blocked_word}"],
            )

        # ── Check 3: short / meaningless input ───────────────────────────────
        # Compute tokens that carry real meaning: not stop-words, not purely
        # numeric, and at least 2 characters long.
        has_metric = bool(_METRIC_TERMS & lower_tokens) or any(
            m in lower_q for m in _METRIC_TERMS
        )
        meaningful_tokens = {
            t for t in lower_tokens
            if t not in _STOP_WORDS and len(t) >= 2 and not t.isdigit()
        }
        is_too_short = len(meaningful_tokens) < _MIN_MEANINGFUL_TOKENS and not has_metric

        if is_too_short:
            LOGGER.info(
                "IntentAgent — too short / meaningless (%d meaningful tokens): %r",
                len(meaningful_tokens),
                question,
            )
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question=self._normalise(question),
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=(
                    "Your question is too short or unclear to process.\n\n"
                    "Please elaborate with more detail — for example:\n"
                    "• \"How many accounts have KYC flag N?\"\n"
                    "• \"Show all accounts in the Mumbai circle.\"\n"
                    "• \"What is the maturity date for senior citizen accounts in Thane branch?\"\n"
                    "• \"List accounts with balance above 100000.\"\n\n"
                    "The more specific your question, the better the result!"
                ),
                stages=["intent_agent:too_short"],
            )

        # ── Check 4: vague / ambiguous phrasing ───────────────────────────────
        # Skip the ambiguous-pronoun sub-check for questions that already contain
        # more than 3 meaningful tokens — those are real, specific queries and
        # should always reach the LLM.  Only the pure-vague phrase check still
        # applies (e.g. bare "give me data").
        is_pure_vague = lower_q.strip("?. ") in _VAGUE_PHRASES
        has_ambiguous = bool(_AMBIGUOUS_TOKENS & lower_tokens)
        _rich_question = len(meaningful_tokens) > 3
        needs_clarification = is_pure_vague or (has_ambiguous and not has_metric and not _rich_question)

        interpreted = self._normalise(question)

        if needs_clarification:
            LOGGER.info("IntentAgent — clarification required for: %r", question)
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                interpreted_question=interpreted,
                intent_ok=False,
                clarification_needed=_CLARIFICATION_MESSAGE,
                stages=["intent_agent:clarification_required"],
            )

        LOGGER.info("IntentAgent — intent resolved: %r", interpreted)
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            interpreted_question=interpreted,
            intent_ok=True,
            clarification_needed=None,
            stages=["intent_agent:ok"],
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _normalise(question: str) -> str:
        """
        Apply lightweight normalisation to the raw question text.

        Strips leading/trailing whitespace and ensures the question ends with
        a question mark.

        Args:
            question: Raw user input string.

        Returns:
            Normalised question string.
        """
        normalised = question.strip()
        if normalised and not normalised.endswith("?"):
            normalised = normalised.rstrip(".") + "?"
        return normalised



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\schema.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Schema — Node 2 of 5 in the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Responsibility
--------------
Introspect the live SQLite database and produce a rich Markdown-formatted
schema context string that the SQL Writer Agent embeds in its LLM prompt.
The schema description is always derived from the actual database file so it
never drifts from reality.

When multiple ``.db`` files exist the agent selects the best match by scoring
keyword overlap between the user question and available table / column names.
"""

import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List

from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.schema_agent")

# Number of sample rows included in the schema context for grounding.
_SAMPLE_ROW_LIMIT: int = 3


class SchemaAgent:
    """
    LangGraph node that resolves the target SQLite schema and formats it for
    LLM prompt injection.

    The agent scans ``data_root`` for ``.db`` files, selects the best match
    for the current question using a keyword-overlap heuristic, and builds a
    structured Markdown schema including column types and sample data.

    Args:
        data_root:        Directory that contains the SQLite ``*.db`` files.
        default_db_path:  Fallback database when no keyword match is found.
    """

    def __init__(self, data_root: Path, default_db_path: Path) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the Schema Agent.

        Args:
            data_root:        Root directory to scan for ``*.db`` files.
            default_db_path:  Absolute path to the primary compliance database.
        """
        self._data_root: Path = data_root
        self._default_db_path: Path = default_db_path

    # LangGraph node entry-point — called by the graph executor.

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """
        Select the best matching database and build the schema context string.

        Args:
            state: Current pipeline state carrying ``interpreted_question``.

        Returns:
            Partial ``AgentState`` with ``db_path``, ``schema_context``, and
            ``stages``.
        """
        question: str = state.get("interpreted_question", state.get("question", ""))

        LOGGER.info("SchemaAgent — selecting database for: %r", question)

        db_path = self._select_database(question)
        schema_context = self._build_schema_context(db_path)

        LOGGER.info("SchemaAgent — selected db=%s", db_path.name)

        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            db_path=str(db_path),
            schema_context=schema_context,
            stages=[f"schema_agent:db={db_path.name}"],
        )

    # Selects the most relevant database file using keyword-overlap scoring.

    def _select_database(self, question: str) -> Path:
        """
        Choose the most relevant ``.db`` file for the given question.

        Scoring: +1 per matching keyword (db stem, column name),
        +2 when a table name appears verbatim in the question.

        Args:
            question: Normalised natural-language question.

        Returns:
            Path to the selected SQLite database file.
        """
        candidates: List[Path] = sorted(self._data_root.glob("**/*.db"))

        if not candidates:
            LOGGER.warning("SchemaAgent — no .db files found; using default.")
            return self._default_db_path

        lower_q = question.lower()
        best_path: Path = self._default_db_path
        best_score: int = -1

        for db_path in candidates:
            score = self._score_database(db_path, lower_q)
            if score > best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_path = db_path

        return best_path

    @staticmethod
    def _score_database(db_path: Path, lower_q: str) -> int:
        """
        Compute a relevance score for a database file against the question.

        Args:
            db_path:  Path to the candidate SQLite database.
            lower_q:  Lower-cased user question.

        Returns:
            Integer relevance score (higher is more relevant).
        """
        score = 0
        if db_path.stem.lower() in lower_q:
            score += 1

        try:
            with sqlite3.connect(str(db_path)) as conn:
                tables = conn.execute(
                    "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table'"
                ).fetchall()
                for (table_name,) in tables:
                    if table_name.lower() in lower_q:
                        score += 2
                    for col in conn.execute(f"PRAGMA table_info('{table_name}')").fetchall():
                        if col[1].lower() in lower_q:
                            score += 1
        except sqlite3.Error as db_err:
            LOGGER.warning("SchemaAgent — cannot inspect %s: %s", db_path, db_err)

        return score

    # Builds a Markdown schema string from the live SQLite database.

    def _build_schema_context(self, db_path: Path) -> str:
        """
        Produce a Markdown-formatted schema description for prompt injection.

        Includes table names, column definitions (name, type, PK, NOT NULL)
        and a sample of real data rows to help the LLM generate accurate SQL.

        Args:
            db_path: Path to the SQLite database file.

        Returns:
            Multi-line Markdown string describing the database schema.
        """
        lines: List[str] = [f"## Database: `{db_path.stem}`", ""]

        try:
            with sqlite3.connect(str(db_path)) as conn:
                conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
                tables = conn.execute(
                    "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table' ORDER BY name"
                ).fetchall()

                for (table_name,) in tables:
                    lines.append(f"### Table: `{table_name}`")
                    lines.append("")
                    lines.append("| # | Column | Type | PK | Not Null |")
                    lines.append("|---|--------|------|----|----------|")

                    cols = conn.execute(f"PRAGMA table_info('{table_name}')").fetchall()
                    for col in cols:
                        cid, name, ctype, notnull, _default, pk = col
                        lines.append(
                            f"| {cid} | `{name}` | {ctype or 'TEXT'} "
                            f"| {'✓' if pk else ''} | {'✓' if notnull else ''} |"
                        )

                    lines.append("")
                    lines.append(f"**Sample rows from `{table_name}`:**")
                    sample_rows = conn.execute(
                        f"SELECT * FROM '{table_name}' LIMIT {_SAMPLE_ROW_LIMIT}"
                    ).fetchall()
                    if sample_rows:
                        col_names = [c[1] for c in cols]
                        lines.append("```")
                        lines.append(", ".join(col_names))
                        for row in sample_rows:
                            lines.append(", ".join(str(v) for v in row))
                        lines.append("```")
                    lines.append("")

        except sqlite3.Error as db_err:
            LOGGER.error("SchemaAgent — failed to read schema from %s: %s", db_path, db_err)
            lines.append(f"*Schema unavailable: {db_err}*")

        return "\n".join(lines)



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\sql_review.py
####################################################################################################

"""
SQL Review — Node 4 of 6 in the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Responsibility
--------------
Validate the SQL statement produced by the SQL Writer Agent before it is
passed to the SQLValidatorAgent (Node 4.5) and then executed.
Four validation dimensions:

  1. Safety    — rejects any DML / DDL keyword (INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, DROP …).
  2. Structure — verifies the statement starts with SELECT and has a FROM clause.
  3. Schema    — checks that every referenced table exists in the live database.

On failure the agent sets ``sql_review_passed = False``, populates
``review_feedback`` with a specific reason, and increments ``retry_count``
so the SQL Writer Agent can self-correct on the next cycle.

After this node passes, the SQL is forwarded to ``SQLValidatorAgent`` which
performs a deeper AST-level check using **sqlglot** before reaching the
``ExecutorAgent``.
"""

import re
import sqlite3
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Final, FrozenSet, List

from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_chatbot.nl2sql_agents.sql_review_agent")

# DML / DDL keywords that must never appear in a SELECT-only pipeline.
_DISALLOWED_KEYWORDS: Final[FrozenSet[str]] = frozenset(
    {
        "INSERT",
        "UPDATE",
        "DELETE",
        "DROP",
        "TRUNCATE",
        "ALTER",
        "CREATE",
        "REPLACE",
        "PRAGMA",
        "ATTACH",
        "DETACH",
        "VACUUM",
    }
)

_FROM_PATTERN: Final[re.Pattern[str]] = re.compile(r"\bFROM\b", re.IGNORECASE)
_TABLE_PATTERN: Final[re.Pattern[str]] = re.compile(
    r"\bFROM\s+([`\"\[]?[\w]+[`\"\]]?)"
    r"|\bJOIN\s+([`\"\[]?[\w]+[`\"\]]?)",
    re.IGNORECASE,
)
_MAX_ALLOWED_RETRIES: Final[int] = 3


class SQLReviewAgent:
    """
    LangGraph node that performs safety and schema validation on generated SQL.

    The agent is intentionally lightweight — regex plus a live PRAGMA query —
    to avoid adding heavy SQL-parser dependencies.

    When the maximum retry count is reached the agent routes the pipeline
    directly to the executor (which surfaces the error) rather than looping
    forever.

    Args:
        max_retries: Maximum writer → reviewer cycles before giving up.
    """

    def __init__(self, max_retries: int = _MAX_ALLOWED_RETRIES) -> None:
        """
        Initialise the SQL Review Agent.

        Args:
            max_retries: Ceiling on writer-reviewer retry cycles.
        """
        self._max_retries: int = max_retries

    # LangGraph node entry-point — called by the graph executor.

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """
        Validate the generated SQL statement through three sequential checks.

        Args:
            state: Pipeline state with ``sql_query``, ``db_path``, and
                   ``retry_count``.

        Returns:
            Partial ``AgentState`` with ``sql_review_passed``,
            ``review_feedback``, ``retry_count``, and ``stages``.
        """
        sql: str = state.get("sql_query", "").strip()
        db_path_str: str = state.get("db_path", "")
        retry: int = state.get("retry_count", 0)

        LOGGER.info("SQLReviewAgent — reviewing SQL (retry=%d): %s", retry, sql)

        # Guard: retry ceiling — surface a clear user-facing error.
        if retry >= self._max_retries:
            msg = (
                f"⚠ The request could not be completed after {self._max_retries} "
                f"attempts. The generated SQL did not pass safety validation.\n\n"
                f"Please rephrase your question and try again. If the problem "
                f"persists, contact your system administrator."
            )
            LOGGER.error("SQLReviewAgent — max retries exceeded.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=msg,
                retry_count=retry,
                stages=["sql_review_agent:max_retries_exceeded"],
                error=msg,
            )

        # Guard: empty SQL.
        if not sql:
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback=(
                    "The SQL Writer returned an empty query. "
                    "Please generate a valid SELECT statement."
                ),
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=["sql_review_agent:empty_sql"],
            )

        # Check 1: disallowed keywords in LLM-generated SQL.
        # If found on first attempt, retry the LLM with feedback.
        # If found after max retries, the ceiling guard above will catch it.
        upper_sql = sql.upper()
        for kw in _DISALLOWED_KEYWORDS:
            if re.search(rf"\b{kw}\b", upper_sql):
                LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewAgent — disallowed keyword in LLM SQL: %s", kw)
                return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                    sql_review_passed=False,
                    review_feedback=(
                        f"The generated SQL contains the disallowed keyword '{kw}'. "
                        f"Only SELECT statements are permitted. "
                        f"Rewrite the query without '{kw}'."
                    ),
                    retry_count=retry + 1,
                    stages=[f"sql_review_agent:disallowed_keyword={kw}"],
                    # Surface a user-facing error so the UI can show a warning
                    # even on the first detection (before max-retry ceiling).
                    error=(
                        f"⚠ Sorry, the keyword **{kw}** is not permitted in this "
                        f"compliance interface.\n\n"
                        f"This system is **read-only** — only SELECT queries are "
                        f"supported. Please rephrase your question as a read-only "
                        f"data request, for example:\n"
                        f"• \"How many accounts have KYC flag N?\"\n"
                        f"• \"Show accounts in the Mumbai circle.\"\n"
                        f"• \"List accounts with balance above 100000.\""
                    ),
                )

        # Check 2: must start with SELECT.
        if not upper_sql.strip().startswith("SELECT"):
            LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewAgent — no SELECT prefix.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback="The query must begin with SELECT.",
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=["sql_review_agent:no_select"],
            )

        # Check 3: must contain FROM.
        if not _FROM_PATTERN.search(sql):
            LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewAgent — missing FROM clause.")
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_review_passed=False,
                review_feedback="The query is missing a FROM clause.",
                retry_count=retry + 1,
                stages=["sql_review_agent:missing_from"],
            )

        # Check 4: referenced tables must exist in the live database.
        if db_path_str:
            invalid_tables = self._check_tables(sql, Path(db_path_str))
            if invalid_tables:
                LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewAgent — unknown tables: %s", invalid_tables)
                return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                    sql_review_passed=False,
                    review_feedback=(
                        f"The following tables do not exist in the database: "
                        f"{', '.join(invalid_tables)}. "
                        "Use only tables visible in the schema context."
                    ),
                    retry_count=retry + 1,
                    stages=[f"sql_review_agent:unknown_tables={invalid_tables}"],
                )

        LOGGER.info("SQLReviewAgent — SQL passed all checks.")
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sql_review_passed=True,
            review_feedback=None,
            retry_count=retry,
            stages=["sql_review_agent:passed"],
        )

    # Checks that all table names referenced in the SQL exist in the database.

    @staticmethod
    def _check_tables(sql: str, db_path: Path) -> List[str]:
        """
        Return table names referenced in *sql* that do not exist in *db_path*.

        Args:
            sql:     SQL statement to inspect.
            db_path: Path to the target SQLite database file.

        Returns:
            Sorted list of unrecognised table name strings (empty if all valid).
        """
        if not db_path.exists():
            return []

        referenced: set[str] = set()
        for match in _TABLE_PATTERN.finditer(sql):
            name = (match.group(1) or match.group(2) or "").strip("`\"[]")
            if name:
                referenced.add(name.upper())

        if not referenced:
            return []

        try:
            with sqlite3.connect(str(db_path)) as conn:
                rows = conn.execute(
                    "SELECT UPPER(name) FROM sqlite_master WHERE type='table'"
                ).fetchall()
            existing: set[str] = {row[0] for row in rows}
        except sqlite3.Error as db_err:
            LOGGER.warning("SQLReviewAgent — cannot read tables from %s: %s", db_path, db_err)
            return []

        return sorted(referenced - existing)



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\sql_validator.py
####################################################################################################

"""
SQL Validator — Node 4.5 of the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Responsibility
--------------
Parse and validate the SQL statement produced by the SQL Writer Agent using
**sqlglot** — a pure-Python SQL parser that understands the SQLite dialect.
This check runs *after* the lightweight regex-based SQLReviewAgent (Node 4)
has already confirmed the statement is a safe SELECT.  It adds a deeper,
AST-level guarantee:

  1. **Parse validity** — sqlglot can fully tokenise and build an AST for the
     statement.  A ``ParseError`` means the SQL is structurally malformed and
     would raise a ``sqlite3.OperationalError`` at execution time.

  2. **Dialect transpilation check** — the statement is round-tripped through
     sqlglot's SQLite dialect to normalise quoting and function names, then
     compared against the original to detect any construct that SQLite does
     not support (e.g. ``ILIKE``, ``LIMIT … OFFSET`` written as ``LIMIT x,y``
     in some dialects that sqlglot catches).

  3. **Forbidden statement types** — even if the regex in SQLReviewAgent
     somehow missed a DML/DDL keyword, the AST ``statement_type`` check here
     provides a second independent gate: only ``SELECT`` ASTs are permitted.

On failure the agent sets ``sql_review_passed = False`` and populates
``review_feedback`` so the SQL Writer Agent can self-correct on the next
retry cycle — the same mechanism used by SQLReviewAgent.

On success it sets ``sqlglot_valid = True`` and records the canonicalised
SQL in ``sql_query`` (if different from the original) so downstream nodes
always receive well-formed SQLite.

Pipeline position
-----------------

    SQLReviewAgent (node 4)
         │
         │ review passed
         ▼
    SQLValidatorAgent (node 4.5)   ← this file
         │
         ├─ valid  → ExecutorAgent (node 5)
         └─ invalid → SQLWriterAgent (node 3, retry cycle)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Optional

from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.sql_validator_agent")

# Allowed AST statement types — only pure SELECT queries may proceed.
_ALLOWED_STMT_TYPES: frozenset[str] = frozenset({"select"})


class SQLValidatorAgent:
    """
    LangGraph node that performs a deep AST-level validation of generated SQL
    using the **sqlglot** library before the query reaches the executor.

    sqlglot is a zero-dependency, pure-Python SQL parser that supports the
    SQLite dialect natively.  It catches malformed syntax, unsupported
    constructs, and non-SELECT statements that the regex-based SQLReviewAgent
    might miss.

    The agent is *non-destructive* by default: if sqlglot produces a
    normalised form that differs from the original only in whitespace or
    quoting, it silently updates ``sql_query`` in the pipeline state so the
    executor always receives canonical SQLite.

    Args:
        dialect:     sqlglot source dialect for parsing (default: ``"sqlite"``).
        strict_mode: When ``True`` any sqlglot ``ParseError`` or unexpected
                     statement type causes a retry.  When ``False`` (default)
                     a ``ParseError`` is logged as a warning but still
                     treated as a failure to force a writer retry.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        dialect: str = "sqlite",
        strict_mode: bool = False,
    ) -> None:
        self._dialect = dialect
        self._strict_mode = strict_mode

    # ── LangGraph node entry-point ────────────────────────────────────────────

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """
        Validate ``state['sql_query']`` with sqlglot.

        Args:
            state: Pipeline state with ``sql_query`` and ``retry_count``.

        Returns:
            Partial ``AgentState``.  On success: ``sqlglot_valid=True``,
            ``sql_query`` (canonicalised), ``stages``.  On failure:
            ``sqlglot_valid=False``, ``sql_review_passed=False``,
            ``review_feedback``, incremented ``retry_count``, ``stages``.
        """
        sql: str = state.get("sql_query", "").strip()
        retry: int = state.get("retry_count", 0)

        LOGGER.info("SQLValidatorAgent — validating SQL with sqlglot: %s", sql)

        # ── Try to import sqlglot (graceful degradation if not installed) ─────
        try:
            import sqlglot  # noqa: PLC0415
            import sqlglot.errors  # noqa: PLC0415
        except ImportError:
            LOGGER.warning(
                "SQLValidatorAgent — sqlglot is not installed; skipping AST validation. "
                "Run: pip install sqlglot"
            )
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sqlglot_valid=True,
                sqlglot_error=None,
                stages=["sql_validator_agent:skipped_no_sqlglot"],
            )

        # ── 1. Parse ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
        parse_error: Optional[str] = None
        parsed_statements = []
        try:
            parsed_statements = sqlglot.parse(sql, dialect=self._dialect, error_level=sqlglot.errors.ErrorLevel.RAISE)
        except sqlglot.errors.ParseError as exc:
            parse_error = str(exc)
            LOGGER.warning("SQLValidatorAgent — ParseError: %s", parse_error)

        if parse_error:
            return self._fail(
                retry=retry,
                reason=(
                    f"sqlglot could not parse the SQL statement (dialect={self._dialect}): "
                    f"{parse_error}. Please rewrite the query using standard SQLite syntax."
                ),
                stage_tag="parse_error",
            )

        if not parsed_statements or parsed_statements[0] is None:
            return self._fail(
                retry=retry,
                reason="sqlglot produced an empty parse tree. Ensure the query is a valid SELECT.",
                stage_tag="empty_ast",
            )

        stmt = parsed_statements[0]

        # ── 2. Statement type — only SELECT allowed ───────────────────────────
        stmt_type: str = type(stmt).__name__.lower()
        if stmt_type not in _ALLOWED_STMT_TYPES:
            LOGGER.warning(
                "SQLValidatorAgent — non-SELECT statement type: %s", stmt_type
            )
            return self._fail(
                retry=retry,
                reason=(
                    f"sqlglot identified this as a '{stmt_type.upper()}' statement. "
                    "Only SELECT queries are permitted."
                ),
                stage_tag=f"disallowed_stmt_type={stmt_type}",
            )

        # ── 3. Round-trip to SQLite dialect — canonicalise ────────────────────
        try:
            canonical_sql: str = stmt.sql(dialect=self._dialect, pretty=False)
        except Exception as gen_exc:  # noqa: BLE001
            # Generation failure is unusual but non-fatal; keep the original.
            LOGGER.warning(
                "SQLValidatorAgent — could not generate canonical SQL: %s", gen_exc
            )
            canonical_sql = sql

        if canonical_sql and canonical_sql.strip() != sql.strip():
            LOGGER.info(
                "SQLValidatorAgent — SQL normalised by sqlglot.\n  Before: %s\n  After : %s",
                sql,
                canonical_sql,
            )

        LOGGER.info("SQLValidatorAgent — SQL passed AST validation.")
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sqlglot_valid=True,
            sqlglot_error=None,
            sql_query=canonical_sql or sql,
            stages=[f"sql_validator_agent:passed dialect={self._dialect}"],
        )

    # ── Helpers ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    @staticmethod
    def _fail(retry: int, reason: str, stage_tag: str) -> AgentState:
        """Return a failed-validation partial AgentState that triggers a writer retry."""
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sqlglot_valid=False,
            sqlglot_error=reason,
            sql_review_passed=False,
            review_feedback=reason,
            retry_count=retry + 1,
            stages=[f"sql_validator_agent:{stage_tag}"],
        )



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\sql_writer_agent.py
####################################################################################################

"""
SQL Writer Agent — Node 3 of 5 in the NL2SQL LangGraph pipeline.

Responsibility
--------------
Translate the normalised natural-language question into a valid SQLite
``SELECT`` statement by calling the configured LLM provider, guided by:

  1. The live schema context produced by the Schema Agent.
  2. The static table metadata and few-shot examples from ``compliance_schema``.
  3. Optional reviewer feedback from a previous failed cycle.

The LLM prompt is structured as a Llama-style ``[INST]`` block that instructs
the model to output SQL exclusively between ``SQL_BEGIN_TAG`` and
``SQL_END_TAG`` delimiters.  On retry cycles the ``review_feedback`` field
from the previous ``SQLReviewAgent`` run is appended so the model can
self-correct.
"""

from typing import Final, Optional

from src.models.compliance_schema import COMP_FEW_SHOT_EXAMPLES, COMP_SCHEMA_DETAILS
from src.nl2sql_agents.state import AgentState
from src.services.model_loader_service import ModelLoaderService
from src.utils.constants import COMPLIANCE_TABLE_NAME, SQL_BEGIN_TAG, SQL_END_TAG
from src.utils.logger import get_logger

LOGGER = get_logger("compliance_bot.nl2sql_agents.sql_writer_agent")

_MAX_RETRIES: Final[int] = 3


class SQLWriterAgent:
    """
    LangGraph node that generates a SQLite SELECT statement for a given question.

    The agent constructs a structured few-shot LLM prompt using the injected
    schema context and calls the configured LLM provider.  On retry cycles
    the ``review_feedback`` field from the previous ``SQLReviewAgent`` run is
    appended to the prompt so the model can self-correct.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model_service : ModelLoaderService
        Initialised LLM provider service used for inference.
    table_name : str, optional
        Primary compliance table name.  Defaults to ``COMPLIANCE_TABLE_NAME``.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        model_service: ModelLoaderService,
        table_name: str = COMPLIANCE_TABLE_NAME,
    ) -> None:
        self._model_service: ModelLoaderService = model_service
        self._table_name: str = table_name

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # LangGraph node entry-point
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, state: AgentState) -> AgentState:
        """
        Generate a SQL SELECT query for the current pipeline state.

        Reads ``interpreted_question``, ``schema_context``, ``retry_count``,
        and optionally ``review_feedback`` from the incoming state, calls the
        LLM, and returns a partial state containing the extracted SQL.

        Parameters
        ----------
        state : AgentState
            Current pipeline state.

        Returns
        -------
        AgentState
            Partial state with ``sql_query``, ``retry_count``, and ``stages``
            populated.  On extraction failure also sets ``error``.
        """
        question: str = state.get("interpreted_question", state.get("question", ""))
        schema_context: str = state.get("schema_context", "")
        feedback: Optional[str] = state.get("review_feedback")
        retry: int = state.get("retry_count", 0)

        LOGGER.info("SQLWriterAgent — generating SQL (retry=%d) for: %r", retry, question)

        prompt = self._build_prompt(question, schema_context, feedback)
        raw_output = self._model_service.generate(prompt=prompt)
        sql = self._model_service.extract_sql(raw_output)

        if not sql:
            LOGGER.warning(
                "SQLWriterAgent — LLM output missing SQL tags. Raw: %.400s",
                raw_output,
            )
            return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
                sql_query="",
                retry_count=retry,
                stages=["sql_writer_agent:extraction_failed"],
                error=(
                    f"LLM did not return SQL within expected delimiters. "
                    f"Raw: {raw_output[:300]}"
                ),
            )

        LOGGER.info("SQLWriterAgent — LLM SQL: %s", sql)
        return AgentState(  # type: ignore[return-value]
            sql_query=sql,
            retry_count=retry,
            stages=[f"sql_writer_agent:llm_generated retry={retry}"],
        )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Internal helpers
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _build_prompt(
        self,
        question: str,
        schema_context: str,
        feedback: Optional[str],
    ) -> str:
        """
        Construct the full few-shot prompt for SQL generation.

        Assembles a Llama-style ``[INST]`` block that:

        * States the agent's role and the single task requirement.
        * Embeds the static table metadata and column descriptions.
        * Injects the live schema context from the Schema Agent.
        * Appends labelled few-shot examples.
        * Optionally appends reviewer feedback on retry cycles.

        Parameters
        ----------
        question : str
            Normalised natural-language question.
        schema_context : str
            Markdown schema description produced by the Schema Agent.
        feedback : str or None
            Reviewer feedback from a previous failed attempt, or ``None`` on
            the first attempt.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            Formatted prompt string ready for LLM inference.
        """
        schema = COMP_SCHEMA_DETAILS.get(self._table_name, {})
        table_desc: str = schema.get("TABLE_DESCRIPTION", "")  # type: ignore[union-attr]
        col_desc: dict[str, str] = schema.get("COLUMN_DESCRIPTION", {})  # type: ignore[union-attr]
        few_shots: str = COMP_FEW_SHOT_EXAMPLES.get(self._table_name, "")

        column_block = "\n".join(
            f"  - **{col}**: {desc}" for col, desc in col_desc.items()
        )

        # Include reviewer feedback only on retry cycles.
        retry_section = (
            f"\n[REVIEWER FEEDBACK — fix these issues in your new SQL]\n{feedback}\n"
            if feedback
            else ""
        )

        return (
            "[INST] <<SYS>>\n"
            "You are the SQL Writer in a 5-agent compliance analytics pipeline.\n"
            "The Intent Agent has confirmed the question is clear.\n"
            "The Schema Agent has provided the live database schema below.\n"
            "Your sole task: write ONE correct SQLite SELECT query.\n\n"
            "Rules:\n"
            "  1. Only SELECT statements — never INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, DROP, or ALTER.\n"
            f"  2. Wrap SQL exactly between '{SQL_BEGIN_TAG}' and '{SQL_END_TAG}'.\n"
            "  3. Reference only columns that appear in the schema below.\n"
            "  4. Output the delimited SQL only — no explanations, no comments.\n"
            "  5. Use UPPER() for case-insensitive text comparisons.\n"
            "  6. Use LIKE '%VALUE%' for partial string matches.\n"
            "<</SYS>>\n\n"
            f"=== Table Metadata: {self._table_name} ===\n"
            f"Description: {table_desc}\n\n"
            f"Columns:\n{column_block}\n\n"
            "=== Live Database Schema (Schema Agent) ===\n"
            f"{schema_context}\n\n"
            "=== Few-shot Examples ===\n"
            f"{few_shots}\n"
            f"{retry_section}\n"
            f"Question: {question}\n"
            "Response:\n[/INST]"
        )



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\nl2sql_agents\state.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Shared LangGraph state definition for the NL2SQL agentic pipeline.

``AgentState`` is the single mutable dict that flows through every node in the
compiled StateGraph.  LangGraph merges partial updates automatically, so each
node only needs to return the keys it owns.
"""

import operator
from typing import Dict, List, Optional

from typing_extensions import Annotated, TypedDict


class AgentState(TypedDict, total=False):
    """
    Pipeline-wide state threaded through all five NL2SQL agent nodes.

    Fields are ``total=False`` so each node writes only the keys it owns.
    The ``stages`` list uses an ``Annotated`` reducer so LangGraph appends
    entries from each node rather than overwriting the whole list.

    Attributes:
        question:             Raw natural-language question from the user.
        question_id:          Caller-supplied idempotency key.
        interpreted_question: Normalised question after intent analysis.
        intent_ok:            True when the intent is clear and the pipeline
                              can proceed; False to request clarification.
        clarification_needed: Follow-up question for the user when intent is
                              ambiguous.
        db_path:              Absolute path to the selected SQLite database.
        schema_context:       Markdown-formatted schema description injected
                              into the SQL writer prompt.
        sql_query:            Generated SQLite SELECT statement.
        sql_review_passed:    True when the SQL Review Agent approves the
                              statement; False when a rewrite is required.
        review_feedback:      Human-readable reviewer feedback for the writer
                              on a retry cycle.
        retry_count:          Number of writer → reviewer round-trips so far.
        result_rows:          Query result as a list of column → value dicts.
        result_columns:       Ordered list of column names for display.
        result_summary:       Human-readable summary produced by the executor.
        error:                Pipeline-level error message on unrecoverable failure.
        stages:               Ordered audit trail of completed stage names.
    """

    # Input
    question: str
    question_id: Optional[str]

    # IntentAgent outputs
    interpreted_question: str
    intent_ok: bool
    clarification_needed: Optional[str]

    # SchemaAgent outputs
    db_path: str
    schema_context: str

    # SQLWriterAgent outputs
    sql_query: str

    # SQLReviewAgent outputs
    sql_review_passed: bool
    review_feedback: Optional[str]
    retry_count: int

    # SQLValidatorAgent outputs (sqlglot AST parse + dialect check)
    sqlglot_valid: bool
    sqlglot_error: Optional[str]

    # ExecutorAgent outputs
    result_rows: List[Dict[str, object]]
    result_columns: List[str]
    result_summary: str

    # Shared bookkeeping
    error: Optional[str]
    stages: Annotated[List[str], operator.add]
